# cv-service

Python gRPC CV inference & training service — real Ultralytics YOLO inference behind `Inference.DetectStream`, with an echo fallback when the model backend is unavailable. `DetectStream` runs each stream's frame receipt and inference concurrently (V-d), bounded by a process-wide inference gate. A `ModelRegistry` (`cv_service/inference/registry.py`, CP-b/`docs/plans/done/CV-MODELS-PLAN.md`) lazily loads whichever local model(s) `model_id` names — including a comma-separated composite list that runs more than one model per frame — falling back to today's single default model for anything unrecognized. A per-stream `StreamTrackingSession` (`cv_service/tracking/`, T1/`docs/plans/done/TRACKING-PLAN.md`) adds stable track ids and a **detect-then-track duty cycle**: in `FOLLOW` a sub-millisecond visual tracker holds the locked target every frame while the detector drops to a ~2 s verify pass, measured 30× fewer detector passes per second. Tracking is **off by default** and byte-identical to the pre-tracking service when a client does not ask for it. `Inference.DetectPulled` (`cv_service/pull/`, MEDIA-SOT-PLAN wave M3) is the worker-pull counterpart: instead of receiving frame bytes, this service dials the pulled RTSP source itself, decodes it on its own deadline schedule, and feeds every served frame through the exact same `_handle_request`/`SessionRegistry`/`ModelRegistry`/`InferenceGate` machinery `DetectStream` uses — see "Pull mode (`DetectPulled`)" below.

**Package shape** (`docs/plans/active/LAYERING-REFACTOR-PLAN.md` §5.2, wave G): the former 1031-line `server.py` monolith (gRPC servicing + training orchestration + zip IO + process bootstrap all in one file) is split into four packages by role:

```
cv_service/
  config.py          frozen Settings dataclass + Settings.from_env() -- the ONE place every
                      CV_* env var is read, resolved once at startup and passed down explicitly.
  grpc/
    servicers.py      InferenceServicer + TrainingServicer -- wire<->domain translation ONLY;
                       the sole place that touches generated cv_pb2 types.
    server.py         serve()/main(), signal handling, composition root (builds Settings,
                       wires servicers, starts the gRPC server).
  inference/
    detector.py        YoloDetector + Detection + decode_frame/map_detections (was inference.py)
    registry.py         ModelRegistry + discover_roster + detect_composite + build_default_registry
    concurrency.py      LatestOnlyMailbox + InferenceGate + process_gate()
  training/
    dataset.py          exported-YOLO dataset layout, path-safety guards, sanitize_dataset_id,
                         zip landing for UploadDataset (the ONE definition -- was duplicated
                         between server.py and trainer.py)
    trainer.py           the ultralytics fine-tune core (TrainingSpec, build_spec,
                          ultralytics_train, output_model_id)
    marker.py             active_model.json read/write (renamed from the misleading training.py)
    orchestrator.py        the StartTraining job-lifecycle/queue-poll state machine, lifted out
                            of the servicer method; yields plain JobEvents, never touches cv_pb2
  tracking/            the detect-then-track duty cycle (docs/plans/done/TRACKING-PLAN.md wave T1;
                       correctness fixes in docs/plans/active/TRACKING-V2-PLAN.md wave C1;
                       ego-motion compensation in wave C2; the cost associator + appearance
                       descriptors in wave C3; the dormant gallery wired in at wave C4; the
                       stream_id-keyed session pool + multi-target FOLLOW in wave C5b)
    params.py           TrackingParams + resolve() -- the ONLY place a `<=0` wire sentinel
                         becomes a number (or, wave C3, an `AssignWeights`/`AssignGates`);
                         resolved on config CHANGE, never per frame
    scheduler.py        DutyCycleScheduler.decide(now, state) -> Decision(run_detector, reason)
                         -- PURE policy, TRACKING-PLAN §3.1's table and nothing else
    track.py            Track + TrackBook: TENTATIVE->CONFIRMED->COASTING->LOST->expired,
                         the per-stream track-id allocator, and the key-epoch mechanism
                         (bump_epoch) that replaces a whole-book wipe on an engine reset.
                         Wave C3: Track.descriptor + observe_descriptor -- the appearance
                         signature EMA, evidence-only like velocity
    predict.py          constant-velocity extrapolation (wave C1) + warp by a `Transform`
                         (wave C2, real transforms now, not just `IDENTITY`); pure stdlib
    lock.py             LockArbiter (lock_seq monotonicity) + target selection (click point,
                         best-IoU re-anchor)
    assign.py           NEW (landed C0, wired in wave C3) -- Candidate/Target/Assignment,
                         AssignWeights/AssignGates, CostAssociator (the platform's own
                         associator, two-stage gated cost matrix), hungarian() -- pure stdlib,
                         property-tested against brute force; FROZEN, not touched this wave
    memory.py           NEW (landed C0/wave "the dormant gallery") -- ObjectMemory, the
                         four-gate dormant-identity gallery; built and unit-tested but NOT YET
                         WIRED into session.py/track.py -- that is wave C4, out of scope here
    registry.py         TrackerRegistry: FOUR rosters of FACTORIES (associators, followers,
                         motion compensators, appearance extractors -- wave C2 added the
                         third, wave C3 the fourth plus `cost` in the associator roster),
                         startup constructibility probe, roster logged once at INFO.
                         Wave C5b: no new roster, but `follower()` is now called more than
                         once per stream (multi-target FOLLOW) -- its existing "a new
                         instance every call" contract already covers it
    sessions.py          NEW (wave C5b) -- SessionRegistry: StreamTrackingSession pool
                         keyed by stream_id with a disconnect grace window (review finding
                         B5), thread-safe, bounded; pure stdlib (threading + time only)
    engines/
      base.py            the FOUR protocols (Associator, SingleObjectTracker,
                          MotionCompensator, AppearanceExtractor) + Box/Observation/
                          TrackerUpdate/Transform/CameraPose/Descriptor -- stdlib only
      bytetrack.py       ByteTrackEngine  -- ultralytics BYTETracker          (Associator)
      lk.py              LkFlowEngine     -- Lucas-Kanade optical flow        (SingleObjectTracker)
                         -- wave C1 adds forward-backward corner rejection + re-seeding
      ncc.py             NccEngine        -- matchTemplate NCC                (SingleObjectTracker)
      flow_gmc.py        NEW (wave C2) -- FlowMotionCompensator: sparse optical flow across
                         the whole frame + RANSAC affine fit  (MotionCompensator, needs cv2)
      pose_gmc.py        PoseMotionCompensator: camera-attitude trigonometry, no pixels
                         (MotionCompensator, pure stdlib) -- wired into the registry/session
                         in wave C2; the module itself landed at C0
      histogram.py       NEW (wave C3) -- HistogramAppearanceExtractor: coarse joint H x S
                         colour histogram, Hellinger metric  (AppearanceExtractor, needs cv2)
    session.py          StreamTrackingSession: COMPOSITION ONLY, one instance per stream --
                         since wave C5b, "per stream" means per `stream_id` across a
                         disconnect too, not per `DetectStream` call (`sessions.py` pools
                         it; `servicers.py` no longer constructs one directly).
                         Wave C3: ASSOCIATE dispatches to `_run_cost_associate` when `cost`
                         is the resolved engine, and gains ego-motion compensation + an
                         appearance extractor -- both gated on `cost`, never on `bytetrack`.
                         Wave C5b: `reset_for_reconnect()` (engines dropped, book/gallery/
                         lock untouched) and multi-target FOLLOW (`_ExtraFollow`,
                         `_extras_verify_observations`/`_extras_observations` -- up to
                         `follow_top_k - 1` non-locked targets, each its own engine)
  pull/                 MEDIA-SOT-PLAN wave M3 -- the DetectPulled worker's own decode loop.
                         No cv_pb2 import (servicers.py alone translates); source.py's cv2
                         import is the package's only `cv`-extra dependency, and it's lazy.
    source.py            PullSource protocol + OpenCvPullSource (CV_PULL_DECODER=opencv,
                          M0's measured choice) + open_source() factory (unsupported
                          backend -> logged fallback to opencv, never a crash)
    clock.py              CaptureClock -- capturedAt = anchorWallclock + (pts - anchorPts),
                           re-anchored past a skew threshold (CV_PULL_CLOCK_MODE=anchor);
                           `arrival` (receipt wallclock) selectable
    loop.py               PullDecodeLoop: reader thread -> latest-wins mailbox (D8) ->
                           DeadlineSampler (ported from spikes/pull/sampler.py, itself a
                           port of StreamPipeline.sampleDue/armScheduleAt)
```

Tests mirror this exactly: `tests/{inference,training,grpc,tracking}/` + `tests/test_config.py` -- **except `tests/pull/`**, which covers both `cv_service/pull/**` AND `InferenceServicer.DetectPulled` (in `grpc/servicers.py`) in one place; wave M3's file scope kept every new test file under that one directory rather than also touching `tests/grpc/`, where `DetectPulled`'s own tests would otherwise mirror-live.

**Depends on:** grpcio, grpcio-tools, protobuf (Python); `cv` extra: ultralytics (pulls in torch transitively) + explicit `opencv-python>=5.0,<6` and `lap>=0.5.12` pins (T1 — see pyproject.toml's comment for why each is pinned rather than floated, and for invariant **P1**: nothing x86- or CUDA-only may ever enter this extra); shared contract `proto/vision/v1/cv.proto` · **Used by:** adapter-cv-grpc, wired end-to-end (`PipelineConfig.model` → `FrameRequest.model_id` — see "Java plumb verification" under CP-b below). **`cv_service/pull/` (wave M3) adds no new dependency** — `OpenCvPullSource` reuses the SAME `opencv-python` pin `detector.py` already needs (M0's own reason to choose it, `docs/conclusions/CV-PULL-SPIKE.md` §2/§7); `DetectPulled` is unreachable (import fails cleanly with a logged, non-crashing degrade) without the `cv` extra, same posture as the rest of real inference.
**Build/test:** `python -m cv_service.grpc.server` (run). `scripts/test.sh` or `python -m pytest` (test; needs `pip install -e '.[dev]'` at minimum, `-e '.[cv,dev]'` for the mock+real-model suites — see Status). Codegen: `scripts/gen_proto.sh` (regenerates `cv_service/gen/`, not committed). The local checkpoints this repo ships (`yolo11n.pt`, `yolo26n.pt`, `orion12l.pt`, `yoloe-26s-seg-pf.pt`, all gitignored, provenance: `docs/plans/done/CV-MODELS-PLAN.md`) exercise the registry/composite tests in `tests/inference/test_registry.py`/`test_real_model.py` when present. Deploying this service on a separate CUDA machine (offloading inference off the laptop): `cv-service/DEPLOY-GPU.md`.

## API surface

### `cv_service/config.py`
The **one place** every `CV_*` environment variable is read. Before this module existed, five vars were read piecemeal across `server.py`/`inference.py`/`trainer.py`/`concurrency.py` (three of them -- `CV_DEVICE`, `CV_MAX_CONCURRENT_INFERENCES`, `CV_DATASET_DIR` -- resolved once at *import* time, so a value could never change without a process restart even though nothing required that).
- `@dataclass(frozen=True) class Settings` — every configurable knob, resolved once: `port` (`CV_PORT`, default `50051` — new, previously unreachable via env), `model` (`CV_MODEL`, default `"yolo26n.pt"`), `imgsz` (`CV_IMGSZ`, default `416`), `device` (`CV_DEVICE`, default `None` = ultralytics auto), `max_concurrent_inferences` (`CV_MAX_CONCURRENT_INFERENCES`, default `min(2, cpu_count // 2)` floored at 1), `dataset_dir` (`CV_DATASET_DIR`, default `<cv-service>/datasets`), `model_dir` (`CV_MODEL_DIR` — new, default `<cv-service>/`), `max_upload_bytes` (`CV_MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES`, default 2 GiB), `grpc_workers` (`CV_GRPC_WORKERS`, default `10`), `shutdown_grace_seconds` (`CV_SHUTDOWN_GRACE`, default `5`). Every default is byte-identical to the literal it replaces.
- `Settings.from_env() -> Settings` — reads `os.environ` fresh (no caching) and resolves one `Settings`. `cv_service/grpc/server.py`'s `main()` calls this exactly once at process startup and threads the result down explicitly (`serve(settings)` → `InferenceServicer(registry=..., inference_gate=...)`/`TrainingServicer(model_dir=..., dataset_dir=..., max_upload_bytes=...)`). Nothing downstream reads `os.environ` directly anymore — modules that can be constructed standalone (`YoloDetector`, `ultralytics_train`) call `Settings.from_env()` themselves when a value isn't explicitly supplied, which re-parses fresh every call, exactly matching the pre-`config.py` per-construction-refresh behavior for `CV_MODEL`/`CV_IMGSZ` (and, deliberately, *changing* `CV_DEVICE`/`CV_MAX_CONCURRENT_INFERENCES` from "frozen at first import" to "fresh at each resolution" — see Gotchas).
- **Tracking knobs (T1)**, backing the wire's `<=0 = server default` sentinels on `TrackingConfig` — `cv_service/tracking/params.py`'s `resolve()` is their only consumer: `track_associate_engine` (`CV_TRACK_ASSOCIATE_ENGINE`, default `"bytetrack"`), `track_follow_engine` (`CV_TRACK_FOLLOW_ENGINE`, default `"lk"`), `track_verify_millis` (`CV_TRACK_VERIFY_MS`, default `2000`), `track_iou` (`CV_TRACK_IOU`, default `0.3`), `track_max_age_frames` (`CV_TRACK_MAX_AGE`, default `30`), `track_min_hits` (`CV_TRACK_MIN_HITS`, default `3`). Two engine knobs, not one, because the two modes are served by two *protocols* that share no method — a single "default tracker" would be meaningless (see "Tracking engine" below).
- **Tracking knobs (TRACKING-V2-PLAN wave C1)**, added for review findings B7/C2 — unlike every knob above, **neither has a wire counterpart**: `TrackingConfig` gained no new field for them (out of scope for a "no wire change" wave), so `params.resolve()` takes both straight from `Settings` with no per-request sentinel to fall back from. `track_max_age_millis` (`CV_TRACK_MAX_AGE_MILLIS`, default `3000`) — the wall-clock LOST backstop; `3000` is deliberately `track_max_age_frames`'s default (`30`) divided by the documented ASSOCIATE sample rate (10 fps), so the new rule is behaviour-preserving there while fixing FOLLOW's 20×-inflated meaning (see `track.py`'s `_settle` below). `track_min_tracker_confidence` (`CV_TRACK_MIN_TRACKER_CONFIDENCE`, default `0.5`, parsed by the same `_parse_unit_fraction` `CV_TRACK_IOU` uses) — below this, a `SingleObjectTracker`'s own reported confidence brings the next verify pass forward (scheduler trigger (b)) instead of waiting for a hard failure.
- **Tracking knob (TRACKING-V2-PLAN wave C2)** — `track_motion_engine` (`CV_TRACK_MOTION_ENGINE`, default `"flow"`, parsed by the same `_parse_engine_id` the two T1 engine knobs use, so `"off"` passes through unmangled). Backs `TrackingConfig.motion_engine_id` (wire field 8, frozen at C0) exactly the way `track_associate_engine`/`track_follow_engine` back `engine_id` — `params.resolve()` takes it when the request's own field is blank. `"off"` disables ego-motion compensation outright; any other value is an engine id (`flow`/`pose`, `registry.py`'s `BUILTIN_COMPENSATORS`) that falls back to the roster default, then to no compensation, exactly like the two T1 engine knobs — see "Tracking engine" below for the pose→flow fallback policy this one additionally has that the other two don't need.
- **Tracking knobs (TRACKING-V2-PLAN wave C3)** — `track_appearance_engine` (`CV_TRACK_APPEARANCE_ENGINE`, default `"histogram"` — **measured, see "Association inversion" below for the harness table**; same `_parse_engine_id` shape as `track_motion_engine`, `"off"` disables appearance evidence outright). Backs `TrackingConfig.appearance_engine_id` (wire field 9, frozen at C0). This default only matters to a stream that has ALSO opted `track_associate_engine`/`engine_id` into `"cost"` — `bytetrack` never resolves an appearance extractor at all (no descriptor input to feed), so shipping `"histogram"` here changes nothing for the (unchanged) default associator. Seven more knobs resolve `assign.py`'s `AssignWeights`/`AssignGates` for `CostAssociator`, straight from `Settings` with no wire field to fall back from (same shape as `track_max_age_millis`/`min_tracker_confidence` before them): `track_cost_weight_iou` (`CV_TRACK_COST_WEIGHT_IOU`, default `1.0`), `track_cost_weight_appearance` (`CV_TRACK_COST_WEIGHT_APPEARANCE`, default `0.5` — non-zero so `cost`+`histogram` together actually do something out of the box; `session.py`'s `_run_cost_associate` is what zeroes this back to `0.0` for a stream where no appearance extractor actually resolved, so a `cost` stream with appearance off behaves as pure geometry, never geometry-plus-a-constant), `track_cost_weight_label` (`CV_TRACK_COST_WEIGHT_LABEL`, default `0.0`), `track_cost_gate_min_iou` (`CV_TRACK_COST_GATE_MIN_IOU`, default `0.0` — no geometric floor; `max_cost`/`max_appearance` are what bound a match, deliberately permissive so a warped-but-imperfect candidate right after a stall is not forbidden outright), `track_cost_gate_max_appearance` (`CV_TRACK_COST_GATE_MAX_APPEARANCE`, default `0.6`), `track_cost_gate_max_cost` (`CV_TRACK_COST_GATE_MAX_COST`, default `float("inf")` — no cap beyond the two gates above, `assign.AssignGates()`'s own default), `track_cost_gate_high_confidence` (`CV_TRACK_COST_GATE_HIGH_CONFIDENCE`, default `0.25`, matching `engines/bytetrack.py`'s own `_TRACK_HIGH_THRESH` so both associators draw the same high/low split even though each tunes it as its own separate constant).
- **Tracking knobs (TRACKING-V2-PLAN wave C4)** — `ObjectMemory`'s `MemoryParams` (`memory.py`), resolved straight from `Settings` the same way the cost weights/gates above are, mirroring `MemoryParams`' OWN dataclass defaults exactly (unlike the cost weights, `MemoryParams`' own defaults are ALREADY the considered production values — "a wrong recovery is worse than a missed one" — so there is no reason for `config.py` to diverge from them the way it deliberately diverges from `AssignWeights().appearance`'s inert `0.0`). `track_memory_ttl_millis` (`CV_TRACK_MEMORY_TTL_MILLIS`, default `30_000`) backs `TrackingConfig.memory_ttl_millis` (wire field 10, frozen at C0) with the SAME `<=0 = deployment default` shape `verify_every_millis`/`max_age_frames` use — but unlike those, the DEPLOYMENT value itself is allowed to be `<=0`, and that is not a typo to correct: it is the one supported way to disable the dormant gallery fleet-wide (`_parse_int_allow_nonpositive`, a new parser -- the ONLY one in this file where a non-positive parsed value is honoured rather than replaced). The other six have no wire field at all, same "no request-level override" shape as the cost weights/gates: `track_memory_capacity` (`CV_TRACK_MEMORY_CAPACITY`, default `32`), `track_memory_gallery_size` (`CV_TRACK_MEMORY_GALLERY_SIZE`, default `4`), `track_memory_max_appearance_distance` (`CV_TRACK_MEMORY_MAX_APPEARANCE_DISTANCE`, default `0.45`), `track_memory_max_speed` (`CV_TRACK_MEMORY_MAX_SPEED`, default `1.0`, normalized frame-widths/second), `track_memory_blend_alpha` (`CV_TRACK_MEMORY_BLEND_ALPHA`, default `0.7`), `track_memory_min_confidence` (`CV_TRACK_MEMORY_MIN_CONFIDENCE`, default `0.35`).
- **Tracking knobs (TRACKING-V2-PLAN wave C5b)** — three, none with a wire field, split across two consumers. `track_session_grace_millis` (`CV_TRACK_SESSION_GRACE_MILLIS`, default `15_000`) and `track_session_capacity` (`CV_TRACK_SESSION_CAPACITY`, default `64`) are read directly by `cv_service.tracking.sessions.SessionRegistry` — never by `params.resolve()`, because they configure the SERVICER's pool across every stream, not one stream's own tracking behaviour (a client choosing its own grace window would let one stream affect every other stream's resource bound). `track_follow_top_k` (`CV_TRACK_FOLLOW_TOP_K`, default `1`) DOES flow through `resolve()` into `TrackingParams.follow_top_k` — same "no wire field, straight from `Settings`" shape as `track_max_age_millis` (wave C1). **The default is `1` — today's exact single-target FOLLOW behaviour — deliberately, not the larger number the feature exists to enable**: `DEFAULT_TRACK_ASSOCIATE_ENGINE`'s own comment already establishes the "ship the less-proven behaviour opt-in" posture for `cost` vs `bytetrack`; multi-target FOLLOW is new code with the same "prove it, then flip the fleet default" discipline, even though its own per-request risk is low (P2 still holds; a wrong "extra" box is cosmetic, never a mis-identified lock). An operator opts a fleet in with `CV_TRACK_FOLLOW_TOP_K=3` (or higher).
- **Tracking knobs (TRACKING-V2-PLAN wave C5c, review §4.6)** — two, neither with a wire field (§2's frozen diff adds none for either), both flowing through `resolve()` into `TrackingParams.roi_enabled`/`.roi_crop_factor` with no per-request override to fall back from — same "no wire field, straight from `Settings`" shape `track_follow_top_k` uses. `track_roi_enabled` (`CV_TRACK_ROI_ENABLED`, default `False`) is the whole feature's on/off switch — `session.py`'s `_roi_rescue` checks it FIRST, before computing anything else, so `False` is a genuine zero-cost no-op, not merely an unlikely trigger. **Ships opt-in, for a stronger reason than `track_follow_top_k`'s own opt-in default**: that knob's risk is an extra sub-millisecond SOT update; this one's is a real, `InferenceGate`-bounded SECOND detector pass — bounded to at most one per frame and gated exactly like a full-frame one (P2), but non-zero CPU/GPU on every deployment that flips it on, so "prove it, then flip the fleet default" applies at least as strongly here. `track_roi_crop_factor` (`CV_TRACK_ROI_CROP_FACTOR`, default `4.0`) is how many times a rescued candidate's OWN predicted box's larger dimension the crop's side extends to, centered on it — see "ROI re-detection" below for the harness math this default is picked from.
- **Tracking knob (TRACKING-V3-PLAN wave V1, §5)** — `track_capability_level` (`CV_TRACK_CAPABILITY_LEVEL`, default `0` = auto-probe). Same `<=0`-is-a-legitimate-value shape `CV_TRACK_MEMORY_TTL_MILLIS` uses, except here `0` is ALSO the parser's own default (auto), not merely "disabled"; `1`-`5` pins a deployment-wide ceiling. `params.resolve()` is the sole consumer — see "Capability ladder" below. The valid-range bound (`5`) is duplicated as a literal in `_parse_capability_level` rather than imported from `cv_service.tracking.levels.MAX_LEVEL` (this file's own "zero internal `cv_service` imports" precedent, unbroken by this wave); `tests/test_config.py::test_the_valid_range_stays_in_sync_with_levels_max_level` guards the duplication against silent drift.
- **Tracking knob (TRACKING-V3-PLAN wave V3, §4.2)** — `track_reupdate_max_gap_millis` (`CV_TRACK_REUPDATE_MAX_GAP_MILLIS`, default `15_000`), parsed by `_parse_int_allow_nonpositive` — the SAME shape and the SAME parser `CV_TRACK_MEMORY_TTL_MILLIS` uses, because ORU needs an identical fleet-wide off switch for invariant P7: a non-positive ceiling accepts no gap (every real gap is positive), so `0` disables both ORU and the OCR re-anchor fallback below it. `15_000` is not a round guess: measured directly against what `ObservationRing.before()` actually brackets in `long_occlusion`'s own FOLLOW replay — the ring's last REAL entry there lands well before occlusion even starts (FOLLOW's `DEFAULT_TRACK_VERIFY_MILLIS=2000` cadence, then `CV_TRACK_REACQUIRE_MILLIS=250` retries after reappearance), so the actually-bracketed gap is **~11.1s**, not the scenario's nominal 9s window — this ships with real margin above that measured figure, while staying below `DEFAULT_TRACK_MEMORY_TTL_MILLIS` (30s) so the two recovery mechanisms keep distinct roles: ORU for a still-live track's ring-remembered gap, `memory.py` for one already deleted and re-adopted under a recovered identity (whose ring starts fresh — nothing left for ORU to bracket there regardless of this ceiling). Full derivation in the constant's own comment. Backs `TrackingConfig.reupdate_max_gap_millis` (wire field 14) via `params.resolve()` into `TrackingParams.reupdate_max_gap_millis` — see "Observation-Centric Re-Update" below.
- **Pull knobs (`docs/plans/active/MEDIA-SOT-PLAN.md` §5.5, wave M3)** — back `cv_service/pull/{source,clock,loop}.py`. None has a wire counterpart except the two noted (`PullControl.target_fps`/`.detect_width`'s own `<=0 = deployment default` sentinels, mirroring `verify_every_millis`'s shape exactly): `pull_decoder` (`CV_PULL_DECODER`, default `"opencv"` — M0's measured choice; any other value degrades with a logged warning, `source.py`'s `open_source` never raises for it), `pull_rtsp_transport` (`CV_PULL_RTSP_TRANSPORT`, default `"tcp"`), `pull_target_fps` (`CV_PULL_TARGET_FPS`, default `10.0` — the floor `PullControl.target_fps<=0` falls back to), `pull_max_width` (`CV_PULL_MAX_WIDTH`, default `640`, matching `adapter-cv-grpc`'s own `MAX_DETECT_WIDTH` — the floor `PullControl.detect_width<=0` falls back to), `pull_open_timeout_millis`/`pull_stall_timeout_millis` (`CV_PULL_OPEN_TIMEOUT_MILLIS`/`CV_PULL_STALL_TIMEOUT_MILLIS`, both default `5000`), `pull_clock_mode` (`CV_PULL_CLOCK_MODE`, default `"anchor"` — M0's measured choice; `"arrival"` is the other legal value, anything else is REJECTED with a logged warning and the default, unlike `pull_decoder`, because `CaptureClock` itself raises on an unrecognized mode rather than degrading), `pull_clock_reanchor_threshold_millis` (`CV_PULL_CLOCK_REANCHOR_THRESHOLD_MILLIS`, default `100.0` — not named by §5.1/§5.5, which only says "a threshold"; chosen to reuse the plan's own 10-minute drift GATE number as the per-event trigger rather than invent an unrelated second constant, see "Pull mode" below).
- Forgiving-parse helpers (unset/garbage → default, never raise): `_parse_imgsz`, `_parse_device`, `_resolve_max_concurrent_inferences`/`_default_max_concurrent_inferences`, `_parse_positive_int` (the last backs the newer `CV_PORT`/`CV_MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES`/`CV_GRPC_WORKERS`/`CV_SHUTDOWN_GRACE` knobs plus `CV_TRACK_VERIFY_MS`/`CV_TRACK_MAX_AGE`/`CV_TRACK_MIN_HITS`/`CV_TRACK_MEMORY_CAPACITY`/`CV_TRACK_MEMORY_GALLERY_SIZE`/`CV_PULL_MAX_WIDTH`/`CV_PULL_OPEN_TIMEOUT_MILLIS`/`CV_PULL_STALL_TIMEOUT_MILLIS`), `_parse_engine_id` (blank → default; deliberately does **not** validate against the engine roster — that is `TrackerRegistry`'s startup probe's job, and validating here would make a typo a startup crash instead of a logged degradation), `_parse_unit_fraction` (backs `CV_TRACK_IOU`; rejects anything outside `(0, 1]` **with a fallback rather than a clamp**, because the likeliest mistake is writing the REST layer's percent `30` into the wire layer's fraction, and clamping `30` to `1.0` would silently produce a tracker that never re-anchors). **Wave C3 adds two more**: `_parse_unit_interval` (backs the three `[0, 1]` cost gates, and wave C4's `CV_TRACK_MEMORY_MAX_APPEARANCE_DISTANCE`/`_BLEND_ALPHA`/`_MIN_CONFIDENCE`; unlike `_parse_unit_fraction`, `0.0` is accepted and means "this gate is off" — a normal configuration for a cost gate, not the FOLLOW re-anchor bug `_parse_unit_fraction` guards against) and `_parse_nonnegative_float` (backs the unbounded weights/`max_cost`, and wave C4's `CV_TRACK_MEMORY_MAX_SPEED`; `>= 0`, no upper bound, so a deliberately large or infinite cap round-trips). **Wave C4 adds one more**: `_parse_int_allow_nonpositive` (backs `CV_TRACK_MEMORY_TTL_MILLIS` only — see above for why this is the one int knob in the file where `<=0` is a legitimate, honoured value rather than a fallback trigger). **Wave C5c adds two more**: `_parse_bool` (the first boolean `CV_TRACK_*` knob this module has needed — case-insensitive `1`/`true`/`yes`/`on` vs `0`/`false`/`no`/`off`, backs `CV_TRACK_ROI_ENABLED` only) and `_parse_positive_float` (backs `CV_TRACK_ROI_CROP_FACTOR` — unlike `CV_TRACK_MEMORY_TTL_MILLIS`, a non-positive crop factor is a misconfiguration to fall back FROM, not a legitimate disable value; that job belongs to `CV_TRACK_ROI_ENABLED`). **Wave M3 adds two more**: `_parse_string` (unset/blank → default, anything else passes through — backs `CV_PULL_DECODER`/`CV_PULL_RTSP_TRANSPORT`, freeform knobs with no roster to validate against here because the CONSUMER degrades gracefully) and `_parse_choice` (backs `CV_PULL_CLOCK_MODE` only — unlike `_parse_string`, this one DOES validate against a fixed roster (`anchor`/`arrival`) and falls back with a logged warning, because `CaptureClock` itself raises on an unknown mode rather than degrading). These used to be duplicated across `inference.py`/`trainer.py`/`concurrency.py`; this is the one definition.

### `cv_service/inference/concurrency.py`
Pure-stdlib (`threading` only — no `cv_pb2`, no cv2/numpy/ultralytics), protobuf-agnostic by the same convention as `detector.py`. Two independent primitives, wired to `FrameRequest`/`DetectStream` by `cv_service/grpc/servicers.py`:
- `class LatestOnlyMailbox[T]` — single-slot, latest-wins handoff between one producer thread and one consumer thread. `put(value)` never blocks/queues: if a value is already waiting unconsumed, it's silently replaced (`.dropped` counts replacements). `get() -> T | None` blocks until a value is available or the mailbox is `close()`d and drained (`None` sentinel — don't use this for a value where `None` is itself meaningful; `FrameRequest`s never are). `close()` unblocks every pending/future `get()` with `None` once any already-pending value has been delivered once.
- `class InferenceGate(max_concurrent: int)` — a plain `threading.Semaphore(max_concurrent)` wrapped as a context manager (`gate.acquire()`), not a thread pool: it gates how many *existing* stream-consumer threads may be inside `detect()` at once, it never creates threads of its own. Raises `ValueError` if `max_concurrent < 1`.
- `process_gate() -> InferenceGate` — the shared, process-wide gate a bare `InferenceServicer()` falls back to when no `inference_gate=` is given. Lazily built (and cached) from `cv_service.config.Settings.from_env()` on first call rather than at import time (see `cv_service/config.py`) — the production composition root, `cv_service.grpc.server.serve()`, always builds and passes a gate explicitly instead, so this lazy singleton is only reached via direct/test construction. Tests construct their own differently-sized `InferenceGate` and inject it instead (`InferenceServicer(detector=..., inference_gate=...)`).

**Acquisition vs. continuation confidence** (`docs/conclusions/CV-RATE-BUDGET.md` §4). Both associators split their match into a high- and a low-confidence stage — `engines/bytetrack.py`'s `_TRACK_LOW_THRESH = 0.1`, `assign.py`'s `AssignGates.high_confidence = 0.25` — because a weak box landing on a predicted position is strong evidence of *continuity* even when it is weak evidence of *existence*. That entire second stage used to be unreachable: the operator's `confidence_threshold` was applied at `model.predict(conf=)`, so no box below it was ever created and the low list was always empty. We paid for ByteTrack and ran half of it.

`detect_floor` (`CV_DETECT_FLOOR`, default `0.15` — deliberately **below** `track_cost_gate_high_confidence`, or the low stage would stay just as empty) is the confidence the detector actually runs at **while tracking is active**; the operator's threshold moves to the response, applied by `servicers._reportable`. Three properties are load-bearing and tested (`tests/grpc/test_confidence_split.py`):

- **`OFF` is untouched.** With tracking off the operator threshold still goes straight to the detector, so that path stays byte-identical (P1).
- **The floor only ever widens recall.** `_detect_floor_for` takes `min(detect_floor, operator)`, so someone who deliberately asked for `0.05` gets `0.05`, not `0.15`.
- **A weak box is withheld until its identity is earned.** `_reportable` passes a sub-threshold box only when its track is `CONFIRMED`/`COASTING` — i.e. it cleared `min_hits` detector confirmations. `TENTATIVE` deliberately does not qualify: that is exactly the noise a lower floor produces more of, and letting it through would trade a recall win for on-screen flicker.

> **`tools/trackeval` cannot measure this.** `replay.py` emits every synthetic detection at a fixed `confidence = 0.9`, so the low stage stays empty in the harness and the report filter passes everything — the scoreboard is unchanged by this feature by construction, which is a limitation of the harness, not evidence the change is inert. Judging it needs either a confidence-aware scenario or real video.

### `cv_service/inference/detector.py`
Independent of the generated protobuf stubs (importable/testable without codegen) — only `cv2`/`numpy`/optionally `ultralytics`.
- `ENCODING_JPEG`, `ENCODING_BGR24: str` — match `ImageEncoding` enum names one-for-one (get via `cv_pb2.ImageEncoding.Name(request.encoding)`).
- `DEFAULT_MODEL = "yolo26n.pt"`, `DEFAULT_IMGSZ = 416`, `DEFAULT_CONFIDENCE = 0.25` — all three re-exported from `cv_service.config` (the canonical home) for backward-compatible imports. `DEFAULT_CONFIDENCE` moved there when the confidence split landed: it is still an inference-behaviour default rather than a `CV_*` env knob, but `grpc/servicers.py` needs it too (to know what threshold to *report* at) and that module must stay importable without the `cv` extra, which importing this one would break.
- `class ModelUnavailableError(RuntimeError)` — raised by `YoloDetector.__init__` when `ultralytics` isn't installed, or the model/weights can't be loaded.
- `@dataclass(frozen=True) class Detection(label: str, confidence: float, x: float, y: float, width: float, height: float)` — mirrors proto `Detection`/`BoundingBox`: `x`/`y` top-left corner, `width`/`height` extend right/down, all normalized to `[0, 1]`.
- `def decode_frame(width: int, height: int, encoding: str, data: bytes) -> np.ndarray` — JPEG via `cv2.imdecode`; BGR24 via `np.frombuffer(...).reshape((height, width, 3))`. Raises `ValueError` on bad length/bytes/encoding.
- `def map_detections(result: Any, frame_width: int, frame_height: int) -> list[Detection]` — maps one Ultralytics `Results` (duck-typed: `.boxes.xyxy`/`.conf`/`.cls`, `.names`) to normalized `Detection`s (Ultralytics already rescales `xyxy` to the original frame, so no letterbox un-padding needed); clamps to `[0, 1]`; `[]` if `boxes` is `None`/empty.
- `class YoloDetector(model_name: str | None = None, *, model: Any = None, device: str | None = None)` — loads `ultralytics.YOLO(model_name or Settings.from_env().model)` once; `model=` injects a test double, bypassing `ultralytics` entirely. `.model_name -> str`. `.imgsz -> int` — from `Settings.from_env().imgsz` (default `DEFAULT_IMGSZ`; unset/non-numeric/non-positive all fall back to the default, never raise). `.device -> str | None` — the constructor `device` kwarg if given (falsy falls through), else `Settings.from_env().device`; logged once at INFO at construction (`"cv-service inference device=%s (model=%r)"`, `"auto"` when `None`) alongside the model-load log. `Settings.from_env()` is called fresh at every construction (no caching) — this is one of the few places outside `config.py` itself that calls `from_env()` directly rather than receiving an already-resolved value, because `YoloDetector` is constructed from more than one call site (the model registry, tests, real-model smoke tests) that don't all have a `Settings` handy; the *parsing* still lives in exactly one place either way. After the model is loaded (real or injected), construction runs one warmup `predict()` on a black `np.zeros((imgsz, imgsz, 3), uint8)` frame; a warmup failure (including an invalid/unavailable `device`, e.g. `"cuda:0"` with no GPU — ultralytics raises inside `predict()`) raises `ModelUnavailableError` same as a load failure, no special-casing needed. `.detect(*, width, height, encoding, data, confidence_threshold: float | None = None) -> tuple[list[Detection], int]` — decode → `model.predict(frame, conf=conf, imgsz=self.imgsz, verbose=False, **({"device": self.device} if self.device is not None else {}))` → map; returns `(detections, inference_millis)`. `device=` is passed to *every* `predict()` call (warmup and `detect()`) only when resolved — omitted entirely (not passed as `None`) when unset, keeping behavior byte-identical to before this knob existed. Falsy/`None` `confidence_threshold` uses `DEFAULT_CONFIDENCE`.

### `cv_service/inference/registry.py` (CP-b, `docs/plans/done/CV-MODELS-PLAN.md`)
Same "no `cv_pb2`" convention as `detector.py` — importable/testable without codegen, but importing it (it imports `cv_service.inference.detector`) requires the `cv` extra. See "Model registry & composite mode" below for the full design/rationale.
- `def discover_roster(base_dir: Path, default_model: str) -> dict[str, str]` — scans `base_dir` for `*.pt` files and `*_openvino_model` directories (one level, non-recursive); always includes `default_model` even if not found there (a not-yet-downloaded alias, or a `CV_MODEL` path elsewhere). Returns `{model_id -> loadable name/path}`.
- `def short_name(model_id: str) -> str` — strips `.pt`/`_openvino_model` for use as a composite-mode label prefix (`"yolo11n.pt"`/`"yolo11n_openvino_model"` → `"yolo11n"`).
- `class ModelRegistry(roster: dict[str, str], default_id: str, *, detector_factory: Callable = YoloDetector)` — lazy `{model_id -> YoloDetector}`; `default_id` must be a `roster` key (raises `ValueError` otherwise — always true when `roster` came from `discover_roster`). `.roster -> dict[str, str]` (a copy). `.default_detector() -> YoloDetector | None` — lazily builds/caches the default; `None` if even it fails to load. `.resolve(requested_model_id: str) -> list[tuple[str, YoloDetector]]` — splits on `,` (composite mode), drops unknown ids (logs once per distinct unknown id, INFO) and ids that fail to load (logs once per id, WARNING) from that specific request, keeping any other known/loadable members; falls back to `[(default_id, default_detector())]` only when *nothing* in the request resolved (empty/unknown/all-failed), and to `[]` if even the default itself is unavailable (caller echoes that frame). `.loaded_ids() -> list[str]` — diagnostics/tests.
- `def detect_composite(resolved: list[tuple[str, YoloDetector]], *, gate: InferenceGate, width, height, encoding, data, confidence_threshold=None) -> tuple[list[Detection], int]` — runs every resolved member's `detect()` (each individually through `gate.acquire()`, same as a lone model), concatenates detections, sums `inference_millis`. When `len(resolved) > 1`, prefixes every label `"{short_name}:{label}"` (e.g. `"orion12l:tank"`) — a single-model call is untouched. Logs each member's own `inference_millis` at DEBUG (not INFO — see Gotchas, this runs every frame).
- `def build_default_registry(settings: Settings) -> ModelRegistry | None` — builds the production registry: `discover_roster(settings.model_dir, settings.model)`, applies a persisted promotion from `cv_service.training.marker.read_active_model` if one names a current roster id, constructs the `ModelRegistry`, eagerly forces the default detector to load (a broken default degrades the whole servicer to echo mode). Assumes the `cv` extra is already available (this module's own import already requires it); callers that don't yet know whether the extra is installed (`cv_service/grpc/server.py`'s `serve()`, `InferenceServicer`'s omitted-`registry` default path) import this module itself inside a `try/except ImportError`, mirroring the pre-split `_build_default_detector()`/`_build_default_registry()` contract.

### `cv_service/training/dataset.py`
Stdlib-only (`os`, `shutil`, `zipfile`, `uuid`, `pathlib`) — importable without the `cv` extra. Never imports `cv_pb2` — `cv_service/grpc/servicers.py` is the sole translation point, turning this module's plain `UploadOutcome` into a `cv_pb2.UploadAck` itself.
- `DATASET_DIRNAME = "datasets"`, `DATA_YAML_NAME`/`IMAGES_DIRNAME`/`LABELS_DIRNAME` — the exported-YOLO layout a dataset dir must carry (Phase 1's `FilesystemDatasetExport`). Format contract shared with another process, so these stay plain constants (not env-configurable — `docs/plans/active/LAYERING-REFACTOR-PLAN.md` §1.3's explicit out-of-scope list).
- `class DatasetNotFoundError(Exception)` — missing/malformed dataset (a *reported* `FAILED`, never a gRPC abort).
- `def resolve_dataset_dir(datasets_root, dataset_id) -> Path` — validates `<root>/<id>/` carries `data.yaml`+`images/`+`labels/`, else raises `DatasetNotFoundError`. Re-exported by `cv_service.training.trainer` (which calls it from `build_spec`) so existing callers importing it via `trainer.resolve_dataset_dir` keep working.
- `def sanitize_dataset_id(dataset_id: str) -> str` — filesystem-safe basename. **The one definition** — previously duplicated between `server.py`'s own sanitize step and an inline copy inside `trainer.output_model_id`; `trainer.output_model_id` now imports this instead.
- `def is_safe_dataset_id(dataset_id: str) -> bool` — `UploadDataset`'s accept test: anything `sanitize_dataset_id` would rewrite (blank, a path separator, `..`, ...) is rejected outright rather than silently accepted under a different name.
- `def is_safe_zip_entry(name: str) -> bool` — zip-slip protection for one archive member's path: accepts only `data.yaml`, `images/<name>`, `labels/<stem>.txt`; rejects an absolute path, a `..` segment, or any other top-level prefix.
- `@dataclass(frozen=True) class UploadOutcome(ok: bool, message: str, dataset_id="", bytes_received=0, file_count=0)` — plain (wire-agnostic) result of landing an archive.
- `def land_dataset(zip_path, datasets_root, dataset_id, bytes_received) -> UploadOutcome` — validates + extracts into a sibling temp dir, validates the extracted tree with `resolve_dataset_dir`, then atomically replaces `<datasets_root>/<dataset_id>/` (remove-then-`os.replace`). Every failure past the streaming/size-cap stage is a *reported* `ok=False`, never raised.

### `cv_service/training/trainer.py` (CV-TRAINING Phase 2, `StartTraining` core)
Stdlib-only at import scope (no `ultralytics`/`torch`/`cv2` at module top — same discipline as `cv_service/grpc/server.py`); the heavyweight `ultralytics` import is **lazy**, inside `ultralytics_train`, so the server stays importable/runnable without the `cv` extra. Re-exports `DATA_YAML_NAME`/`IMAGES_DIRNAME`/`LABELS_DIRNAME`/`DatasetNotFoundError`/`resolve_dataset_dir` from `cv_service.training.dataset` (its own `build_spec` calls `resolve_dataset_dir` directly).
- `DEFAULT_EPOCHS = 50` (fallback when `TrainingJobSpec.epochs` is unset/≤0), `DEFAULT_BASE_MODEL = "yolo26n.pt"`.
- `@dataclass(frozen=True) TrainingSpec(job_id, base_model, dataset_id, epochs, dataset_dir, data_yaml, output_dir)` — everything `ultralytics_train` needs. `@dataclass(frozen=True) EpochProgress(epoch, total_epochs, loss, map50)` — one epoch's reported metrics, mapped 1:1 to `TrainingProgress` fields (via `cv_service.training.orchestrator.JobEvent`, see below).
- `class TrainingCancelled(Exception)` — raised out of a `train_fn` on caller cancel.
- `build_spec(*, job_id, base_model, dataset_id, epochs, datasets_root, output_dir) -> TrainingSpec` — resolves the dataset (`cv_service.training.dataset.resolve_dataset_dir`) + defaults epochs/base_model.
- `output_model_id(dataset_id, epochs) -> str` — the produced artifact's id/filename: **`<dataset_id>-<epochs>e.pt`** (deterministic; `dataset_id` sanitized via `cv_service.training.dataset.sanitize_dataset_id` so a path separator can't escape the model dir).
- `TrainFn = Callable[..., Path]` — the injectable trainer contract: `train_fn(spec, *, on_epoch: Callable[[EpochProgress],None], is_cancelled: Callable[[],bool]) -> Path` returning the produced `best.pt`; raises `TrainingCancelled` if cancelled, any `Exception` on failure. Default is `ultralytics_train`; tests inject a fast fake.
- `ultralytics_train(spec, *, on_epoch, is_cancelled) -> Path` — lazily imports `ultralytics`, resolves `device`/`imgsz` via `cv_service.config.Settings.from_env()` (device auto if unset — **never hard-pins CPU**), hooks `on_fit_epoch_end` for per-epoch progress (suppressing Ultralytics' one **trailing** post-training-validation callback where `trainer.epoch==trainer.epochs`, so callers see exactly one update per epoch), sets `trainer.stop=True` on cancel, and returns the run's `best.pt` (`<output_dir>/runs/<job_id>/weights/best.pt`). `loss` is read from `trainer.loss` (scalar; falls back to summing the `trainer.tloss` dict); `map50` from `metrics["metrics/mAP50(B)"]`. `_warn_cpu_once` logs the one-time "training on CPU — this is slow" note when no CUDA is available.

### `cv_service/training/marker.py` (renamed from `cv_service/training.py`)
Stdlib-only (`json`), importable without the `cv` extra — the misleading old name (`training.py`) suggested it owned the whole training feature; it only ever read/wrote `active_model.json`, so it's named for that now.
- `ACTIVE_MODEL_MARKER = "active_model.json"` (written under `Settings.model_dir`, gitignored — runtime state); `read_active_model(model_dir) -> str | None` (forgiving — missing/corrupt/empty marker reads as `None`, never raises); `write_active_model(model_dir, model_id, version="")`. `build_default_registry()` (`cv_service/inference/registry.py`) reads the marker at startup and, if it names a **current roster id**, seeds the registry's `default_id` from it (a marker naming an absent id, or no marker, falls back to `settings.model`) — this is what makes a promotion survive the rsync-deploy restart.

### `cv_service/training/orchestrator.py`
The job-lifecycle/queue-poll state machine lifted out of the (formerly ~120-line) `StartTraining` gRPC handler, so it can be read/tested independent of wire translation. **Never imports `cv_pb2` or `grpc`** — `cv_service/grpc/servicers.py` is the sole place that turns its output into wire messages.
- `@dataclass(frozen=True) class JobEvent(kind: str, epoch=0, total_epochs=0, loss=0.0, map50=0.0, message="", model_id="")` — the plain, wire-agnostic counterpart of `cv_pb2.TrainingProgress`. `kind` is `"running"` (one per epoch) or a terminal `"succeeded"`/`"failed"`; a job ending via cancellation yields no event at all (the generator just stops), same as before extraction.
- `run_training_job(spec, *, job_id, train_fn, is_context_active, register_cancel_callback, publish_artifact) -> Iterator[JobEvent]` — runs `train_fn` on a background worker thread (mirrors `DetectStream`'s `_StreamReader` "keep long-lived work off the calling thread" shape); this generator only polls a `queue.Queue` for progress + yields, so it stays responsive to cancellation. `is_context_active`/`register_cancel_callback` are plain callables `cv_service/grpc/servicers.py` wires to `context.is_active()`/`context.add_callback()` — this function itself never touches `grpc.ServicerContext`. `publish_artifact` is the servicer's own callback (it needs `self._registry`/`self._model_dir`) for landing a successful run's `best.pt`.

### `cv_service/tracking/` (T1, `docs/plans/done/TRACKING-PLAN.md` + `docs/extracts/TRACKING-ORCHESTRATION.md`)
Same "no `cv_pb2`" convention as `inference/`/`training/`. **Everything except `engines/{bytetrack,lk,ncc}.py` is pure stdlib** — no `cv2`/`numpy`/`ultralytics` at module scope, including `engines/base.py` (its `np.ndarray` annotations are `TYPE_CHECKING`-only). That is what keeps `python -m cv_service.grpc.server` runnable with no `cv` extra even though `grpc/servicers.py` imports the session at module scope, and what makes the duty cycle testable against a fake clock with no frames. Both properties have their own tests (`tests/tracking/test_registry.py`). Full design: "Tracking engine" below.

- **`params.py`** — `MODE_OFF`/`MODE_ASSOCIATE`/`MODE_FOLLOW`/`MODE_UNSPECIFIED: str` (match `cv_pb2.TrackingMode`'s enum **value names** one-for-one, the same convention `detector.py`'s `ENCODING_*` uses, so the servicer maps with `Name()`/`Value()` and keeps no lookup table). `@dataclass(frozen=True) TrackingRequest` / `LockRequest` — the protobuf-free counterparts of `cv_pb2.TrackingConfig`/`TargetLock`, **sentinels intact**; `TrackingRequest.motion_engine_id: str = ""` (wave C2, wire field 8), `.appearance_engine_id: str = ""` (wave C3, wire field 9), `.memory_ttl_millis: int = 0` (wave C4, wire field 10). `@dataclass(frozen=True) TrackingParams(mode, engine_id, verify_every_millis, redetect_iou_threshold, max_age_frames, min_hits, track_max_age_millis, min_tracker_confidence, motion_engine_id, appearance_engine_id, cost_weights, cost_gates, memory_params, follow_top_k, roi_enabled, roi_crop_factor)` + `.active`. `track_max_age_millis`/`min_tracker_confidence` (wave C1), `follow_top_k` (wave C5b) and `roi_enabled`/`roi_crop_factor` (wave C5c) have no wire sentinel to interpret — `resolve()` copies all five straight from `Settings`. `motion_engine_id` (wave C2) is the one field `resolve()` does NOT fully resolve to a concrete engine id: blank → `settings.track_motion_engine`, but anything else — including the legitimate value `"off"` — passes through untouched, because deciding what actually SERVES it needs a live `CameraPose` (`session.py`'s job, not `resolve()`'s — see `_resolve_motion_compensator` below). `appearance_engine_id` (wave C3) is the same shape, minus the live-signal check: blank → `settings.track_appearance_engine`, `"off"` passes through, and deciding what serves it is `session.py`'s `_resolve_appearance_extractor` (constructibility IS availability for an extractor — no `CameraPose`-style gate to check). `cost_weights: assign.AssignWeights` / `cost_gates: assign.AssignGates` (wave C3) are built DIRECTLY by `resolve()` from the seven `CV_TRACK_COST_*` `Settings` fields — no wire field exists for either, so this is `resolve()`'s "the only place a sentinel becomes a number" extended to "and the only place these become an `AssignWeights`/`AssignGates`"; `session.py`'s `_run_cost_associate` may still zero `cost_weights.appearance` at RUNTIME for a stream where no appearance extractor resolved, which needs state `resolve()` doesn't have. `memory_params: memory.MemoryParams` (wave C4) is built the SAME way — `ttl_millis` uses the wire's `<=0 = deployment default` sentinel (`request.memory_ttl_millis`, same shape `verify_every_millis` uses), the other six fields come straight from `Settings`. Unlike `motion_engine_id`, a resolved `ttl_millis <= 0` is NOT a value `session.py` interprets further with its own policy ladder — it means memory is off, full stop, and `session.py`'s `_resolve_memory` reads it directly. `normalize_mode(str) -> str` (an unknown mode from a newer client → OFF). `resolve(request, settings) -> TrackingParams` — **the only place a `<=0` sentinel becomes a number**; the engine default is per mode. Imports `cv_service.tracking.assign` AND `cv_service.tracking.memory` at module scope (for `AssignWeights`/`AssignGates`/`MemoryParams`) — still pure stdlib, since both of those modules are (P3 unaffected). `TrackingRequest.capability_level: int = 0` (wire field 11, TRACKING-V3-PLAN wave V1) / `TrackingParams.capability_level: int` join the field list, resolved through the SAME `<=0 = server default` shape `verify_every_millis` uses — with one exception: unlike every other int field, the value `resolve()` hands to `TrackingParams` may ITSELF still be `0` (auto-probe, a legitimate resolved value, not merely "unset"), the same "resolve the sentinel here, decide what serves it there" split `motion_engine_id`'s own docstring draws. `session.py`'s `_resolve_capability_level` (`cv_service.tracking.levels.probe`/`resolve`) is what turns it into an actual served level. `TrackingRequest.reupdate_max_gap_millis: int = 0` (wire field 14, TRACKING-V3-PLAN wave V3) / `TrackingParams.reupdate_max_gap_millis: int` join the field list, resolved through the exact `<=0 = server default` shape `memory_ttl_millis` uses (not `verify_every_millis`'s — the DEPLOYMENT default itself may legitimately be `<=0` too, per `config.py`'s own field above) — unlike `capability_level`, once resolved this field is never itself a value the reader interprets further: `<=0` means ORU (and its `session.py` fallback) are off for this stream, full stop, and both `reupdate.py`/`_select_target` read it straight.
- **`levels.py`** (NEW, TRACKING-V3-PLAN wave V1, §5) — pure stdlib, no `cv_service` imports of its own, including of `config.py` (deliberately dependency-free — see the module's own docstring). `LEVEL_L1`..`LEVEL_L5: int` (`1`..`5`) + `LEVEL_NAMES`/`name(level)` (`RELAY`/`FILL`/`DETECT`/`IDENTIFY`/`STUDY`); `MIN_LEVEL`/`MAX_LEVEL`. `probe() -> int` — the highest level THIS HOST affords right now, checked entirely via `importlib.util.find_spec` (never `import`, which would itself pay the weight L1 exists to avoid): no `cv2`+`numpy` → L1; no `ultralytics`+`torch` → L2; below an available-memory floor (`/proc/meminfo`'s `MemAvailable`, falling back to `os.sysconf`) — 512 MiB, deliberately above the measured 347 MiB `YoloDetector` RSS (§5.1), so a host with barely enough for the detector and nothing left over is correctly denied L3 rather than probing green and failing under real load — → L2; no importable `openvino` → L3; else L4. Never caches — a re-probe costs a handful of `find_spec` calls plus one file read, cheap enough that a caller re-probing on every config change sees a busy host's headroom shrink in real time (decision E12). **Never auto-selects L5**: L5 (`tools/trackeval`/training/capture) adds no new importable dependency beyond what already ships in this package, and its own resource profile is unmeasured anywhere in §5.1, so inventing a threshold for it would be exactly the unmeasured guess decision E9 warns against. L5 is reachable only by an EXPLICIT `capability_level=5` request, capped by whatever `probe()` actually found (typically L4) — the same "measured floor, deliberate ceiling" shape `vision.training.enabled` already uses on the Java side for this identical feature. Both the 512 MiB floor and "never auto-L5" are judgment calls the plan's own §5 leaves open, flagged here rather than pinned silently. `resolve(requested, probed) -> (served, reason)` — decision E12's whole ceiling arithmetic: `requested <= 0` auto-probes (`served = probed`, `reason = ""`); a positive `requested` is a CEILING (`served = min(requested, probed)`), `reason` is `""` exactly when nothing was capped. Clamps both inputs into `[MIN_LEVEL, MAX_LEVEL]` first, never raises.
- **`scheduler.py`** — `REASON_*: str` (match `cv_pb2.DetectorReason` value names). `@dataclass(frozen=True) Decision(run_detector, reason)`; `reason` is `UNSPECIFIED` exactly when `run_detector` is False. `@dataclass(frozen=True) SchedulerState(mode, has_lock, last_detector_millis, tracker_failed, box_invalid, coasting_frames)` — everything `decide()` may look at, as a value. `DutyCycleScheduler(params)` with `.decide(now_millis, state) -> Decision` and `.retune(params)`. In FOLLOW the triggers are evaluated most-specific-first, so a frame that is both past cadence and holding no lock reports `NO_LOCK`. **No cadence/threshold/count literal appears in this file.**
- **`track.py`** — `STATE_*: str` (match `cv_pb2.TrackState`). `@dataclass Track(track_id, key, box, label, confidence, first_seen, last_seen, last_confirmed, state, source, velocity_x, velocity_y, age_frames, hits, misses, descriptor, history, history_transform, reupdated)`. `history_transform: Transform = IDENTITY` (TRACKING-V3-PLAN wave V3, §4.1b) — "the transform from `history.latest()`'s own capture frame to wherever `box` is expressed NOW", composed forward by `TrackBook.warp()` every frame (the SAME per-frame `Transform` that already warps `box`/`velocity_x/y` — one extra `Transform.compose`, O(1) per live track per frame, not O(ring capacity)) and reset to `IDENTITY` by `_observe` exactly when the ring admits a genuinely NEW entry; `reupdate.py`'s `reupdate()` is the one reader — see "Observation-Centric Re-Update" below. `reupdated: bool = False` (wave V3) is a THIS-FRAME flag, not persistent state: `_observe` resets it to `False` on every call before deciding whether ORU actually ran, same "event on this one frame" shape `identity_confidence`/`dormant_millis` already use on `session.py`'s `TrackedBox` — safe to read directly off `Track` (rather than moved onto `TrackedBox`) for the same reason `velocity_x`/`state`/`age_frames` already are: every code path that emits a `Detection` for a track called `_observe` on it THIS frame. `last_confirmed` (wave C1) moves ONLY on a `SOURCE_DETECTOR` observation — never on a tracker-only touch, unlike `last_seen`, which is why `predict.py`'s confidence decay and `_settle`'s wall-clock LOST rule read this field, not that one. `descriptor: Optional[Descriptor] = None` (wave C3) is EMA-updated by `observe_descriptor` below, never inside `_observe` itself. `history: ObservationRing` (TRACKING-V3-PLAN wave V2, `history.py` — a fresh, never-shared ring per `Track` via `default_factory`): `_born`/`_adopt`/`_observe` each call `track.history.record(observation, now)` unconditionally on every booking path, and `ObservationRing.record` itself — not the call site — decides whether an observation survives; see the `history.py` bullet below. **`_observe` is the first reader (wave V3)** — see "Observation-Centric Re-Update" below. `@dataclass(frozen=True) RecoveredIdentity(track_id, first_seen, descriptor=None, velocity=(0.0, 0.0))` (wave C4) — what `apply()` needs to book an observation under a REMEMBERED id; built by `session.py` from `ObjectMemory.claim()`'s return value, the one place a recovery's facts cross into the book (mirrors `Observation`'s own role for a detection's facts). `TrackBook(params, *, memory=None)`: `.apply(observations, now, *, detector_ran, recoveries=None) -> list[Track]` (books in order, ages the untouched, settles every state, expires), `.tracks`, `.get(track_id)`, `.retune(params)`, `.bump_epoch()`, `.forget_keys()`, `.set_memory(memory)` (wave C4). `.last_reupdate_millis`/`.last_reupdated_tracks: int` properties + `.reset_reupdate_stats() -> None` (wave V3) — this frame's ORU cost/count (`DetectionResponse.reupdate_millis`/`.reupdated_tracks`, wire fields 22/23); `reset_reupdate_stats()` is called by `session.py`'s `process()` BEFORE the mode dispatch, every frame, NOT inside `apply()` itself — `apply()` only runs from SOME of `process()`'s branches (never `OFF`), so resetting inside it would leave a frame that ran no tracking at all reporting the LAST active frame's numbers; the properties are read AFTER the one `apply()` call a frame is ever allowed to make. **The book allocates track ids, not the engine** — see the Gotcha on ByteTrack's global counter. `.bump_epoch()` (wave C1, review findings D1/D2) namespaces future engine keys into a fresh epoch **without retiring a single live track** — the caller that wants an actual wipe (tracking going OFF) still calls `.forget_keys()`. `observation_for(detection, key, *, det_index, source, authoritative)` adapts a duck-typed `Detection`. Velocities are normalized units/second; `age_frames` is 0 on the birth frame. `_settle` declares `STATE_LOST` on **either** of two independent rules now (wave C1, review finding B7) — see "Tracking engine" below for what each means. **`.warp(transform) -> None`** (wave C2, TRACKING-V2-PLAN — see "Ego-motion compensation" below for the defect this fixes) — maps every LIVE track's stored `box` (full affine) and `velocity_x`/`velocity_y` (the transform's linear part only, no translation) through `transform`, in place; a no-op for `IDENTITY`. Called once per frame in `session.py`'s `process()`, before `apply()` runs and before anything reads a track — this is what makes a stalled track accumulate N frames of camera motion instead of picking up only the last frame's delta whenever something finally reads it. **Still does not touch `history` itself** (wave V2) — a recorded `Observation.box` stays expressed in whatever frame it was captured in, forever. **What wave V3 adds instead**: this same loop also composes `track.history_transform = track.history_transform.compose(transform)` for every live track, so a reader (`reupdate.py`) can warp a stored box into the current frame ON DEMAND without this method ever touching a ring entry — see the `Track.history_transform` note above and "Observation-Centric Re-Update" below for the measurement that chose this over re-warping the whole ring here. **`observe_descriptor(track, descriptor, *, alpha=_DESCRIPTOR_SMOOTHING) -> None`** (wave C3, module-level function, not a `TrackBook` method — `session.py`'s `_run_cost_associate` calls it directly on a `Track` it already has from `TrackBook.apply()`'s own return value) — EMAs `track.descriptor` toward `descriptor` (`Descriptor.blend`, renormalized), or sets it outright if `track.descriptor is None`; a `None` `descriptor` (no appearance evidence this frame, or none for this box) is a no-op, never erasing an already-accumulated signature. Lives here, not in `session.py`, for the SAME reason `.warp()` does: this class's own docstring says "nothing outside this module mutates a `Track`", so the mutation has to happen inside a function `track.py` defines even when `session.py` decides WHEN to call it. **`_adopt(observation, recovery, now) -> Track`** (wave C4, private, called from `apply()`'s own booking loop, ONLY when `book_key` is not already tracked) — the `_born()` counterpart for a REMEMBERED id: books `recovery.track_id` exactly as given (never `self._next_id`, which is instead bumped past it defensively), sets `_confirmed=True` outright (the same anti-flicker carve-out `Observation.authoritative` already documents — an id the operator has already seen confirmed does not re-earn `min_hits`), and seeds `first_seen`/`descriptor`/`velocity_x`/`velocity_y` from `recovery` rather than starting cold. **`_retire(track, now) -> None`** (wave C4, private, called ONLY from `_expire()`, which is itself the ONLY place a track leaves `self._tracks` for an "it might come back" reason) — hands `track` to `self._memory.remember(...)` (a no-op, P5, when `self._memory is None`) immediately before it is deleted. Routing every such deletion through this one method — rather than a bare `del self._tracks[key]` inline in `_expire`'s loop — is what makes it structurally hard for a future deletion path to reintroduce the exact defect this wave closes: a track that ceases to exist without ever having been remembered.
- **`history.py`** (NEW, TRACKING-V3-PLAN wave V2, §4.1) — pure stdlib (`collections.deque`, `dataclasses` only), importable at capability level L1 (invariant P8), same as `predict.py`/`track.py`. `@dataclass(frozen=True) TimedObservation(timestamp, observation)` pairs a recorded `Observation` with the clock reading it arrived at — `Observation` itself carries no timestamp, and wave V3's `reupdate.py` needs BOTH bracketing endpoints' timestamps to compute ORU's interpolation fraction (§4.2), not just their boxes. This is a deliberate departure from the plan's own §4.1 sketch (`before()`/`span()` returning bare `Observation`s), made and documented during implementation, not silently changed. `class ObservationRing(capacity=_DEFAULT_CAPACITY)`: `.record(observation, now) -> None` (silent no-op unless `observation.source == SOURCE_DETECTOR and not observation.predicted` — the ring itself owns this decision, not its callers), `.latest() -> TimedObservation | None`, `.before(timestamp) -> TimedObservation | None` (the last REAL observation strictly before `timestamp` — ORU's `t1`, wave V3), `.span(frames) -> tuple[TimedObservation, TimedObservation] | None` (oldest-first: the latest REAL observation paired with the one `frames` REAL observations before it — OCM's direction pair, wave V4; `frames` counts RECORDED entries, not raw video frames, since only `SOURCE_DETECTOR` evidence is ever recorded), `.capacity`, `__len__`. Bounded by construction (`deque(maxlen=capacity)`) — no separate trim step, never grows past `capacity` no matter how long the stream runs. `_DEFAULT_CAPACITY = 16` — sized against OCM's default span (`momentum_span_frames = 3`) with headroom, following `_LOST_RETENTION_MULTIPLIER`'s own "double the nominal" idiom (`track.py`, above) applied twice over (3 → 6 → ~16) — a reasoned number, not a round one; see the constant's own comment for the full derivation. `TrackingConfig.history_size` (TRACKING-V3-PLAN §3, wire field 12 — not yet in `cv.proto`) is NOT wired through `params.py`/`TrackingRequest` by this wave (outside its file scope): every `Track` uses this default until a later wave threads the `<=0 = server default` sentinel shape `verify_every_millis` already uses.

  **The load-bearing rule: `SOURCE_DETECTOR` only, never merely "not predicted".** `SOURCE_TRACKER` observations reach `record()` in TWO shapes: `predicted=True` (a coast on a stalled tracker) and `predicted=False` (LK/NCC's own successful per-frame optical-flow/template match). **Both are excluded** — the second is the non-obvious one: it is a real pixel measurement, but it is the visual tracker's OWN local estimate of where the target went, not independent evidence, so admitting it would let wave V3's ORU bracket a gap with exactly the drift ORU exists to delete (`Observation.predicted`'s own docstring, `engines/base.py`). FOLLOW's operator-chosen lock re-anchor needs no special case: `observation_for`'s default `source=SOURCE_DETECTOR` (`track.py`, above) means it is already real evidence by the `source` test alone.

  **Store, don't copy.** `Observation`/`Box` are frozen dataclasses (`engines/base.py`) — nothing about a stored entry can change after recording, which is what makes storing a reference safe. This is NOT the `Track`/`TrackedBox.track` mutable-aliasing hazard documented under `session.py`'s entry above (a *mutable* record handed out live, so a later mutation is visible through an old reference) — there is no mutable state here to alias.

  **Zero behavioural delta, as of wave V2 itself.** The ring was populated on every booking path but read by nothing yet at V2 — verified, not assumed: the full `tools/trackeval` scoreboard (below) reproduced byte-identical accuracy columns before and after that wave. `reupdate.py`'s `.before()` (wave V3) is the first actual reader; `assign.py`'s momentum term (`.span()`, wave V4) is still to come.

  **Closed by wave V3: which frame is a ring entry expressed in?** `TrackBook.warp()` (above) moves every live track's `box` into the current frame every frame; ring entries themselves are still NOT warped — each stays in the camera frame it was captured in, forever. The three candidate resolutions this paragraph used to leave open are decided: `Track.history_transform` (see the `track.py` bullet above) accumulates the SAME per-frame warp separately, so `reupdate.py` can correct a ring entry into the current frame on read without this module or `track.py`'s `warp()` needing to touch a ring entry at all. Full measurement (`pan_occlusion`, 160 px of error uncorrected vs. 0 corrected) in "Observation-Centric Re-Update" below.
- **`reupdate.py`** (NEW, TRACKING-V3-PLAN wave V3, §4.2) — ORU, the Observation-Centric Re-Update. Pure stdlib (`math`, `dataclasses` only), importable at capability level L1 (invariant P8), same as `history.py`/`predict.py` — this wave's entire accuracy win runs on the ARMv6 companion. `@dataclass(frozen=True) Reupdate(steps, gap_millis, drift_before, drift_after, velocity_x, velocity_y)` — `steps` is `max(1, track.misses)` at call time (diagnostic only, nothing downstream keys behaviour off it); `drift_before` is `|track.box − z̃(t₂)|` at re-anchor, normalized (exactly the error ORU deletes); `drift_after` is always `0.0` by construction (`z̃(t₂)` IS the real detection `track.box` gets set to immediately after this runs); `velocity_x`/`velocity_y` — the one field §4.2's own sketch omitted despite describing exactly the value a caller needs, recorded in this module's own docstring as the one place the plan turned out underspecified. `reupdate(track, ring, observation, now, *, max_gap_millis) -> Reupdate | None` — `None` when `ring.before(now)` finds no bracketing pair, the gap is non-positive, or it exceeds `max_gap_millis` (too long to reconstruct honestly; `memory.py`'s dormant-gallery recovery serves that case instead, consulting no ring at all). `ring` is an explicit parameter, never read off `track` internally, so this stays testable against a bare `ObservationRing` with no `Track` to construct — the same reason `history.py` itself is a separate structure from `Track`. **This function is the ONLY source of `t₁`** — never `track.box`/`track.velocity_*`, which are the estimator's own drifted state, the exact error this function exists to correct. Warps the bracket through `track.history_transform` (`.apply_box`) before interpolating — see "Observation-Centric Re-Update" below for the full §4.1b measurement. `track.py`'s `_observe` is the sole caller.
- **`lock.py`** — `@dataclass(frozen=True) LockTarget(track_id, point, box)`. `LockArbiter`: `.apply(LockRequest|None) -> bool` (**strictly-greater `lock_seq` only**, so restating the same lock every frame is a no-op), `.bind(track_id)`/`.unbind()`/`.drop()`, `.target`, `.bound_track_id`, `.generation`, `.applied_seq`. Plus two pure selectors used by the session: `select_by_point(boxes, x, y)` (smallest containing box, else nearest centre) and `best_iou_match(box, candidates, min_iou)` (the §3.1 re-anchor test).
- **`assign.py`** (landed at C0, WIRED IN at wave C3 — **frozen this wave, not touched**; see "Association inversion" below) — pure stdlib, property-tested against brute force. `@dataclass(frozen=True) Candidate(key, box, label, descriptor, confirmed)` — one live track, already predicted+warped by the caller. `@dataclass(frozen=True) Target(box, label, confidence, descriptor, det_index)` — one detection. `@dataclass(frozen=True) Assignment(matches, unmatched_candidates, unmatched_targets)`. `@dataclass(frozen=True) AssignWeights(iou=1.0, appearance=0.0, label=0.0)` / `AssignGates(min_iou=0.0, max_appearance=1.0, max_cost=FORBIDDEN, high_confidence=0.0)` — the defaults are "pure geometry, no gates" so a caller with no appearance engine gets exactly today's behavior without stating it. `class CostAssociator`: `engine_id = "cost"`, `.cost(candidate, target) -> float` (the ONE place the identity cost is defined; `FORBIDDEN` for any gated-out pair), `.assign(candidates, targets) -> Assignment` (two stages: high-confidence targets first, then low — ByteTrack's own insight, kept), `.retune(*, weights, gates)`. `_appearance_distance(left, right)` — a missing descriptor scores the NEUTRAL midpoint (`0.5`), never the maximum: "no evidence" must not be punished like "evidence says no". `hungarian(cost) -> list[(row, column)]` — O(n³) shortest-augmenting-path with potentials, pure stdlib (no `lap`/`scipy`, which would break the "no `cv` extra needed" invariant); `FORBIDDEN` pairs are never returned, even from a square matrix. `session.py`'s `_run_cost_associate` is the ONLY caller — see that entry below for how `Candidate`/`Target` are built and how the `Assignment` is booked back onto `TrackBook`.
- **`memory.py`** (landed at C0, **WIRED IN at wave C4 — frozen this wave, not touched**; see "Object memory" below) — the dormant gallery: `MemoryParams`, `ObjectMemory` (`.remember`/`.match`/`.forget_expired`/`.claim`/`.size`/`.identities`/`.clear`/`.retune`), four required match gates (label, appearance, motion plausibility, TTL). `TrackBook._retire` (`track.py`) is the ONLY caller of `.remember`; `session.py`'s `_attempt_recovery` is the ONLY caller of `.match`/`.claim`. Pure stdlib, unmodified since C0 — this wave is entirely about the two callers, not this module.
- **`engines/base.py`** — `SOURCE_DETECTOR`/`SOURCE_TRACKER: str`. `@dataclass(frozen=True) Box(x, y, width, height)` normalized, with `.center`, `.valid` (threshold-free: non-positive extent, or zero overlap with the unit square), `.iou(other)`, `.contains(x, y)`. `@dataclass(frozen=True) Observation(key, box, label, confidence, source, det_index, authoritative)`. `@dataclass(frozen=True) TrackerUpdate(box, confidence)`. `class Associator(Protocol)`: `engine_id`, `associate(detections, now) -> list[Observation]`, `reset()`. `class SingleObjectTracker(Protocol)`: `engine_id`, `init(frame, box) -> bool`, `update(frame) -> TrackerUpdate | None`, `reset()`. **Wave C2 additions** (landed at C0, wired into `registry.py`/`session.py`/`servicers.py` at C2): `@dataclass(frozen=True) CameraPose(yaw_degrees, pitch_degrees, roll_degrees, hfov_degrees, vfov_degrees, timestamp_millis)` + `.known` (`hfov_degrees > 0`); `@dataclass(frozen=True) Transform(a, b, c, d, e, f)` — normalized-image affine, `x'=a·x+b·y+c`, `y'=d·x+e·y+f` — + `.identity`, `.apply_point`, `.apply_box` (corner-transform, re-axis-aligned), `.compose`; `IDENTITY = Transform()`. **Direction is part of the contract**: a `Transform` maps a point in the PREVIOUS frame to where it appears in the CURRENT one — see the Gotcha below, and `pose_gmc.py`'s/`flow_gmc.py`'s own docstrings, for why this is called out explicitly rather than left implicit. `class MotionCompensator(Protocol)`: `engine_id`, `available(pose) -> bool`, `estimate(frame, pose) -> Transform`, `reset()`. **Wave C3 additions** (also landed at C0, wired into `registry.py`/`session.py`/`engines/histogram.py` at C3): `METRIC_HELLINGER`/`METRIC_COSINE: str` — a descriptor's metric travels WITH it, so an embedding engine dropped in later is a new constant plus one branch, not a change to every caller. `@dataclass(frozen=True) Descriptor(engine_id, values, metric=METRIC_HELLINGER)` — `.distance(other) -> float` on `[0, 1]` (`1.0` = "no usable comparison", covering a different engine/metric/length or an absent `other`, never raises); `.blend(other, alpha) -> Descriptor` — EMA, renormalized for the metric (Hellinger: L1 back onto the simplex; cosine: back onto the unit sphere) so a repeatedly-blended descriptor's metric stays bounded. `class AppearanceExtractor(Protocol)`: `engine_id`, `describe(frame, boxes) -> list[Descriptor | None]` (positional, `None` for any box it cannot describe), `reset()`.
- **`engines/bytetrack.py`** — `ENGINE_ID = "bytetrack"`; `ByteTrackEngine(max_age_frames=...)` (an `Associator`) and `create(*, max_age_frames)`. Adapts plain normalized `Detection`s to the minimal `Results`-like view `BYTETracker.update` consumes.
- **`engines/lk.py` / `engines/ncc.py`** — `ENGINE_ID`; `LkFlowEngine()` / `NccEngine()` (both `SingleObjectTracker`) and `create()`. `init()` returns **False**, never raises, for a target the engine cannot hold (featureless patch, sub-2px box). `lk.py` (wave C1, review finding C3): `update()` now rejects corners via a forward-backward round trip (`_forward_backward_inliers`, `_FB_ERROR_MAX_PIXELS`) before trusting them, and re-seeds the surviving set (`_reseeded`, masked to the CURRENT box) once it thins past `_RESEED_FRACTION` of what `init()` started with — see "Tracking engine" below and the Gotchas for why the corner count used to only ever decay.
- **`engines/pose_gmc.py`** (module landed at C0, wired into the registry/session at wave C2) — `ENGINE_ID = "pose"`; `PoseMotionCompensator(*, aspect_ratio=0.0)` (a `MotionCompensator`) and `create(*, aspect_ratio=0.0)`. Pure stdlib (`math` only) — trigonometry, not image processing, which is what makes it the one compensator available on a build with no `cv` extra at all. `available(pose)` is `pose.known`. `estimate(frame, pose)` ignores `frame`, holds the PREVIOUS pose as per-stream state, and returns `IDENTITY` for: no pose, first pose of a stream, a stale pose (`_MAX_POSE_GAP_MILLIS`, only checked when the client actually stamps `pose_timestamp_millis`), an implausible attitude step (`_MAX_STEP_DEGREES` — a telemetry glitch/reconnect, not a real camera movement), or a step below telemetry noise (`_MIN_STEP_DEGREES`, returned as the exact `IDENTITY` singleton so `.identity` stays an exact comparison). Otherwise: yaw/pitch become a first-order translation (`_normalized_shift`, `du = -d_yaw / tan(hfov/2)`, `dv = +d_pitch / tan(vfov/2)`, halved for the `[0,1]` convention), roll becomes a rotation about the frame centre corrected for the frame aspect (`_roll_transform` — without the correction a roll on a non-square frame is a shear, not a rotation), composed via `Transform.compose`.
- **`engines/flow_gmc.py`** (NEW, wave C2) — `ENGINE_ID = "flow"`; `FlowMotionCompensator()` (a `MotionCompensator`) and `create()`. Needs `cv2`/`numpy` — the second of the two named exceptions to `engines/`'s pure-stdlib-outside-these-two rule (P3), alongside `bytetrack.py`/`lk.py`/`ncc.py`. `available(pose)` is always `True` — no telemetry needed. `estimate(frame, pose)` ignores `pose`; holds the PREVIOUS greyscale frame as per-stream state; `cv2.goodFeaturesToTrack` over the **whole** frame (unmasked — deliberately does not exclude the tracked target, see the module docstring for why), `cv2.calcOpticalFlowPyrLK` to the current frame, `cv2.estimateAffinePartial2D(..., method=cv2.RANSAC)` for the global fit. Returns `IDENTITY` for: no frame, a first frame, a frame-size change, too few tracked points (`_MIN_TRACKED_POINTS`), a failed fit, an inlier ratio below `_MIN_INLIER_RATIO` (the moving-subject-fills-the-frame guard — a foreground object contributing a comparable share of the point cloud splits RANSAC's vote, and a confident wrong answer is worse than none), or a fit whose linear part's determinant strays too far from 1 (`_MAX_DETERMINANT_DEVIATION` — a degenerate, non-similarity fit). The pixel-space affine → normalized `Transform` conversion (`_normalized_transform`) is the one place a sign or scale error would be invisible in isolation, so it has its own explicit test (`tests/tracking/test_flow_gmc.py`): a translation term divides by the matching dimension (`c = m02 / width`), a linear term additionally picks up the frame's aspect ratio (`b = m01 / aspect`, `d = m10 · aspect`) because `[0,1]` x and y are not the same physical distance on a non-square frame.
