- **ASSOCIATE is NOT compensated.** `_estimate_motion` is gated on `self._params.mode == MODE_FOLLOW`, so an ASSOCIATE stream never resolves a motion compensator and never decodes a frame for motion purposes (consistent with ASSOCIATE never touching pixels for anything else today). `DetectionResponse.motion_millis`/`motion_engine_id` are `0`/`""` on every ASSOCIATE (and OFF) response. `TrackBook.warp()` still runs unconditionally every frame regardless of mode, but it is a guaranteed no-op there — `transform` is only ever non-`IDENTITY` when `MODE_FOLLOW` resolved a real compensator.
- **This is a falsifiable, deliberately-stated prediction, not an oversight**: the `pan | ASSOCIATE` row in the harness below is EXPECTED not to move in this wave. Wave C3, which gives cv-service its own association core, is what has to move it.

**Warping the STATE, not the read — the fix for an undercorrection found in review.** The first cut of this wave threaded `transform` as an argument into `predict(track, now, transform)`, applied once wherever a call site happened to read a track. That looked right in isolation and passed every unit test, but it undercorrected badly in exactly the case ego-motion compensation exists for: a track stalled for N frames (the SOT engine lost it, nothing but prediction is left) only ever picked up the CURRENT frame's single-frame delta at the moment something finally called `predict()` — never the N frames of camera motion that had accumulated while nothing read it. A target hidden for 30 frames behind an occluder while the camera panned 0.005 frame-widths/frame got a 0.005 correction instead of 0.150 — a 30x undercorrection, silent because every individual unit test only ever checked ONE call. The fix: **`TrackBook.warp(transform)`**, called once per frame in `process()` right after the transform is estimated and BEFORE anything reads a track box (prediction, the re-anchor test, a lock-by-id lookup, coasting) — it maps every LIVE track's stored `box` (full affine) and `velocity_x`/`velocity_y` (the transform's LINEAR part only — `a`/`b`/`d`/`e`, no `c`/`f`, since a rate has no position to translate) through `transform`, mutating the `Track` in place (it is a mutable dataclass by design). No-op for `IDENTITY` (`if transform.identity: return`, before the loop even starts). `predict()` (`predict.py`) now takes **no `transform` argument at all** — by the time it runs, `track.box`/`velocity_*` are already expressed in the CURRENT frame's coordinates, because the book carried them there one frame at a time. The accumulation invariant has its own tests at both layers: `tests/tracking/test_track.py::test_n_consecutive_identical_warps_move_a_track_by_n_steps_not_one` (direct, book-level: 30 identical warps move a track's box by 30 steps) and `tests/tracking/test_session.py::test_n_stalled_frames_accumulate_n_warps_not_one` (end to end: 20 consecutive stalled FOLLOW frames against a fixed per-frame transform).

**Selection policy (`StreamTrackingSession._resolve_motion_compensator`).** `TrackingParams.motion_engine_id` (wire field 8; `""` → `CV_TRACK_MOTION_ENGINE`, default `"flow"`; `"off"` disables outright and skips the registry entirely) resolves to an actual engine once per stream, lazily, on the first active FOLLOW frame: `"pose"` is always constructible (pure `math`) but is checked against a LIVE `CameraPose` — if `available(pose)` is `False` (no `hfov_degrees` on the wire, the normal state today since no Java client populates `camera_pose` yet), the session falls back to `"flow"`, logged once per engine id. If nothing at all is constructible, no compensation runs — never an exception, never a dead stream (P5), same posture as every other degradation in this file.

**Cost discipline.** `flow` needs decoded pixels; FOLLOW already decodes the frame for the SOT engine (`lk`/`ncc`), and `_estimate_motion` reuses the SAME memoized `frame()` callable the servicer supplies (`_frame_loader`) rather than decoding again. In ASSOCIATE the session still never touches pixels at all — unaffected by this wave. **Future work, not done here**: when C3 gives ASSOCIATE its own compensable association core, that mode will need its own frame decode for `flow` (today's `_frame_loader` is lazy specifically because ASSOCIATE never calls it) — a real, new per-frame cost that did not exist before, to be measured and paid for explicitly rather than inherited silently.

**Wire plumbing (`grpc/servicers.py`).** `_camera_pose_from_wire` maps `cv_pb2.CameraPose` → the plain `CameraPose`; `_handle_request` passes `pose=_camera_pose_from_wire(request.camera_pose)` into `session.process(...)` only on the active-tracking branch, so `OFF` never touches it (P1 unaffected — verified by the existing byte-identical test, still green). `FrameOutcome.motion_millis`/`motion_engine_id` → `DetectionResponse` fields 14/15 via `_tracked_response`.

**Harness numbers (`tools/trackeval`).** On the original five scenarios, every accuracy column (`IDSW`/`FM`/`MT`/`PT`/`ML`/`recovery%`/`life_mean`) stays **byte-for-byte identical before vs after this wave** — zero regressions. `pan | ASSOCIATE` is unchanged as designed (the falsifiable prediction above, not yet moved — that is wave C3's job). `pan | FOLLOW` is also unchanged (`MT=0`, `PT=1`): traced frame-by-frame, `pan`'s FOLLOW target (`primary_gt_id=1`) pans permanently OUT of frame by frame ~14 of 70 and never returns, so (a) while genuinely visible `lk`'s own real optical flow already tracks it correctly without any compensation, and (b) once invisible, `metrics.py` never scores it again regardless of prediction quality — the compensated code path DOES run (frames 15–61 coast on `predict()`, confirmed by tracing), its accuracy is just structurally unobservable by a scenario whose existence-window spans the WHOLE clip for an object visible only ~14–47% of it, below the 80% MT threshold no matter how good the tracking is. Not a defect; a harness-scenario limitation, out of scope to fix (`tools/`) this wave.

A second scenario, **`pan_step`, exists specifically to force the fork this wave's fix is FOR** (see the scenario table below) — a world-static target and a world-placed distractor, both hidden behind a uniform occluder exactly as the camera starts to pan, so the SOT stalls, the learned velocity is stale, and the distractor sits precisely where an uncompensated prediction still points when the bar clears. Running it two ways:

```bash
PYTHONPATH="$PWD" CV_TRACK_MOTION_ENGINE=off  .venv/bin/python -m tools.trackeval --scenario pan_step --mode FOLLOW
PYTHONPATH="$PWD" CV_TRACK_MOTION_ENGINE=flow .venv/bin/python -m tools.trackeval --scenario pan_step --mode FOLLOW
```

produces **byte-identical metrics tables** — but this is NOT evidence the fix is a no-op, and the honest reason takes two layers to state:

1. **At the time this was diagnosed, `run_replay`'s own default was a bare `Settings()` construction, never `Settings.from_env()`, so the CLI silently ignored every `CV_TRACK_*` env var** — the two commands above were therefore not actually a controlled A/B. **Corrected within this same wave's commit** (`replay.py`'s `settings = settings or Settings.from_env()`, verified in the source and re-verified by wave C3, which depends on this working correctly for its own harness table below): the CLI now genuinely reads `CV_TRACK_*` env vars through `run_replay`'s own default, so `CV_TRACK_APPEARANCE_ENGINE=off python -m tools.trackeval --engine cost ...` (for example) now toggles for real. What follows below documents the diagnosis AS PERFORMED, with an explicit `settings=` used to bypass the (now-fixed) gap.
2. **Even with the engine genuinely toggled, `pan_step`'s own metrics table does not move — but the underlying position DOES, correctly, and a second, unrelated, pre-existing (C1-era) defect is what hides it.** Traced frame-by-frame with an explicit `settings=`: at frame 71 (the first verify pass after the bar clears), the held/predicted box's X position is 0.446 with `flow` vs 0.620 with `off`, against a true target X of 0.415 — the compensated X is correct within 0.03, the uncompensated one is off by 0.20. But `Track.velocity_y`, measured from the small ground-truth position jitter DURING the static/visible phase (frames 0–29, before any camera motion at all) and never re-measured once the SOT stalls (`_observe`'s velocity recompute on a coasting frame is mathematically a no-op — predicting `box + v·dt` and then re-deriving velocity from that exact delta returns the same `v`), gets extrapolated for the entire 60-frame occlusion. By frame 71 that is a Y drift of ~0.14–0.16 — larger than the box height — which alone drops the re-anchor IoU below the 0.3 threshold **in both conditions equally**, so neither run re-anchors and the metrics table cannot tell them apart. This is orthogonal to ego-motion compensation (it reproduces identically with `transform` forced to `IDENTITY` throughout, i.e. it is entirely a `predict()`/`_observe()` velocity-noise property, present since wave C1, not touched by this wave) — **confirmed as the blocking factor, not merely suspected**: neutralizing `velocity_y` the instant the target goes invisible (a diagnostic probe only, never shipped) reproduces the fork EXACTLY as `pan_step` was designed to show it — `off` locks the final box onto the DISTRACTOR (`IoU=0.983` vs distractor, `0.0` vs target); `flow` locks onto the TRUE TARGET (`IoU=0.957` vs target, `0.0` vs distractor).
3. **Reported, not papered over, per instruction.** This wave's fix (`TrackBook.warp`, the accumulation invariant) is correct and proven at three independent levels — the engine's own direction/magnitude tests, the book-level and session-level accumulation tests, and this diagnostic — but `pan_step`'s metrics table cannot currently show it because of (1) the CLI's pre-existing `Settings()`-not-`from_env()` gap and (2) a genuinely separate, pre-existing velocity-noise-extrapolation defect in `track.py`'s constant-velocity model (no floor or decay on a velocity estimate that turns out to have been measured from position jitter rather than real motion, combined with `last_seen` refreshing every touch so `predict.py`'s `_MAX_EXTRAPOLATION_SECONDS` clamp — which bounds one gap — never bounds the CUMULATIVE drift across many short re-predictions). Neither is `tools/`-scoped to fix here (the first would need a `tools/replay.py`/`__main__.py` change; the second is a `track.py`/`predict.py` velocity-model change well beyond "warp the ego-motion transform through"). Both are recorded as findings for whichever wave picks them up next — the second in particular is worth a look before C4's `ObjectMemory` re-acquisition logic is judged against real drift, since a corrupted Y this large would defeat appearance-gated re-acquisition too, not just IoU.

### Association inversion + appearance descriptors (TRACKING-V2-PLAN wave C3, `docs/conclusions/TRACKING-REVIEW.md` §3 group B / §4.3)

**The defect this closes (REVIEW B1, §4.3's root cause).** Before this wave, `ByteTrackEngine` held the tracks, the motion model and the matching; `TrackBook` ran downstream of it, merely renaming what it was handed. Nothing else — appearance, ego-motion, memory — had a seam to plug into the identity decision, and an IoU-only matcher has no way to break a genuine geometric tie: two similar-sized objects crossing paths is an unresolvable ambiguity for it (`crossing | ASSOCIATE`'s baseline `IDSW=4`). `assign.py`'s `CostAssociator` (frozen, landed at C0) inverts this: the platform (`session.py`) now holds the tracks — `TrackBook`, unchanged since T1 — and asks the matcher for an `Assignment` over `Candidate`s (the book's own live tracks, predicted + ego-motion-warped) and `Target`s (this frame's detections, optionally carrying an appearance descriptor). `bytetrack` remains fully available, unmodified, and selectable — the no-appearance, no-compensation baseline `CV_TRACK_ASSOCIATE_ENGINE` still defaults to.

**Why `bytetrack` cannot be warped but `cost` can, restated precisely.** C2 deferred ASSOCIATE compensation because `bytetrack`'s association state lives entirely inside `ultralytics.BYTETracker`'s own Kalman filters — a third-party object `TrackBook.warp()` has no access to. `cost` has no such state: its candidates ARE `TrackBook`'s own tracks, which `TrackBook.warp()` already carries through every frame of camera motion (C2's own mechanism, unmodified). So `process()`'s compensation gate is now `MODE_FOLLOW OR (MODE_ASSOCIATE AND engine_id == "cost")` — checked against the ACTUAL resolved engine, not the requested one — and this is also why the DEFAULT ASSOCIATE path pays no new frame-decode cost: `_estimate_motion`/`_describe` are only ever reached when `cost` is what is actually serving the stream.

**The cost model, and what each weight/gate defaults to.** `cost(c, t) = w_iou·(1−IoU) + w_app·appearance_distance + w_lab·label_penalty`, gated by `min_iou`/`max_appearance`/`max_cost` before ever being compared, solved in two stages (high-confidence targets first, then low — ByteTrack's own insight, kept). Defaults (`config.py`, all `CV_TRACK_COST_*`): `w_iou=1.0`, `w_app=0.5`, `w_lab=0.0`, `min_iou=0.0` (no geometric floor — a warped-but-imperfect candidate right after a stall must not be forbidden outright; the solver's own global optimality is trusted instead of a hard per-pair cutoff), `max_appearance=0.6`, `max_cost=∞`, `high_confidence=0.25` (matches `bytetrack`'s own `_TRACK_HIGH_THRESH`). `w_app` is configured non-zero so `cost`+the default `histogram` extractor do something out of the box, but `session.py`'s `_run_cost_associate` zeroes it at RUNTIME whenever no appearance extractor actually resolved for the stream (`"off"`, or nothing constructible) — a `cost` stream with no appearance evidence behaves as pure geometry, never geometry-plus-a-constant (`assign.py`'s own `test_appearance_is_ignored_entirely_when_it_is_not_weighted`).

**An unplanned, honest side effect: `occlusion`'s recovery rate moves too, and it is NOT appearance or ego-motion.** `cost`'s candidates are drawn from `self._book.tracks`, which includes `STATE_LOST` tracks TrackBook has not yet `_expire()`d (retained until `misses > max_age_frames × 2` — T1's own, unmodified rule). `bytetrack`'s own internal `track_buffer` is exactly `max_age_frames`, so a track lost that long is already gone from ITS retention when `occlusion`'s 40-frame gap (deliberately longer than the default `max_age_frames=30`) ends. `cost`'s book-backed retention is 2× that, so the SAME still-in-book LOST track is offered as a `Candidate` again once the object reappears, and pure geometry (no appearance, no motion — confirmed by ablation below) re-matches it. This is a preview of what wave C4's `ObjectMemory` formalizes with proper TTL/label/motion/appearance gating — arriving early, here, as an honest side effect of "candidates are TrackBook's own live tracks" (the task's own instruction), not a feature built for it. Flagged so C4 is not measured against a baseline this wave has already partially moved.

**Harness table — every scenario, ASSOCIATE, before vs after, both `cost` configurations** (`tools/trackeval --scenario X --mode ASSOCIATE [--engine cost]`, `CV_TRACK_APPEARANCE_ENGINE` as noted; seed = `DEFAULT_SEED`). FOLLOW rows are omitted because they are **byte-for-byte unaffected** by this wave (verified: full `--all` diff before/after this wave's changes shows zero difference in any accuracy column, on every scenario, both modes — only the timing columns move, and only by measurement noise). Reading the columns: `IDSW`=id switches, `FM`=fragmentations, `MT`/`PT`=mostly/partially-tracked object count, `recov%`=of objects with an internal gap, the fraction that resumed with the SAME id, `life_mean`=mean emitted-track lifetime in frames.

| scenario | engine (config) | IDSW | FM | MT | PT | recov% | life_mean |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `linear` | `bytetrack` (default) | 0 | 0 | 3 | 0 | n/a | 60.0 |
| `linear` | `cost`, appearance off | 0 | 0 | 3 | 0 | n/a | 60.0 |
| `linear` | `cost`, appearance `histogram` (default) | 0 | 0 | 3 | 0 | n/a | 60.0 |
| `occlusion` | `bytetrack` (default) | 1 | 1 | 0 | 1 | **0%** | 34.5 |
| `occlusion` | `cost`, appearance off | **0** | 1 | 0 | 1 | **100%** | 70.0 |
| `occlusion` | `cost`, appearance `histogram` (default) | 0 | 1 | 0 | 1 | 100% | 70.0 |
| `crossing` | `bytetrack` (default) | **4** | 0 | 2 | 0 | n/a | 50.0 |
| `crossing` | `cost`, appearance off | 2 | 0 | 2 | 0 | n/a | 50.0 |
| `crossing` | `cost`, appearance `histogram` (default) | **0** | 0 | 2 | 0 | n/a | 50.0 |
| `pan` | `bytetrack` (default) | 0 | 0 | **0** | 4 | n/a | 24.0–25.0 |
| `pan` | `cost`, appearance off | 0 | 0 | **0** | 4 | n/a | 33.0 |
| `pan` | `cost`, appearance `histogram` (default) | 0 | 0 | **0** | 4 | n/a | 24.8 |
| `pan_step` | `bytetrack` (default) | 2 | 2 | 0 | 2 | **0%** | 39.7 |
| `pan_step` | `cost`, appearance off | **0** | 2 | 0 | 2 | **100%** | 60.0 |
| `pan_step` | `cost`, appearance `histogram` (default) | 0 | 2 | 0 | 2 | 100% | 60.0 |
| `dropout` | `bytetrack` (default) | 0 | 11 | 0 | 1 | 100% | 29.0 |
| `dropout` | `cost`, appearance off | 0 | 11 | 0 | 1 | 100% | 30.0 |
| `dropout` | `cost`, appearance `histogram` (default) | 0 | 11 | 0 | 1 | 100% | 30.0 |

**Reading it row by row, nothing hidden:**

- **`linear`, `dropout`: the floor holds, and detection recall stays isolated from identity.** Byte-identical (mod ±1 frame on `dropout`'s `life_mean`, timing-adjacent, not an accuracy column) across all three configurations — `cost` regresses nothing.
- **`crossing`: THE targeted fix, and appearance is what actually does it.** `bytetrack`'s baseline `IDSW=4` (REVIEW B1, reproduced). `cost` with appearance OFF already halves it to `2` — the Hungarian solver's global optimality alone helps some — but `histogram` appearance is what takes it to **`0`**: verified by direct ablation (`CV_TRACK_MOTION_ENGINE=off CV_TRACK_APPEARANCE_ENGINE=off` reproduces the exact `bytetrack` baseline of `4`, confirming the geometry-only `2` came from a small, genuinely incidental motion-compensation signal on this scenario's own moving objects, not the solver itself). `tests/tracking/test_cost_appearance_integration.py` pins both ends of this.
- **`occlusion`, `pan_step`: real, ablation-confirmed wins, from two DIFFERENT and INDEPENDENT mechanisms.** `occlusion`'s `recov% 0→100` is the `TrackBook`-retention side effect explained above (confirmed by ablation: identical with `CV_TRACK_MOTION_ENGINE=off`). `pan_step`'s `IDSW 2→0`, `recov% 0→100` is ego-motion compensation working exactly as designed — THE case this plan names as the numbers this wave must move — confirmed by ablation the other direction: `CV_TRACK_MOTION_ENGINE=off` on `cost` reproduces the `bytetrack` baseline (`IDSW=2`, `recov%=0`) exactly. (`pan_step | FOLLOW`'s own re-anchor still cannot show this fix, for the pre-existing `track.py` velocity-drift reason documented above under "Ego-motion compensation" point 2 — that defect is FOLLOW-cadence-specific: ASSOCIATE gets a match attempt on the very next frame after the occluder clears, before the drift accumulates as far as FOLLOW's ~2s cadence lets it.)
- **`pan | ASSOCIATE`'s `mostly_tracked=0 of 4`, the plan's own named target — does NOT move, and it is a confirmed harness-scenario limitation, not a code defect.** Directly probed: `engines/flow_gmc.py.estimate()` against `pan`'s own frames returns a REAL, correct transform (`c≈-0.035`, matching `PAN_CAMERA_VELOCITY=0.035`) when a landmark object is on screen to supply corners, and `IDENTITY` on every frame where nothing is visible — because, unlike `pan_step`, `sequences.pan()` renders NO explicit world texture (`_draw_world_texture`, the `underlay=` parameter `pan_step` passes and `pan` does not), so the ONLY corners available are the 1–2 landmark objects' own borders/stripes, present only part of the time. Compensation genuinely runs and is genuinely correct when it can, but too intermittently across this specific scenario's own construction to move a coverage metric gated at 80%. This is the SAME class of finding C2's own MODULE.md section already recorded for `pan_step`'s FOLLOW row (a harness limitation, `tools/`-scoped to fix, out of scope here) — not a new one, and not evidence the ASSOCIATE compensation path is broken; `pan_step | ASSOCIATE`'s clean ablation-confirmed win above is the direct proof it works when the scenario actually supplies texture.
- **`pan | ASSOCIATE`'s `life_mean`: a real, small, honestly-reported cost from appearance's default gate.** `cost`+no-appearance raises `life_mean` `24.0→33.0` (fewer, longer-lived ids — geometry+intermittent compensation alone already helps a little here). Adding `histogram` appearance PULLS IT BACK DOWN to `24.8` — `max_appearance=0.6`'s gate is rejecting some continuations that a partially-visible, edge-of-frame box's noisy colour histogram makes look less similar than the same physical object really is, causing a few extra re-births instead of continuations. No headline identity metric (`IDSW`/`FM`/`MT`/`PT`) moves because of it — it is a fragmentation-cost finding, not a correctness one — but it is reported rather than hidden, per instruction, and is worth a look if a later wave tunes `CV_TRACK_COST_GATE_MAX_APPEARANCE`.

**The default decision, and the evidence for it.** `CV_TRACK_ASSOCIATE_ENGINE` **stays `"bytetrack"`** — `cost` is shipped **opt-in**, selected per-stream via `TrackingConfig.engine_id="cost"` or fleet-wide via the env var. `cost` is better-or-equal to `bytetrack` on every scenario measured above (never strictly worse on any headline column), which is the bar TRACKING-V2-PLAN §5's own flip discipline sets — but the deliberate call here is NOT to flip anyway, for reasons the harness cannot settle: these are six synthetic, low-object-count scenes, and `cost`'s own defaults (`min_iou=0.0` — no geometric floor at all beyond the solver's own optimality; the TrackBook-retention-driven recovery noted above) have not been stress-tested against a cluttered, many-object real scene where a permissive gate could re-identify the WRONG lost track. `bytetrack` is a heavily-used, production-tuned library; `cost` is new code three commits old. **`CV_TRACK_APPEARANCE_ENGINE` defaults to `"histogram"`** — safe to default on regardless, because it only ever activates once an operator has ALREADY opted into `cost` (`bytetrack` never resolves it), and the evidence above shows it net-positive (`crossing`'s decisive fix) with only one small, non-headline cost (`pan`'s `life_mean`). An operator who explicitly asks for `cost` should get its own appearance signal working by default, the same way asking for FOLLOW gets a default SOT engine without a second knob.

**Session-level tests** (`tests/tracking/test_session.py`, pure stdlib — `CostAssociator`/`AssignWeights`/`AssignGates`/`Descriptor` are all pure stdlib, so this file's own "no cv2/numpy/gRPC" contract holds even with `cost` exercised): id stability and birth across frames, motion compensation asked for (and warping the book before matching, proven with a tight IoU gate a stale box could not pass), appearance extraction asked for/degraded/released-on-config-change/off-skips-the-registry, `bytetrack` remaining fully selectable and touching neither motion nor appearance. **Wire-level tests** (`tests/grpc/test_detect_stream_tracking.py`): `appearance_engine_id` (wire field 9) reaching the session end-to-end, `cost` populating `motion_millis`/`motion_engine_id` where `bytetrack` still does not, and a P2 proof (`test_associate_with_cost_motion_and_appearance_never_acquires_the_inference_gate`) that a live motion compensator AND a live appearance extractor on the ASSOCIATE path still never touch `InferenceGate`. **Integration test** (`tests/tracking/test_cost_appearance_integration.py`, needs `cv2`/`numpy`): the acceptance criterion in the operator's own terms, via the REAL harness and its `crossing` scenario — `bytetrack` still swaps ids (`IDSW>0`, pins the baseline this wave is measured against), `cost`+`histogram` swaps none (`IDSW==0`). **New engine tests** (`tests/tracking/test_histogram.py`, needs `cv2`/`numpy`): HSV extraction, `None` for off-frame/sub-pixel/empty boxes, and — the property this engine exists for — two differently-coloured patches are far apart under `Descriptor.distance` while two same-coloured ones are close.

### Object memory (TRACKING-V2-PLAN wave C4, `docs/conclusions/TRACKING-REVIEW.md` §4.4)

**The defect this closes.** Every wave before this one made ASSOCIATION better — a track survives longer, matches more correctly, resists ego-motion and geometric ambiguity — but none of them let an identity survive being **forgotten**. `TrackBook._expire()` (T1, unmodified rule: `misses > max_age_frames × 2`) has always deleted a track outright once it was genuinely gone; a re-appearance after that point was, by construction, a brand-new id. That is the operator's own complaint ("it loses the subject") in its purest form, and it is why this is the wave the whole plan exists for — everything upstream only delays the moment identity gets thrown away, this wave is what stops it being thrown away at all.

**Two structurally-separate additions, one at each end of a track's life.** (1) `TrackBook._retire` (private, called ONLY from `_expire()`) hands a track to `ObjectMemory.remember()` in the SAME method that deletes it — see `track.py`'s own entry above for why this is deliberately NOT a callback/event `session.py` reacts to after the fact: a single, un-bypassable deletion path is what makes "a track that disappears without being remembered" structurally hard to reintroduce, not merely a convention future code has to remember to follow. (2) `session.py`'s `_run_cost_associate` offers every genuinely UNMATCHED target to `ObjectMemory.match()`+`.claim()` (`_attempt_recovery`) BEFORE `TrackBook.apply()` would otherwise mint it a fresh id via `_born()` — a match books the observation under the remembered id instead, via a NEW `TrackBook.apply(..., recoveries={...})` parameter and the private `_adopt()` method (see `track.py`'s entry above for both).

**Why this is `cost`-only, not universal, even though remembering is.** `_resolve_memory()` (unlike `_resolve_appearance_extractor()`) runs unconditionally for every active mode/engine — a `bytetrack`-associate or FOLLOW track that expires is remembered too, for free, since `TrackBook._retire` does not know or care which associator produced the track it is retiring. But only `_run_cost_associate` ever QUERIES the gallery back: `bytetrack` holds its own opaque Kalman state and never exposes an "unmatched target" seam this book-level mechanism could intercept, and FOLLOW's `_follow_key()` deliberately mints a FRESH id on every re-lock (an operator's release-then-relock is a NEW decision, not an occlusion — see that method's own docstring, unchanged by this wave). So a `bytetrack`/FOLLOW track's memory entry sits inert until it expires from the GALLERY too (`ttl_millis`), unless the stream is later switched to `cost` — at which point it becomes genuinely recoverable, which is a real, tested, and deliberate property (`test_a_track_expiring_under_one_associator_is_recovered_after_switching_to_cost`), not an oversight.

**The four gates are `memory.py`'s, untouched — this wave is entirely about wiring, not tuning.** `memory.py` was landed fully-built and fully-tested at C0 and this wave does not modify it: label compatibility, appearance distance, motion plausibility (reach = `max_speed·elapsed + box slack`, exact — a candidate beyond it is REJECTED, not scored low), and TTL, all four required, confidence always REPORTED not assumed. The one new integration risk is entirely on the calling side: two unmatched targets in the SAME frame can both score above threshold against the SAME dormant identity (a crowd is exactly where this happens). `ObjectMemory.claim()` only pops an entry once, so `_attempt_recovery` returning `None` for the SECOND target is not a bug to fix — the confidence-losing target falls back to a fresh id exactly as if nothing had matched, which is the refuse-rather-than-guess posture the whole gallery is built on. No gate was loosened to make `long_occlusion` pass (see the harness table below) — the number moved because the wiring finally existed for the gates to fire on, not because they fire more permissively.

**Honest reporting on the wire (`Detection.identity_confidence`/`.dormant_millis`, fields 10/11, frozen at C0).** Both are properties of a recovery EVENT, not of the persisting `Track` — they live on `session.py`'s `TrackedBox` (per-frame), not on `track.py`'s `Track` (persists across many frames), and are `0.0`/`0` on every frame that is not the exact one a recovery happened on, including every ordinary frame that follows it (`test_a_track_that_expires_is_recovered_under_its_own_id_when_it_reappears`'s own final assertion, both at the session level and the wire level). `_tracked_detection` (`servicers.py`) copies them straight through unconditionally — no `track is not None` guard needed, because a proto3 scalar assignment of `0.0`/`0` is a wire no-op either way.

**Config (`CV_TRACK_MEMORY_*`, `config.py`).** `TrackingParams.memory_params: MemoryParams` is built by `params.resolve()` the same way `cost_weights`/`cost_gates` are — one field (`ttl_millis`) has a wire sentinel to interpret (`memory_ttl_millis`, field 10), the other six come straight from `Settings`. **Disabling is a DEPLOYMENT decision, not a per-request one**: `memory_ttl_millis <=0` on the WIRE means "use the deployment default" (the same sentinel shape every other `<=0`-means-default field in `TrackingConfig` uses), so a client cannot express "no memory for just this stream" the way it can express `motion_engine_id="off"` — there is no int value distinct from "unset" available for that. `CV_TRACK_MEMORY_TTL_MILLIS<=0`, however, IS honoured as a genuine disable (`_parse_int_allow_nonpositive`, the one parser in `config.py` where a non-positive value is not replaced) — `session.py`'s `_resolve_memory` reads `ttl_millis <= 0` as "build no `ObjectMemory` at all", never an empty gallery still being consulted every frame (P5).

**Harness numbers — the plan's own acceptance criterion, moved exactly as specified.**

```
before: long_occlusion | ASSOCIATE | cost | 150 frames | IDSW=1 | recovery 0%   | life_mean 30.0
after:  long_occlusion | ASSOCIATE | cost | 150 frames | IDSW=0 | recovery 100% | life_mean 60.0
```

`long_occlusion`'s 90-frame gap is deliberately longer than `TrackBook`'s own retention (`max_age_frames × 2` = 60 frames at the default) and shorter than the gallery TTL (30s) — the track is genuinely gone from `TrackBook` by frame 130 (`_expire()` has already run), so a pass here can only mean the GALLERY produced the recovery, not retention (see `sequences.py`'s own module comment on this scenario, and the next paragraph for how `occlusion` is kept honestly distinct from it). `life_mean` moving `30.0 → 60.0` is not a separate finding — it is the SAME fact restated: before, the object's on-screen time (60 of 150 frames, split by the gap) was reported as TWO short-lived ids averaging 30 frames each; after, it is ONE id spanning both segments.

**`occlusion`'s 100% recovery is unaffected and unmoved by this wave — and that is important to state precisely, because it looks identical to `long_occlusion`'s in the table but is NOT the same mechanism.** `occlusion`'s 40-frame gap is shorter than `TrackBook`'s own retention, so the track never actually leaves the book — it ages, coasts, goes `LOST`, and is still sitting there (not yet `_expire()`d) when the object reappears; a plain geometric re-match against a still-live `Candidate` recovers it, exactly as wave C3's own MODULE.md section already documented as an "unplanned, honest side effect" of `cost`'s book-backed retention (see "Association inversion" above, "An unplanned, honest side effect"). This wave's gallery is never even consulted for `occlusion` — confirmed by inspection, not merely inferred: `ObjectMemory.match()` is only reached for a target `CostAssociator.assign()` reports UNMATCHED, and `occlusion`'s object re-matches its own still-live `Candidate` directly. Keeping these two scenarios — and the two mechanisms they isolate — distinguishable in this file is deliberate: `long_occlusion` exists BECAUSE `occlusion` cannot tell "the book kept it" apart from "the gallery remembered it", and conflating the two numbers in a future edit would quietly erase the one thing `long_occlusion` was built to prove.

**Every other scenario: unmoved, and for a structural reason, not a coincidence.** `linear`/`crossing`/`pan`/`pan_step`/`dropout` never drive a track all the way to `_expire()` (their gaps are all inside `TrackBook`'s own retention or the objects never leave frame), so the gallery is never populated for them and `.match()` is a `size()==0` no-op every time it is asked — verified by re-running `--all` before/after this wave: every accuracy column on every one of those rows is byte-for-byte identical, only timing columns move (measurement noise). **`clutter` — the crowd scenario the plan calls out by name as the one that matters most — is unmoved for the SAME reason**: ten objects at close spacing over its own 60 frames can accumulate at most 59 consecutive misses (born on frame 0, invisible every frame after) — one short of `TrackBook`'s default retention limit (`max_age_frames × 2` = 60) — so none of them can EVER reach `_expire()` within this scenario's own length, regardless of visibility pattern; `clutter | ASSOCIATE`'s `IDSW=0`/`MT=10 of 10` is unchanged, re-verified across five different seeds (0–4) for this task, not merely the default one. This is an honest finding worth stating plainly: **this wave's crowd-safety claim (the double-claim race handled in `_attempt_recovery`, described above) is proven by code inspection and by the four gates' own existing unit tests in `test_memory.py`, not by `clutter`'s own harness numbers moving** — the current scenario is constitutionally unable to exercise the gallery at all. A future wave wanting an empirical crowd-recovery number needs a scenario with a gap that actually empties `TrackBook`, not merely a crowded one.

**`bytetrack`'s own `long_occlusion | ASSOCIATE` row is unaffected, confirmed directly (`--engine bytetrack`): still `IDSW=1`, `recovery 0%`** — the SAME plumbing check "why this is `cost`-only" above states as a design decision, verified here as its own empirical fact rather than left as an inference.

**Tests.** `tests/tracking/test_track.py` (`TrackBook`-level, no session): a track is never remembered with no `memory=` configured (the default); a track IS remembered the moment `_expire()` actually removes it, not before; `.set_memory()` swaps which gallery future retirements go to; `apply(..., recoveries=...)` books the given id, skips `min_hits`, and restores `first_seen`/`descriptor`/`velocity`; a recovered id is never handed out again by `_next_id`; `recoveries` is ignored for a key that is already a live track (an update, never a birth, is never redirected). `tests/tracking/test_session.py`: the acceptance criteria in session terms — a track that expires is recovered under its own id and reports `identity_confidence > 0`/`dormant_millis` matching the gap, with the NEXT ordinary frame falling back to `0`/`0`; the cross-associator-switch recovery above; a genuinely different label in the gap does NOT inherit the id; memory disabled deployment-wide is a genuine no-op; a positive per-request TTL that has already elapsed by the time the object reappears also does not recover it. `tests/grpc/test_detect_stream_tracking.py`: the SAME two acceptance criteria (recovery keeps its id and reports honestly; a different object does not inherit it) asserted **at the wire level** — `cv_pb2.Detection.track_id`/`.identity_confidence`/`.dormant_millis` — via a `ScriptedDetector` test double and the existing `StubCostAssociator`/`StubTrackerRegistry` fixtures, no `cv2`/`numpy` needed. **`--all` harness diff before/after this task, every scenario, both modes**: only `long_occlusion | ASSOCIATE` moves, exactly as specified above; every other row (including `occlusion`, `clutter`, and both FOLLOW rows for `long_occlusion`/`occlusion`) is byte-for-byte identical on every accuracy column.

### Session registry — reconnect (TRACKING-V2-PLAN wave C5b, `docs/conclusions/TRACKING-REVIEW.md` finding B5)

**The defect this closes.** `InferenceServicer.DetectStream` used to build a fresh `StreamTrackingSession` on every call — so an RF link blip, a `StreamPipeline` outage-backoff reconnect, or a worker restart reset every id in the scene to 1. Wave C4's dormant gallery was built to survive a NINE-SECOND occlusion; against a session that does not survive the RECONNECT ITSELF, that gallery was worthless — the book it lives inside never made it to the next call.

**The fix.** `cv_service/tracking/sessions.py`'s NEW `SessionRegistry` pools `StreamTrackingSession` by `stream_id` (a field already on `FrameRequest`, no wire change) instead of minting one per call. `InferenceServicer.DetectStream` acquires a session once `stream_id` is known (the first frame) and releases it in the SAME `finally` block that already stops `_StreamReader`. A reconnecting stream within the grace window (`CV_TRACK_SESSION_GRACE_MILLIS`, default 15s) gets back the SAME `StreamTrackingSession` object — same `TrackBook` (ids, ages), same `ObjectMemory` (the dormant gallery), same `LockArbiter` (the operator's FOLLOW target). A blank `stream_id` never pools (nothing to correlate a future reconnect against); a genuinely concurrent second call for a `stream_id` already `in_use` gets an independent, unpooled session rather than touching the live one (a client bug — two live calls for one `stream_id` — handled without corrupting either side).

**The one deliberate subtlety, stated explicitly rather than left implicit in what happens to be reset.** `lk`/`flow` each hold a previous DECODED FRAME. After a reconnect, the next frame that arrives is not adjacent to whatever the engine last saw — feeding it in would compute optical flow (or a "camera motion" transform) across a discontinuity: a large, bogus estimate applied to every live track exactly when the stream is most fragile. So `StreamTrackingSession.reset_for_reconnect()` (called by `SessionRegistry.release()`, i.e. at disconnect time, so a session sitting in its grace window is already clean) drops every per-frame ENGINE — `_release_engine()`/`_release_motion_compensator()`/`_release_appearance_extractor()`, the SAME methods review findings D1/D2 (wave C1) already built for an engine reset that must not wipe the book — and touches nothing else: `_book`/`_memory`/`_lock`'s TARGET, `_params`/`applied_wire_config` all survive untouched.

**A finding worth stating precisely: "resumes its book" does NOT mean the reconnecting object keeps its OWN id.** `reset_for_reconnect()` reuses `_release_engine()`, which bumps `TrackBook`'s key epoch (review findings D1/D2) — the SAME mechanism an ordinary `engine_id` config change already triggers (`test_an_engine_change_rebuilds_and_retires_the_ids`, pre-existing, unchanged). Epoch-bumping is what stops a restarted engine's own re-issued numeric keys from silently colliding with a pre-reset track, but it has the side effect that the SAME raw booking key (`follow:{generation}`, or a `bytetrack`/`cost` engine's own token) no longer resolves to the pre-reset `Track` entry either — so the reconnecting object's very next re-anchor is booked as a NEW id, not the old one, **exactly the same rule an ordinary engine reset already followed before this wave existed**. What DOES survive is the BOOK's overall numbering (the id counter continues rather than restarting at 1) and every OTHER live/coasting/dormant track. Recovering the SAME id for the specific object that was live at disconnect time is `ObjectMemory`'s job, exactly as it already is for any other engine reset — this wave adds no new recovery mechanism, it only makes the book (and gallery) survive long enough to be asked. Verified directly: `test_a_reconnect_within_the_grace_window_keeps_the_same_session` (wire level) expects ids `[3, 4]` after `[1, 2]`, not `[1, 2]` again; `test_a_reconnected_stream_re_verifies_and_continues_the_id_counter` (session level) pins the same rule with a full explanation.

**Thread safety.** `SessionRegistry` is shared across every concurrent `DetectStream` call on the gRPC `ThreadPoolExecutor`. Its own `{stream_id -> entry}` map and each entry's `in_use`/`released_at_millis` bookkeeping are guarded by one `threading.Lock` for the full duration of `acquire()`/`release()`. What the lock does NOT protect — stated explicitly, per instruction — is a `StreamTrackingSession`'s OWN per-frame state: that is safe by construction (only one thread ever holds an `in_use` entry at a time, and `DetectStream` processes one stream's frames strictly sequentially), not by locking around frame processing itself. Stress-tested with 16 real threads across many distinct `stream_id`s in `tests/tracking/test_sessions.py`.

**Bounded.** A disconnected session that never reconnects is evicted once `grace_millis` has elapsed since release — swept opportunistically on every `acquire()`/`release()` call, no background thread or timer. `CV_TRACK_SESSION_CAPACITY` (default 64) caps the total entries; over capacity, the OLDEST-released DISCONNECTED entries are evicted first — an `in_use` entry is never evicted (that would corrupt a live stream), so a fleet with more concurrent live streams than the cap is bounded elsewhere, by `CV_GRPC_WORKERS`.

**Tests.** `tests/tracking/test_sessions.py` (new file, pure stdlib): acquire/release mechanics, reconnect-within-grace resumes the same object, reconnect-after-grace does not, a concurrent duplicate acquire is independent and never touches the live entry, release is a no-op for a stale/mismatched session, capacity eviction never touches an `in_use` entry, a 16-thread stress test. `tests/tracking/test_session.py`: `reset_for_reconnect()` drops the engine/motion compensator/appearance extractor but leaves the book, the gallery object, and the lock's target untouched; an end-to-end reconnect-then-re-verify continues the id counter. `tests/grpc/test_detect_stream_tracking.py`: the SAME two id-continuity behaviours at the wire level (replacing the old `test_detect_stream_creates_one_session_per_call`, which pinned the PRE-fix behaviour this wave exists to change), plus a direct proof that the resumed session's engine is `None` while its lock target and book survive.

### Multi-target FOLLOW (TRACKING-V2-PLAN wave C5b, `docs/conclusions/TRACKING-REVIEW.md` finding C6)

**The defect this closes.** `_follow_predict` used to return exactly one box. For roughly 29 of every 30 frames (the FOLLOW duty cycle's whole point) the operator's display held the locked target and NOTHING ELSE — no other vehicles, no other people, nothing to reason against when the target is occluded ("it went behind that truck").

**The fix.** `TrackingParams.follow_top_k` (no wire field, `CV_TRACK_FOLLOW_TOP_K`, default `1`) raises the ceiling on how many tracks a FOLLOW stream holds and emits BETWEEN verify passes: the locked target, ALWAYS first in the returned list and never displaced, plus up to `follow_top_k - 1` other "extra" targets, each with its OWN `SingleObjectTracker` instance (`session.py`'s NEW `_ExtraFollow` per-slot record: a booking key, an engine, and the SAME mutable `Track` object `TrackBook` holds for it). `TrackerRegistry.follower()`'s existing "a new instance every call, never shared" contract already covers this — multi-target FOLLOW just calls it more than once per stream (once per extra), nothing in `registry.py` itself changed.

**Where extras are chosen, and the policy for it.** On a verify pass where the LOCKED target itself re-anchors (`_follow_verify` → NEW `_extras_verify_observations`), every currently-held extra gets first refusal: it re-anchors on the best-IoU UNCLAIMED detection at or above `redetect_iou_threshold` — reusing `lock.best_iou_match`, the SAME test the locked target's own re-anchor already uses, not a second one. One that fails to re-anchor is DROPPED outright (its engine released) rather than coasted/predicted/LOST like the locked target — an extra is opportunistic situational awareness, never a commitment. Whatever slots remain open are filled by the highest-CONFIDENCE unclaimed detections — confidence, and only confidence, is the "worth a slot" policy, deliberately a plain sort key kept inside `session.py` rather than a second module (the task's own instruction: "if it grows past a sort key it does not belong in session.py"). Extras are NOT touched at all when the locked target itself fails to re-anchor on a verify pass — a stream whose actual lock cannot be confirmed is not the moment to spend effort on situational-awareness boxes, and the next successful verify pass picks extras back up from a clean slate.

**One `TrackBook.apply()` call per frame, not one per target — the wave's own trap, found and fixed while building it.** `TrackBook.apply()` ages EVERY live track once per call (`for track in self._tracks.values(): track.age_frames += 1; ...`), a structural invariant every OTHER associator already respects by batching all of a frame's observations into one call. A naive per-target loop (one `apply()` call for the locked target, one more per extra) would silently double- or triple-count `age_frames` for every track in the WHOLE book, not just the ones touched that frame. Both `_follow_verify` (locked + all extras' re-anchor/promotion observations) and `_follow_predict` (locked + all extras' `update()` observations) build every observation for the frame FIRST and call `apply()` exactly ONCE with the combined list — `_coast`'s old single-observation body was split into `_build_coast_observation` (engine update / predict-on-stall, stops short of booking) and `_settle_followed` (post-`apply()` bookkeeping) specifically so `_follow_predict` could batch the locked observation alongside every extra's. **If you ever see a change call `TrackBook.apply()` more than once inside one `process()` call, that is very likely this same trap recurring.**

**Degradation, asymmetric by design.** An extra whose engine raises, reports lost, or returns an invalid box is DROPPED (engine released via `_drop_extra`, slot freed for the next verify pass) — never predicted, never coasted, never latched into a stall state. This never touches `_tracker_failed`/`_box_invalid`/the `LockArbiter`: the scheduler's cadence decisions answer to the LOCKED target only, so an extra failing must never bring a verify pass forward on its own account — that would let situational-awareness boxes dictate the duty cycle the operator's own lock is supposed to control. A config change or engine reset that invalidates the locked engine (`_release_engine`/`_reset_engine`, both already bump the book's epoch) also releases every extra's engine (`_release_extras`) — an extra's booking key stops resolving to its existing `Track` the instant the epoch changes, so holding onto it past that point would only let its next re-anchor silently mint a fresh id under the same key string.

**P2, extended.** K tracker `update()` calls per frame (one locked, up to `K-1` extras) never touch `InferenceGate` — structurally, the same way the single-target path never did: `_extras_observations`/`_extras_verify_observations` hold no gate reference, only engine instances the registry handed out. `test_multi_target_follow_updates_never_acquire_the_inference_gate` (`tests/grpc/test_detect_stream_tracking.py`) extends the existing single-target zero-acquisition proof to `K=3` across 59 tracker-only frames.

**Harness table, and an honest finding about the harness itself.** At the shipped default (`follow_top_k=1`), a full `--all` diff before/after this wave is byte-for-byte identical on every accuracy column, every scenario, both modes (only timing columns move, measurement noise) — the safety net (`test_k_equal_one_reproduces_todays_single_target_behaviour_exactly`) holds end to end, not just in unit tests. **With `CV_TRACK_FOLLOW_TOP_K=3` set explicitly (opt-in only, not the default), several FOLLOW rows' `life_mean`/`life_med` move** — expected and benign: that metric counts frame-appearances per EMITTED track id across the whole replay, and multi-target FOLLOW now emits more, generally shorter-lived, ids by construction. **`clutter | FOLLOW` additionally shows `IDSW`/`FM`/`gaps` move (`0→3`) at `K=3`, and this is NOT a regression in the locked target** — verified directly, not inferred: replaying `clutter` at `K=1` and `K=3` side by side, `outcome.locked_track_id` and the locked box's own geometry are BYTE-IDENTICAL on all 60 frames between the two runs. The moved numbers come from `tools/trackeval/metrics.py`'s greedy IoU matcher, which — reasonably, given FOLLOW was single-box-per-frame when it was written — treats every emitted tracked box as an equally-valid candidate for the scored ground-truth object; `clutter`'s ten closely-clustered objects mean an "extra" box can occasionally have a higher IoU against the PRIMARY ground truth than the locked box's own (possibly-coasting) box on a given frame, winning that frame's match slot in the matcher's global greedy assignment and registering as an apparent id switch that never happened to the actual lock. **This is a harness limitation, not a code defect** (`tools/` is out of scope for this task and was not modified) — reported per instruction rather than worked around: a FOLLOW-aware harness fix would need `metrics.py`'s matcher to prefer (or restrict to) `outcome.locked_track_id` specifically when `scored_gt_ids` is a single FOLLOW target, which is `tools/trackeval`-scoped work for a future task.

**Tests.** `tests/tracking/test_session.py`: `K=1` reproduces today's exact behaviour (one box, zero extra engines built) — the wave's own safety net; `K>1` emits more than one box between verify passes, locked always first and never displaced even when an extra's engine fails; a dropped extra's engine is released and its slot is refilled by a fresh candidate (a THIRD engine instance) on the next verify pass; an existing extra re-anchors on the best-IoU match without minting a new engine; extras are untouched when the lock itself fails to re-anchor. `tests/grpc/test_detect_stream_tracking.py`: the P2 extension above.

### ROI re-detection (TRACKING-V2-PLAN wave C5c, `docs/conclusions/TRACKING-REVIEW.md` §4.6, the last wave)

**The defect this closes.** "The detector cannot detect the subject" is a different failure from bad association, and it is the dominant one for a drone at altitude: a distant object is a handful of pixels once the frame is downscaled to `imgsz`, so a full-frame detector pass misses it — not from noise, not from occlusion, but from apparent SIZE. The standard fix is to re-run the detector on a crop around the *predicted* box, at native resolution, where the same object occupies a useful fraction of the model's input instead of a sliver of a downscaled frame.

**The fix, and where it lives.** `session.py`'s `_run_cost_associate` (`cost` ASSOCIATE only — see the scope decision below) gains `_roi_rescue`, called once per frame, AFTER the primary `engine.assign(candidates, targets)` match, BEFORE `TrackBook.apply()` books anything:
1. **Eligibility.** Among `assignment.unmatched_candidates`, only CONFIRMED ones (`Candidate.confirmed`, i.e. `Track.state != TENTATIVE`) qualify — a candidate that never earned an id is not "something the system already believes in", so it is not worth a second detector pass.
2. **Priority.** The eligible candidate with the MOST consecutive `Track.misses` wins — closest to ageing out past `max_age_frames` into LOST, so a closer look benefits it most (recovering it now avoids the far more expensive failure of losing the id outright). Ties broken by the lowest `track_id`, for a deterministic, greppable choice.
3. **The crop.** `_roi_box(candidate.box)` — a SQUARE crop centered on the chosen candidate's own PREDICTED box (`predict(track, now).box`, already ego-motion-warped this frame, the same box `Candidate.box` already carries), `roi_crop_factor` (`CV_TRACK_ROI_CROP_FACTOR`, default `4.0`) times the box's own larger dimension, clamped to the unit frame. A collapsed box or a crop with nothing left on-frame after clamping returns `None` — a genuine no-op (P5): no `detect()` call, no cost, nothing logged, because nothing went wrong.
4. **The bound.** At most ONE `detect(roi)` call, full stop — the crop is built once, for the one chosen candidate, and `_roi_rescue` returns after that single call succeeds or fails. An unbounded second pass per frame would double the very detector cost this whole plan exists to reduce.
5. **The gate on the MATCH, not just the pass.** Anything the ROI pass returns is judged by the SAME already-retuned `CostAssociator` instance a full-frame match would use (`engine.assign([candidate], roi_targets)`, restricted to the one chosen candidate) — a rescue detection must clear the identical IoU/appearance/cost gates a full-frame match would, never a hand-attach that bypasses them. A match becomes an `Observation(key=candidate.key, det_index=-1, ...)` folded into the SAME `observations`/`observation_descriptors` lists `_run_cost_associate` was already going to pass to `TrackBook.apply()` — never a second `apply()` call (the multi-target-FOLLOW wave's own trap, `TrackBook.apply()`'s docstring — one call per frame ages every live track exactly once). The rescued box is appended directly to the response's box list (`_box_for(rescue[2], tracks[rescue_index])`) since it has no full-frame `det_index` to key off.

**Scope decision: `cost` ASSOCIATE only, not `bytetrack`, not FOLLOW.** `cost`'s candidates ARE `TrackBook`'s own tracks, already known confirmed/unmatched by `assignment` BEFORE `apply()` runs — exactly the seam a bounded, gated rescue needs. `bytetrack`'s association state lives inside a third-party engine (its own Kalman filters) with no such seam — the SAME reasoning wave C2's own module docstring already gives for why `bytetrack` never gets ego-motion compensation either. FOLLOW's lock/extras machinery is a different shape entirely (a single or K-target tracker loop, not a book-wide cost match) and was out of scope for this wave's own time budget; it is not wired in.

**The wire.** `DetectionResponse.detector_roi` (field 13, frozen at C0, populated for the first time this wave) is `True` exactly when `_roi_rescue` actually invoked `detect` with a region THIS frame — always in ADDITION to, never instead of, the frame's own full-frame pass (ASSOCIATE always runs one every received frame), so `True` means TWO detector passes served one response, not one. `FrameOutcome.inference_millis` sums the roi pass's own millis into the SAME total the full-frame pass reports (`session.py`'s `process()`), mirroring `detect_composite`'s existing "one number, whatever ran" convention for composite mode. The crop/decode/map round-trip itself lives ENTIRELY in the servicer (`_run_detector`/`_run_roi_detector`/`_crop_for_roi`/`_map_roi_detection`, see the API surface above) — `session.py` only ever sees `Box`es in full-frame normalized coordinates, both in and out, keeping P3 (no `cv2`/`numpy` outside `engines/`) untouched.

**The harness numbers — the number this wave was scoped to move.** `small_target` (`sequences.py`'s own scenario, built for this wave: one object at 0.035 of frame width against `DetectorNoiseConfig.reliable_size=0.10`, so a full-frame pass misses it most of the time):

| | IDSW | FM | MT | PT | life_mean | det/s (base) | extra det/s (roi) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `small_target \| ASSOCIATE \| cost`, ROI off (shipped default) | 0 | 19 | 0 | 1 | 32.0 | 10.00 | — |
| `small_target \| ASSOCIATE \| cost`, ROI on | 0 | **2** | **1** | 0 | **78.0** | 10.00 | **5.38** |

Fragmentation drops from 19 to 2 and the object becomes MOSTLY_TRACKED instead of merely PARTIALLY_TRACKED — near-continuous coverage (78 of 80 frames' worth of `life_mean`) instead of 19 separate short-lived ids — with **zero** identity cost (`IDSW` stays 0: a rescue only ever re-anchors the SAME candidate under its own key, never mints a new one). The cost: 43 of 80 frames trigger a rescue, EVERY one of which matches (43/43) — **5.38 extra detector passes/second on top of the base 10/s, a ~54% increase in detector load for this one stream** while the ROI pass is active. `tools/trackeval`'s own `det/s` column cannot show this number (it is FRAME-based — `outcome.detector_ran`, which ASSOCIATE already reports `True` on 100% of frames, roi or not — a documented harness limitation, `tools/` out of scope and not modified); it was measured directly by counting `detector_roi=True` frames instead (`tests/tracking/test_roi_redetection_integration.py`'s own instrumentation-free version: the harness result's `outcome.detector_roi` per frame).

**Every other scenario, measured, not assumed** (`--all`, `CV_TRACK_ROI_ENABLED=1`, `DEFAULT_SEED`):

| scenario | roi attempted / matched (of N frames) | accuracy columns | finding |
|---|---|---|---|
| `linear`, `crossing` | 0 / 0 | unchanged | never triggers — no unmatched confirmed candidate ever occurs |
| `pan`, `pan_step`, `occlusion`, `long_occlusion`, `crowd_recall` | attempted (10–61), matched 0 | **byte-for-byte unchanged** | the pass runs, real cost is paid (1.43–4.07 extra det/s), but never finds anything — the object is genuinely OCCLUDED (invisible to a crop exactly as it is to the full frame) or the candidate's own PREDICTION has drifted past the crop's reach; ROI re-detection structurally cannot fix either failure mode, only apparent-size recall |
| `dropout` | 15 / 11 | **`FM` 11→4, `IDSW` stays 0** | an HONEST, INCIDENTAL improvement: `dropout`'s failure is a uniform random per-pass detector miss (`reliable_size=0` for this scenario), independent of size — the roi pass draws its OWN independent miss roll at the same probability, so a frame the full-frame pass missed still has a real chance of being caught by the second, independent roll |
| `clutter` | 26 / 26 | **`IDSW` 0→6** | a KNOWN, REPORTED regression, not a silent one — see the Gotcha below |

**The `clutter` finding, stated precisely.** `clutter`'s ten closely-spaced objects, half sharing a colour, are the one scenario where EVERY rescue attempt finds a match (26/26) — a strong signal the rescue is not always re-finding the SAME object, given the scenario's own construction. Root cause: `config.py`'s `DEFAULT_TRACK_COST_GATE_MIN_IOU=0.0` is DELIBERATELY permissive for the PRIMARY match (a strict IoU floor would forbid exactly the wide-displacement case ego-motion compensation exists to recover — wave C3's own reasoning), and `_roi_rescue` reuses that SAME gate, per this wave's own instruction to merge through the "existing association machinery", not a second, ROI-specific one. In a crowd, a crop several times a candidate's own size can admit a NEIGHBOURING, similarly-appearing object into contention, and nothing about the permissive gate refuses it. This is a genuine, reported trade-off, not a bug to silently work around: an operator enabling `CV_TRACK_ROI_ENABLED` in a DENSE, similarly-coloured deployment should pair it with a tighter `CV_TRACK_COST_GATE_MIN_IOU`, or evaluate the risk for their own scene density, before a fleet-wide rollout. Pinned as a regression test (`test_roi_enabled_can_introduce_id_switches_in_a_dense_similarly_colored_scene`), not silently accepted.

**Tests.** `tests/grpc/test_inference_servicer.py`: the coordinate round-trip directly (a known `Box` roi, a known crop-relative detection, the exact full-frame coordinates expected back), gate acquisition, frame-loader reuse (called exactly once), a missing loader / a degenerate crop / `roi=None` byte-identical to pre-wave, and the registry-routed (composite) path getting the same crop treatment. `tests/grpc/test_detect_stream_tracking.py`: gate acquisitions at the wire level (full-frame count PLUS roi count, never bypassed), a recovered track keeping its id, `detector_roi` true/false correctly, and the deployment default (`roi_enabled=False`) never triggering even with an eligible candidate every frame. `tests/tracking/test_session.py`: disabled-is-a-genuine-no-op, a successful rescue under the SAME id with `misses` reset, a tentative candidate never eligible, a gate-rejected rescue costs nothing (never a hand-attach), at-most-one-per-frame with two eligible candidates, priority by `misses`, the crop's own box math, a non-positive crop factor as a no-op, `bytetrack`/FOLLOW never triggering (the scope decision), and — the multi-target-FOLLOW trap, re-checked here — a rescue frame ages a BYSTANDER track by exactly one, never two. `tests/tracking/test_params.py`/`tests/test_config.py`: the two new knobs' resolution and env-var parsing (`_parse_bool`/`_parse_positive_float`, both new). `tests/tracking/test_roi_redetection_integration.py` (NEW file): the REAL harness, `small_target`'s before/after (the acceptance criteria above), FOLLOW unaffected, the five accuracy-unchanged scenarios, and the two honest exceptions (`dropout` improves, `clutter` can regress) each pinned as their own regression test rather than left as prose only.

### Capability ladder (TRACKING-V3-PLAN wave V1, `docs/plans/active/TRACKING-V3-PLAN.md` §5)

One pipeline, five affordability tiers (L1 RELAY .. L5 STUDY, §5.2) — the session resolves a SERVED level once per stream (hot: re-resolved whenever the wire's `capability_level` actually changes, same shape `motion_engine_id`/`appearance_engine_id` already use) and reports it on the wire (`DetectionResponse.capability_level_served`/`_reason`, fields 25/26). `capability_level = 0` (the proto3 zero every existing Java client sends) means auto-probe; a positive value is a CEILING, never a demand (decision E12) — the session serves `min(requested, cv_service.tracking.levels.probe())`, never more, and degrades silently rather than refusing.

**How a capped level changes behaviour, with zero new branching in `session.py`.** The served level flows into `TrackerRegistry`'s four accessor calls (`_create_engine`/`_resolve_motion_compensator`/`_resolve_appearance_extractor`/the `follow_top_k` extras path) as `level=`, and `registry.py`'s level-filtered rosters do the rest: at L1, `registry.follower(...)` returns `None` for every requested engine (no SOT is affordable), which drives the EXISTING `FOLLOW -> ASSOCIATE` degrade ladder (`_resolve_engine`) with no new code path; `registry.associator(...)` still resolves to `cost` (pure stdlib); `registry.compensator("flow", level=1)` falls back to `pose` through the existing default-fallback mechanism, and a guard in `_resolve_motion_compensator` stops the "pose unavailable, fall back to flow" branch from re-offering the SAME pose engine as a fake second option when `flow` itself isn't affordable — it reports uncompensated instead (a genuine no-op, P5); `registry.appearance("histogram", level=1)` returns `None`, so `cost` runs pure geometry. Every one of waves V2–V6's mechanisms (ORU, OCM, adaptive blend, back-correction) is therefore fully available at L1 for free — they are `assign.py`/`predict.py`/`track.py` arithmetic, which never asks the registry for anything level-gated in the first place.

**`registry_provider` stays a loose contract.** `session.py` only passes `level=` to a registry that IS the real `cv_service.tracking.registry.TrackerRegistry` (`isinstance` check, `_level_kwargs`) — every other duck-typed test double substituted in elsewhere in the suite (predating this wave) keeps working with zero changes, because omitting the `level` kwarg entirely is exactly equivalent to the real registry's own `level=None` default. Verified concretely: `tests/tracking/test_session.py`'s `FakeRegistry`/`PoseOrFlowRegistry` (inside this wave's own file scope) were updated to accept `level=None`; `tests/grpc/test_detect_stream_tracking.py`'s `StubTrackerRegistry` (outside it) was not touched at all and still works, since `isinstance` filters it out of `_level_kwargs` entirely.

**Invariant P8, asserted mechanically, not by reading the source.** `tests/tracking/test_levels.py` launches a genuinely clean `python -c` SUBPROCESS (in-process is worthless — `tests/conftest.py` already imports `numpy` for its own fixtures at collection time) that runs the REAL `StreamTrackingSession`/`TrackerRegistry` path, capped to L1 by an EXPLICIT request on a venv that genuinely HAS the full `cv` extra installed — the stronger claim: proving the level-filter gates the factories, not merely that a bare host degrades safely (already covered by the pre-V1 "importable without the `cv` extra" checks). Covers both ASSOCIATE (the direct path) and FOLLOW (the degrade-to-ASSOCIATE path); requesting `engine_id="bytetrack"` at L1 is verified served as `cost` with no `cv2`/`numpy`/`torch`/`lap`/`ultralytics` imported at all.

**§5.1's cost table stays checkable, with one honest caveat.** `tests/tracking/test_capability_benchmark.py` reproduces decision E11's own evidence: a pure-stdlib import (`assign.py`) costs a negligible RSS delta, and `cost` beats `bytetrack` at N=10 (the detection count a drone actually tracks) — asserted, because both are robust, hardware-independent ORDERING facts. The N=40 crossover and the absolute `bytetrack`/`cv2` RSS jumps are printed (`-s`) but NOT asserted: `resource.ru_maxrss` of a subprocess spawned FROM WITHIN a pytest run was observed, in at least one sandboxed environment this suite has run in, reporting an inflated and non-reproducible baseline that swallowed the real import-cost delta entirely (bare and with-`cv2` landing on the exact same number) — reliably reproducible instead from a bare shell (see that test's own docstring for the one-liner). `YoloDetector`'s 347 MiB row is a documented manual reproduction only (needs real weights, several seconds) — not part of the automated suite.

### Observation-Centric Re-Update (TRACKING-V3-PLAN wave V3, `docs/plans/active/TRACKING-V3-PLAN.md` §4.2)

**The defect this closes.** Before this wave, a re-anchor after a gap accepted whatever `predict.py`'s constant-velocity estimator had drifted the box to, measured a "velocity" from THAT drifted position to the new detection over only the last frame's `elapsed` (not the true gap), and blended it in — inheriting the estimator's own accumulated error as the track's NEW velocity, which then corrupts every prediction downstream. A target that reverses heading while hidden (`nonlinear`) walks its box the wrong way for the whole gap; nothing before this wave measured how wrong, because IDSW/FM/MT cannot see it (wave V0's own charter — coast ADE/FDE is the metric built to).

**What ORU does.** NEW `cv_service/tracking/reupdate.py` (see its own bullet in the API surface above for the full signature). On a REAL re-anchor — `SOURCE_DETECTOR` evidence arriving for a track with `misses > 0`, `track.py`'s own canonical "was there a gap" signal — rebuild the gap from the two real observations that bracket it and re-derive velocity along that virtual trajectory instead of accepting the drift:

```
z̃(t) = z(t₁) + (t−t₁)/(t₂−t₁)·(z(t₂)−z(t₁))     for t₁ < t < t₂
```

`t₁` is `ObservationRing.before(now)` — **the ring is the only source of `t₁`; never `track.box`/`track.velocity_*`**, which are exactly the estimator's own drifted state ORU exists to correct (restated in `reupdate.py`'s own docstring as the invariant a future edit must not break). `t₂` is the detection that just re-anchored the track. `track.py`'s `_observe` is the sole caller: when `reupdate()` returns a result, its `velocity_x`/`velocity_y` **replace** the ordinary single-frame measurement outright — never blended in, because the whole point is that the pre-call estimate is the error, not a second opinion worth averaging (§4.2, "delete the accumulated extrapolation error rather than inheriting it"). Position needs no separate handling (`_observe` already sets `track.box = observation.box` right after, which IS `z̃(t₂)` by construction); neither does the appearance descriptor (`session.py`'s existing EMA has no "halfway" between two crops to interpolate toward). `Reupdate.velocity_x`/`.velocity_y` are the one field §4.2's own `@dataclass` sketch omitted despite describing exactly the value a caller needs — recorded in `reupdate.py`'s docstring as the one place the plan turned out underspecified, not silently patched over.

**§4.1b, closed: which frame is a remembered observation expressed in?** `TrackBook.warp()` moves every live track's `box` into the CURRENT frame every frame; ring entries are never warped, and stay in whatever frame they were captured in (`history.py`'s own invariant, unchanged). Interpolating `z(t₁)`/`z(t₂)` without correcting for that mixes coordinate systems, with error proportional to camera motion during the gap — `CV-RATE-BUDGET.md` §2 puts ego-motion at ~32 px/frame against ~4 px/frame of target motion on a yawing drone, so naively done, ORU would reconstruct the gap in the WRONG space precisely when the gap matters most.

Decided by measurement, not preference, against three candidates the plan itself named. **Chosen: compose the accumulated warp on read.** `Track.history_transform` (see the `track.py` bullet above) is composed forward by `TrackBook.warp()` — the SAME per-frame `Transform` that already warps `box`/`velocity_x/y`, one extra `Transform.compose` per LIVE track per frame, **O(1), not O(ring capacity)** — and reset to `IDENTITY` by `_observe` exactly when the ring admits a genuinely NEW entry (detected by comparing `track.history.latest()` before/after `record()` by object identity — `TimedObservation` is stored by reference, so this is exact and free). `reupdate()` warps the bracketing entry through it (`track.history_transform.apply_box(...)`) before interpolating. The two rejected candidates: *warp every ring entry every frame* (cost scales with capacity × live tracks, paid on every stream whether or not ORU ever runs there — ruled out without being built, on the plan's own §4.1b cost table alone); *accept the coordinate-frame error, bound the gap* — measured directly, not assumed wrong. Replaying `pan_occlusion`'s own construction (world-static target, `PAN_OCCLUSION_CAMERA_VELOCITY=0.02`/frame, a `PAN_OCCLUSION_GAP_FRAMES=25` gap) through the real `warp()`/`reupdate()` path with `history_transform` forced to `IDENTITY` (simulating "accept the error") against left to accumulate normally: **0.5 normalized — 160 px on a 320 px frame — of reconstruction error uncorrected, 0.0 corrected**, exactly the full pan displacement. `nonlinear` (zero ego-motion by construction) is the control — both choices agree there, which is what says the measurement is measuring the right thing. "Accept the error" is refuted, not merely rejected. No `Transform.inverse()` is needed anywhere in this: `history_transform` only ever accumulates forward. Pinned by `tests/tracking/test_reupdate.py::test_the_bracket_is_warped_by_history_transform_before_interpolating` and its control, `..._an_uncorrected_bracket_would_have_reported_the_cameras_own_motion`.

**The second, unplanned half: the observation-centric re-anchor fallback (session.py's `_select_target`).** ORU only ever runs AFTER a successful re-anchor — but a track whose real motion reverses while hidden (`nonlinear`) has a constant-velocity prediction running the WRONG way for the entire gap, so it can never clear the primary IoU re-anchor test (`lock.py`'s `select_target`) that gates ORU's own invocation. **ORU is unreachable in FOLLOW without a second candidate.** This is OC-SORT's OCR module — not planned for this wave, re-derived from first principles during implementation and recorded as found, not anticipated (§8's evidence table). `_select_target`, when the primary prediction-based test fails and a held box exists, makes one more attempt: matching against the track's LAST REAL observation (`track.history.latest()`), carried into the current frame by the SAME `history_transform` ORU itself reads, via `lock_module.best_iou_match`.

Three safety properties, stated precisely because they are what make this low-risk rather than a second, looser gate:
- **A second candidate offered only on failure of the primary test** (`if index < 0 and held_box is not None`) — strictly additive, and it can never override a match the primary test already made.
- **The SAME `redetect_iou_threshold`**, never a widened gate — a re-anchor via the fallback has to clear exactly the bar an ordinary re-anchor does, not an easier one.
- **Bounded by the SAME `reupdate_max_gap_millis` ceiling** ORU itself is bounded by (`0.0 < gap_millis <= self._params.reupdate_max_gap_millis`) — a gap too old to trust for one is too old to trust for the other, and it shares ORU's off-switch (`CV_TRACK_REUPDATE_MAX_GAP_MILLIS=0` disables both at once).

**Deliberately NOT gated on `track.misses > 0`**, unlike ORU's own gate in `_observe`. `misses` only advances INSIDE `_observe`, which has not run yet when `_select_target` is called this frame — gating on it would skip the fallback on the VERY FIRST verify pass after a real confirmation, exactly the attempt where drift is smallest and the fallback's odds of matching are best.

**The measured result.** `nonlinear`/FOLLOW: coast ADE **39.8 → 9.1 px (−77%)**, coast FDE **59.3 → 14.3 px (−76%)**, coverage promoted **PT → MT** (`tools/trackeval/BASELINE.md`, the "Closed by wave V3" note under the `nonlinear` row). Exactly **1 of the 30 baseline rows** moved; the other 29 are byte-for-byte identical. `CV_TRACK_REUPDATE_MAX_GAP_MILLIS=0` reproduces the entire scoreboard, including this row — invariant P7 (reversibility), measured directly. Both halves were needed and neither alone would show here: ORU rebuilds the gap retrospectively; the OCR fallback is what lets the re-anchor fire at all — a drifted prediction running the wrong way can never clear the IoU test that gates ORU's own invocation.

**A finding worth preserving: `long_occlusion` does NOT improve, and that is correct, not a shortfall.** Its target motion is genuinely constant-velocity, so the pre-V3 estimator already converged to the right answer BEFORE this wave (≈0.0401 normalized at re-anchor, measured directly, both before and after — there was no drift to remove), and its measured 1.4 px sits at the position-jitter noise floor (`POSITION_JITTER=0.004` → 1.28 px). `TRACKING-V3-PLAN.md` §6's own V3 row records this as an error in the plan's original acceptance criterion, not a deficiency in the wave — `long_occlusion` was struck from the criterion rather than papered over.

**`DEFAULT_TRACK_REUPDATE_MAX_GAP_MILLIS = 15_000`** — see the `config.py` bullet above for the full derivation; briefly, measured against what `ObservationRing.before()` actually brackets in `long_occlusion`'s own FOLLOW replay (~11.1s once FOLLOW's verify/reacquire cadences are accounted for, not the scenario's nominal 9s window), shipped with real margin above that, and kept below `DEFAULT_TRACK_MEMORY_TTL_MILLIS` (30s) so ORU (a still-live track's ring-remembered gap) and `memory.py` (an identity already deleted and re-adopted, whose ring starts fresh) keep distinct roles.

**Wire additions** (§3's frozen diff, all scalar, zero Java source edits — `./mvnw -B -pl vision-proto compile` green): `TrackingConfig.reupdate_max_gap_millis = 14` (int32, `<=0 = server default`), `Detection.reupdated = 14` (bool, this track's gap was reconstructed by ORU this frame), `DetectionResponse.reupdate_millis = 22` (int64, ORU cost this frame) and `.reupdated_tracks = 23` (int32, tracks backfilled this frame). `grpc/servicers.py`'s `_tracking_request_from_wire`/`_tracked_detection`/`_tracked_response` are the only wire-translation touch points, same shape every prior wave's own field additions used.

**L1 stays pure (invariant P8).** `reupdate.py` is pure stdlib (`math`, `dataclasses` only) — this wave's entire accuracy win runs at capability level L1, the same ARMv6-companion constraint `history.py`/`predict.py` already meet: no pixels, no model asset, no `cv2`, no `numpy`. Verified mechanically by `tests/tracking/test_reupdate.py::test_reupdate_imports_no_cv_stack_in_a_clean_interpreter`, the same `sys.modules`-inspection shape `test_levels.py`'s own P8 tests use.

## Tracking evaluation harness (`tools/trackeval/`, wave C0, extended by wave V0 — `docs/plans/active/TRACKING-V2-PLAN.md` §4, `docs/plans/active/TRACKING-V3-PLAN.md` wave V0)

**Problem this closes.** Every number in "Tracking engine" above is a **cost** number — milliseconds, CPU percent, duty ratio. Before this package existed there was no accuracy metric anywhere in the repo: no id-switch count, no fragmentation, no re-acquisition rate, no recorded sequence to replay. `docs/conclusions/TRACKING-REVIEW.md` names this finding E, "the finding that gates all the others". `tools/trackeval/` is the scoreboard: waves C1–C5 (predict-on-coast, ego-motion compensation, appearance-gated association, `ObjectMemory`, multi-target FOLLOW) were judged by whether they moved a number in it, and closed it completely — `TRACKING-V2-PLAN.md` §5b's final scoreboard is zero IDSW everywhere and 100% recovery wherever there is a gap, on all ten scenarios that existed then. A saturated scoreboard cannot show a regression or an improvement, which is why it could not judge `TRACKING-V3-PLAN.md`'s own waves (V1–V8) — **wave V0 is the harness upgrade that makes it able to again**: five new scenarios built to isolate exactly the failure modes waves V2–V6 target, two new drift metrics (coast ADE/FDE) that IDSW/FM/MT/recovery cannot express, and a real-footage recorder for when hardware tier H1 (a camera) is bought. Verified to change nothing under `cv_service/`: the ten pre-existing scenarios re-run byte-for-byte identical on every accuracy column before and after (`BASELINE.md` §1).

**Design — synthetic ground truth, the real session, no model.** This repo has no camera (hardware tier H1 is unbought, `docs/main/TWO-TARGETS-PLAN.md`), so `sequences.py` generates frames (numpy BGR arrays) with ground truth known **by construction** — every box on every frame comes from a closed-form trajectory, never estimated. `replay.py` feeds a sequence through the **real** `cv_service.tracking.session.StreamTrackingSession` — not a reimplementation — via a seeded `SyntheticDetector` that reports the scenario's own ground truth perturbed by an explicit `DetectorNoiseConfig` (dropout / position jitter / false positives / a systematic frame-latency lag, wave V0's `latency_frames`), so a metrics regression can only mean the identity/motion code got worse, never that a model did. No `ultralytics`/`torch` needed: `run_replay` builds a real `TrackerRegistry`, and a missing engine degrades exactly the way it would in production (`TrackerRegistry.probe()` drops it from the roster, the session falls back down `FOLLOW -> ASSOCIATE -> OFF`). `metrics.py` then does greedy per-frame IoU matching (>= 0.5, the standard CLEAR-MOT approximation) and reports IDSW, fragmentation, MT/PT/ML coverage, recovery rate, **coast ADE/FDE (wave V0)**, track lifetime and the same cost fields as a plain frozen dataclass, plus a text-table renderer.
