Every module is **pure stdlib at module scope**, mirroring `cv_service/tracking/`'s own invariant — `numpy` is imported lazily inside `sequences.py`'s `_render_frame`, so importing the package costs nothing and building a `Sequence` needs `numpy` only when it actually renders pixels. `recording.py` (wave V0, below) needs neither `numpy` nor `cv2` for its own replay path at all.

**Run it** (from `cv-service/`, `PYTHONPATH` required so imports resolve to this checkout rather than another one, same reason the "Build/test" line above needs it in a worktree):

```bash
PYTHONPATH="$PWD" .venv/bin/python -m tools.trackeval --scenario occlusion --mode ASSOCIATE
PYTHONPATH="$PWD" .venv/bin/python -m tools.trackeval --all
```

**Fifteen scenarios** (`sequences.py`'s `SCENARIOS`) — five built for wave V0 to isolate what waves V2–V6 target, each proven to score visibly imperfect on the shipped configuration (`BASELINE.md` §2); the other ten score a saturated, perfect scoreboard (`BASELINE.md` §1):

| Scenario | Isolates | Traces to |
|---|---|---|
| `nonlinear` (V0) | target REVERSES heading the instant it is hidden by an occlusion bar — constant-velocity extrapolation points the OPPOSITE way from where it actually goes | breaks `predict.py`'s CV model. ASSOCIATE force-matches (see below), so the real signal is FOLLOW: at V0, `PT=1`, coast ADE/FDE 39.8/59.3 px. **Closed by wave V3's ORU + OCR fallback**: `MT=1`, coast ADE/FDE 9.1/14.3 px (−77%/−76%) — see "Observation-Centric Re-Update" above |
| `tiny_fast` (V0) | three ~8×6 px world-static landmarks under a fast, steady pan (~19 px/frame, ~2× `pan`'s own rate) — coverage stays perfect but WHICH id gets matched swaps | `CV-RATE-BUDGET.md` §2 — a tiny box leaves almost no IoU budget for residual ego-motion error. ASSOCIATE: `IDSW=2` (needed 3 competing landmarks — a lone one can never show this, see below); FOLLOW: `ML=1`, a total loss (`lk`'s `_MIN_TRACKED_CORNERS` floor cannot hold a box this small) |
| `pan_occlusion` (V0) | continuous ego-motion AND a genuine occlusion gap, superposed | `pan` alone and `occlusion` alone are each survivable — combined, neither correction runs during the gap, so residual per-frame compensation error compounds for the whole 25-frame window instead of one. FOLLOW: coast ADE/FDE 83.3/104.8 px, the largest drift of any scenario except `crossing_similar` |
| `latency` (V0) | the detector reports a position `latency_frames` (default 8, 800 ms @ 10 fps) old, not the current one | wave V6's target — the normal case for an offboard detector (TRACKING-V3-PLAN §5.2, "L1 RELAY"). Both modes: `ML=1` from a purely systematic bias, `IDSW=0`/`FM=0` (continuously CONFIRMED, just wrong) — `coast_n=0` too, and that is itself the finding: this defect lives in `SOURCE_DETECTOR` frames that are simply incorrect, outside coast ADE/FDE's honest scope |
| `crossing_similar` (V0) | two IDENTICALLY-coloured objects cross, both coasting through the crossing point | sharpens `crossing`: appearance (`engines/histogram.py`, wave C3) is uninformative when both objects look alike. ASSOCIATE: `IDSW=0`, `FM=2`, recovery 100% — costs continuity, not identity (see the correction below). FOLLOW: coast ADE/FDE **95.9/196.8 px**, the largest final drift of any scenario |
| `linear` | the easy case: 2-3 well-separated objects, constant velocity, always visible | the floor nothing later should regress |
| `occlusion` | one object behind a static bar for longer than `CV_TRACK_MAX_AGE` (a genuine `LOST`, not a coast) | B3 — closed by wave C4's dormant gallery |
| `crossing` | two DIFFERENT-coloured, similar-sized objects' paths intersect | B1 — closed by wave C3's appearance model |
| `pan` | the CAMERA moves; objects also move | A1/A2 — closed by wave C2's ego-motion compensation |
| `dropout` | the object is always visible; the detector is configured to intermittently miss it | §4.6 — detection recall, isolated from identity |
| `pan_step` (C2 follow-up) | camera STILL while a world-static target and distractor are visible, then a UNIFORM occluder hides both exactly as the camera starts panning | A1/A2, the FOLLOW re-anchor case `pan` cannot isolate |
| `clutter` (C3 follow-up) | ten similarly-sized objects at close spacing, half sharing a colour, converging and crossing | whether `cost`'s permissive gates mis-identify a lost track in a CROWD |
| `long_occlusion` (C4) | one object hidden LONGER than `TrackBook`'s own retention but SHORTER than the gallery TTL | B3, the "identity survives being FORGOTTEN" case retention alone cannot produce (see "Object memory" above) |
| `crowd_recall` (C4) | three same-coloured PAIRS, all hidden long enough to be deleted from `TrackBook`, all returning under continued motion | whether the dormant gallery can be fooled into a WRONG recovery, not just a missed one — `clutter` is too short to ever empty the book, so this is the crowd scenario that actually exercises it (see "Object memory" above) |
| `small_target` (C5c) | one object below the detector's `reliable_size` | recall, not identity — the acceptance scenario for ROI re-detection (see "ROI re-detection" above) |

**A structural fact worth knowing before reading any single-object ASSOCIATE row** (`nonlinear`, `pan_occlusion`, a single-landmark variant of `tiny_fast`): with one live candidate and one target, `cost`'s matcher always force-matches them — `AssignGates.min_iou`/`max_cost` default to `0.0`/`inf`, no hard geometric floor — regardless of how far the predicted box has drifted. A single-ground-truth-object ASSOCIATE scenario can therefore show a COVERAGE defect (`FM`, a gap) but structurally never an IDENTITY one (`IDSW`), which is why `nonlinear`/`pan_occlusion`'s real claim is in their FOLLOW row and why `tiny_fast` needed three competing landmarks rather than one. Full writeup: `BASELINE.md` §3.

**What each metric means** (`metrics.py`): **IDSW** is id switches summed across every ground-truth object's trajectory, counted by walking each object's matched-frame timeline and counting every place the assigned id changes — across an occlusion gap exactly as much as between two adjacent frames, deliberately, since both are the same event from an identity point of view. **FM** (fragmentations) is how many times a ground-truth object's coverage was interrupted and later resumed. **MT/PT/ML** is a coverage axis (>=80% / partial / <20% of the object's own on-screen window matched to *some* id) deliberately kept separate from identity — a track whose id never changes can still be `mostly_lost` if it stops matching the true position. **Recovery rate** — of the ground-truth objects that had an *internal* gap (bounded by a match on both sides; a leading/trailing gap is real lost coverage with nothing to "recover" toward, so it is excluded), the fraction that resumed with their **original** track id; saturated at 100% wherever a gap exists on the ten pre-existing scenarios, which is exactly why it cannot judge waves V1 onward. **Coast ADE/FDE (wave V0)** — average / final displacement error between the reported box and the TRUE ground-truth box, in normalized units and pixels at the sequence's own frame size, measured ONLY over frames where the reported box came from the TRACKER rather than a fresh `SOURCE_DETECTOR` observation. Deliberately follows the scored identity through INVISIBLE frames (unlike the IDSW/FM/MT walk, which cannot), because a coasting box's drift during a full occlusion is exactly what this metric exists to catch; it stops at the object's own LAST VISIBLE frame, not its last matched one, so an object that has permanently left the scene does not score an unbounded "drift toward infinity". Populated almost entirely in FOLLOW: `_run_cost_associate` builds its response boxes one per DETECTION, never one for an unmatched, merely-coasting candidate, so ASSOCIATE structurally has no coasting box to measure drift on today (`coast_n=0` on every ASSOCIATE row) — a fact about the shipped associator, not a metric defect. **Track lifetime** (mean/median, frames) counts how many frames each *emitted* track id appeared in — an appearance count, not a first-to-last span, matching this file's own T8 reporting convention above — so a fragmented identity shows up as several short-lived ids instead of one long one with a hole in it. **Cost** (`det/s`, `trk_ms_avg/p95`) is `session.py`'s own real `time.perf_counter()` measurement, included only so a wave that buys accuracy with more CPU shows that trade-off honestly.

**Baseline headline numbers (`BASELINE.md`).** §1 is the current V0 scoreboard (all fifteen scenarios) that waves V1–V8 are judged against; §2 reads the five new scenarios one at a time; §3 records the two harness-level facts V0 found (the single-object force-match fact above, and the `Track`-mutability trap below); §5 keeps the pre-C1 snapshot (commit `cdef30a`) for archaeological value only, superseded as the thing waves are judged against.

**A corrected reading, kept on record rather than smoothed over.** `crossing_similar`/ASSOCIATE scores `IDSW=0`, `FM=2`, recovery 100% — it does **not** swap ids. An earlier draft of `BASELINE.md` recorded `IDSW=2` with zero fragmentation for this row; re-measured directly, and re-measured again with wave V1's `cv_service/` changes stashed out to rule them out as the cause, the answer both times is `IDSW=0`/`FM=2` — the original number was simply stale, not caused by any later wave. Wave V4's acceptance is therefore stated against `FM → 0`, not against an id swap: the crossing must stop breaking the track, not stop swapping ids, because it never swapped them. `TRACKING-V2-PLAN.md` §5b already records five apparatus defects that each made the system look worse than it was, never better; this is the sixth of the same family.

**A real trap, found while building coast ADE/FDE, that any future harness work will hit again.** `TrackedBox.track` is a REFERENCE to a mutable, per-stream `Track` (`track.py`'s own docstring — the book's own entry, updated in place once per frame), not a snapshot; `TrackedBox.box` has no such problem (`Box` is frozen, and `track.box = ...` rebinds the attribute rather than mutating the old one). Reading `outcome.boxes[i].track.source` after a full replay has finished therefore reports every past frame's status as whatever the LAST frame's touch happened to be, not the frame actually being inspected — invisible until a metric tried to read a track's history backward for the first time. `ReplayResult.coast_track_ids` (`replay.py`) is captured immediately after each `session.process()` call specifically to avoid this.

**Real-footage recording (`recording.py`, wave V0).** A recorder (`record_stream`/`write_recording`) and offline replay (`replay_recording`) for a REAL detector stream — every scenario above is synthetic, and this is the forward path for when hardware tier H1 is bought. **Pixel-free by design, not merely an allowed option**: a `Recording` stores detections + `CameraPose` per frame, never pixels, small enough to commit and diff like any other fixture; `replay_recording` has no `mode` parameter at all (ASSOCIATE is the only mode a pixel-free recording can honestly serve — FOLLOW's SOT engines and ASSOCIATE's own `flow`/`histogram` compensators all need real pixels) and forces `motion_engine_id`/`appearance_engine_id` to `"off"` explicitly rather than feeding either a `None` frame. There is still no camera in this repo to record FROM, so this is proven today only against a synthetic "live" source (`sequences.py` + the real `SyntheticDetector`), not against footage that does not yet exist. `metrics.summarize_recording` is the no-ground-truth counterpart of `metrics.compute` — track count, lifetimes, cost, coasting fraction — explicitly not an accuracy number, since accuracy needs a known true position real footage does not carry unless separately hand-labelled later.

**Tests** mirror the package (`tests/trackeval/{test_sequences,test_replay,test_metrics,test_recording,test_main}.py`; 152 tests). `test_metrics.py` is deliberately pure stdlib with no dependency on a real replay — every collaborator it needs (`GroundTruthObject`, `FrameOutcome`/`TrackedBox`) is pure stdlib at module scope, and it stands in a lightweight duck-typed `_FakeTrack` (just `.track_id`) rather than constructing the real, actively-evolving `cv_service.tracking.track.Track` dataclass, so this file does not break every time a later wave adds a field to `Track` for reasons that have nothing to do with metrics correctness — the same reasoning extends to its coast-ADE/FDE tests, which build `coast_track_ids` by hand rather than depending on `Track.source`. `test_recording.py` (new) exercises record/write/read/replay/summarize against a synthetic "live" source built from `sequences.py` + `SyntheticDetector`, needing `numpy` only to build that source, never for `recording.py` itself.

## Routable model roster (which `model_id` to send)

`request.model_id` (comma-separated for composite) picks the model per the discovered roster. The three intended routable ids on this checkout:

| `model_id` | role | classes | speed (imgsz=416 CPU) | notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `yolo26n.pt` **(default)** | General, fast | 80 COCO (person/car/truck/bus/…) | **~41ms/frame** (synthetic 64×48) | the fast default; `DEFAULT_MODEL` in `cv_service/config.py`, unchanged |
| `orion12l.pt` | Military vehicles | user-provided YOLO12-L classes | ~343ms/frame | provenance in docs/plans/done/CV-MODELS-PLAN.md |
| `yoloe-26s-seg-pf.pt` | **Everything incl. buildings** (open-vocab) | ~4585 LVIS+Objects365 (person, car, truck, bus, building, house, skyscraper, office building, …) | **~69ms/frame** synthetic, **~85–100ms/frame** on real imagery | **opt-in, NOT the default**; see below |

### `yoloe-26s-seg-pf.pt` — prompt-free open-vocabulary YOLOE (opt-in, CV-MODELS-PLAN follow-up)

Satisfies the "one model covering people **+** vehicles **+** buildings" need, controllable via model selection without disturbing the fast default.

- **Zero code change to route it.** It loads via the *plain* `ultralytics.YOLO(name)` class `YoloDetector` already uses — there is **no `YOLOE` class and no `set_classes()` call**; prompt-free mode auto-loads a built-in ~1200-class-scale LVIS+Objects365 vocabulary (the loaded checkpoint reports **4585** class names). `discover_roster` already globs `*.pt`, so dropping the file into `cv-service/` makes it a routable `model_id` immediately. Verified end-to-end (discovery → `resolve` → `detect` → `map_detections`) against the real weights.
- **It is instance-segmentation, but boxes work identically.** The `*-seg-pf` checkpoints are the only prompt-free variant (there is **no detection-only prompt-free YOLOE**). Their Ultralytics `Results` carry a non-None `.masks` **in addition to** the usual `.boxes` (`.xyxy`/`.conf`/`.cls`) and `.names`. `map_detections` is already mask-agnostic — it reads only `.boxes`/`.names` and never touches `.masks` — so segmentation and detection checkpoints flow through the same box-mapping path. Confirmed on real weights: a `bus.jpg` predict returned a `Results` with `.masks is not None` and `map_detections` still produced well-formed boxes (e.g. `person` @ conf 0.88). No mask handling was added; masks are simply dropped.
- **Real emitted labels (measured, this box, imgsz=416):**
  - *people* → `person` (vocabulary also has `man`/`woman`).
  - *vehicles* → `bus` (on `bus.jpg`); vocabulary also has `car`, `truck`, `van`, `motorcycle`, `bicycle`, `motor vehicle`, `land vehicle`.
  - *buildings* → the literal labels **`building`, `skyscraper`, `office building`, `house`, `brick building`, `glass building`** are all in the vocabulary, **but** the model — being a ~4585-class LVIS+scene vocabulary — is quite scene-descriptive and often emits a *more specific* label than the bare `building`: on real city photos it produced `downtown`, `city nightview`, `sky tower`, `apartment`, `roof`, `Prague Castle`, `auto factory`, `fishing village`. **Open item / caveat:** don't assume a stable literal `"building"` string — the exact building-ish label is image-dependent; a UI that wants to bucket "buildings" should match a *set* of these labels, not one string.
- **Latency:** ~69ms/frame on a synthetic 64×48 frame, ~85–100ms on real imagery (`bus.jpg`) at imgsz=416 on this 12-thread CPU — i.e. **~1.7–2.5× slower than `yolo26n`**, still well under `orion12l`'s ~343ms. Directional only (shared dev box under load), same caveat as the tables above.
- **One-time acquisition** (the file is gitignored via `*.pt`, so a clean checkout won't have it and the real test skips): from `cv-service/`, with the `cv` extra installed, run
  `python -c "from ultralytics import YOLO; YOLO('yoloe-26s-seg-pf.pt')"`
  — Ultralytics auto-downloads the official asset (~31MB, `github.com/ultralytics/assets` release `v8.4.0`) into the cwd. No network fetch is ever done by the test suite itself; this is a manual one-off. (Other sizes exist: `yoloe-26n/26m/…-seg-pf.pt`, `yoloe-11s/11m/11l-seg-pf.pt`, `yoloe-v8*-seg-pf.pt`.)
- **OpenVINO export — OPEN ITEM, not verified.** `openvino` is deliberately not in the `cv` extra (only installed inside the Dockerfile), so export feasibility for this checkpoint was **not** verified in this task. Expectation (unconfirmed): `yolo export model=yoloe-26s-seg-pf.pt format=openvino imgsz=416` should produce a `yoloe-26s-seg-pf_openvino_model/` IR that the registry would auto-discover (it globs `*_openvino_model`), and the **same imgsz-coupling caveat applies verbatim** — the export `imgsz` MUST equal runtime `CV_IMGSZ` or inference silently runs at the wrong size. A segmentation export also carries mask outputs the box path ignores, same as the `.pt`. **Do not assume this works until someone runs it in the Docker/openvino environment** and confirms the IR loads and `.boxes` still populate. The default `yolo11n`/`yolo26n` OpenVINO export in the Dockerfile is unchanged; this model would run plain-PyTorch inside the container today (same as `orion12l`, see the Docker gotcha below).
- **`short_name("yoloe-26s-seg-pf.pt")` → `"yoloe-26s-seg-pf"`** (only the trailing `.pt` is stripped; `-seg-pf` is part of the model's own name, not a known export suffix). This is correct, not a bug — the prefix only ever labels detections in composite mode (`yoloe-26s-seg-pf:building`), where the full name disambiguates it from other yoloe variants.

## Running on ARM64 / a companion computer

cv-service is the deployable unit and it is **placement-blind**: `Settings`, the registries and the servicers have no notion of where they run, which is why the third placement below costs no redesign. The same package, the same `Inference.DetectStream` contract, the same `CV_*` env vars run in all three:

| Placement | Frames arrive by | Status |
|---|---|---|
| Co-located (laptop/server, beside vision-app) | localhost gRPC | works today |
| LAN inference box (GB4005, `vision.cv.endpoint`) | LAN gRPC, rsync deploys | works today |
| **Onboard companion computer** (Raspberry Pi 4/5, ARM64) | localhost gRPC from an onboard frame-feeder | dependency-verified; **no runtime numbers, no hardware** |

**Install path — plain pip + rsync, deliberately not a multi-arch image.** From `cv-service/` on the ARM host:

```bash
pip install -e '.[cv]'          # NOTE: no --extra-index-url on ARM
./scripts/gen_proto.sh
python -m cv_service.grpc.server
```

**The `--extra-index-url https://download.pytorch.org/whl/cpu` line is x86-only.** PyPI's plain `torch` wheel for aarch64 *is* the CPU build — there are no generic-ARM CUDA wheels — so the extra index buys nothing there and only risks resolution surprises. Deploy by rsync, the same pattern GB4005 already uses (`cv-service/DEPLOY-GPU.md`). There is deliberately **no buildx multi-arch target**: the amd64 image's defining feature is its baked OpenVINO IR export, an Intel-CPU optimization that does not apply to ARM, so a second architecture of a ~1.5 GB image would be cost with no payoff.

**Invariant P1 — nothing x86-only or CUDA-only may ever enter the `cv` extra.** It is free to hold and expensive to recover: one CUDA-gated dependency, or `openvino` promoted out of the Dockerfile into `pyproject.toml`, silently ends the onboard story. This is why the shipped tracker engines are pure core-OpenCV (`lk`, `ncc`) and pure Python over numpy (`bytetrack`), and why `openvino` stays a Docker-image-only install.

**aarch64 wheel availability — verified by downloading the wheels, not by reading a table.** This is
TRACKING-PLAN wave T8's **portability acceptance check**, and it is a *resolution* check, nothing
more: **it does not promise a green ARM build.** There is no ARM CI in this repo and no Pi. What it
establishes is that every pinned member of the `cv` extra has a real aarch64/cp312 wheel that pip
will actually select — which is the honest bound available without hardware. Re-run verbatim on
**2026-08-11 (wave T8)**, exit code 0:

```bash
pip download --no-deps --platform manylinux_2_28_aarch64 --python-version 312 \
    --only-binary=:all: -d /tmp/arm 'opencv-python>=5.0,<6' 'lap>=0.5.12' ultralytics torch==2.13.0
```

```
Collecting opencv-python<6,>=5.0
  Using cached opencv_python-5.0.0.93-cp37-abi3-manylinux_2_28_aarch64.whl.metadata (19 kB)
Collecting lap>=0.5.12
  Using cached lap-0.5.13-cp312-cp312-manylinux2014_aarch64.manylinux_2_17_aarch64.manylinux_2_28_aarch64.whl.metadata (6.6 kB)
Collecting ultralytics
  Using cached ultralytics-8.4.117-py3-none-any.whl.metadata (45 kB)
Collecting torch==2.13.0
  Using cached torch-2.13.0-cp312-cp312-manylinux_2_28_aarch64.whl.metadata (38 kB)
Successfully downloaded torch opencv-python lap ultralytics
```

| Package | aarch64 wheel | Size on disk |
|---|---|---|
| `opencv-python 5.0.0.93` | `cp37-abi3-manylinux_2_28_aarch64` | 47.5 MB |
| `lap 0.5.13` | `cp312-cp312-manylinux2014/2_17/2_28_aarch64` | 1.6 MB |
| `ultralytics 8.4.117` | `py3-none-any` (pure Python) | 1.4 MB |
| `torch 2.13.0` | `cp312-cp312-manylinux_2_28_aarch64` | **407 MB** |

**What this check does NOT establish**, stated plainly so nobody reads it as more than it is: that
the wheels import on ARM, that the engines construct there, that `python -m cv_service.grpc.server`
starts, or that any of it is fast enough on a Pi. An actual `pip install -e '.[cv]'` +
`python -m cv_service.grpc.server` smoke run on real hardware supersedes this the day H4 exists;
until then this is the bound, and it is labelled as one.

**`torch` requires glibc ≥ 2.28** — its aarch64 wheel is tagged `manylinux_2_28`, not `manylinux2014`. Raspberry Pi OS Bullseye (glibc 2.31) and Bookworm (2.36) both satisfy it. **Use `--platform manylinux_2_28_aarch64` for the resolution check, not `manylinux2014_aarch64`**: measured here, the `manylinux2014` (glibc 2.17) platform silently resolves `torch` back to **2.5.1** (87.6 MB) because 2.13.0's wheel does not satisfy that tag — a check that passes while validating a different torch than the one that will actually install. **Re-confirmed independently in wave T8** — `pip download --no-deps --platform manylinux2014_aarch64 --python-version 312 --only-binary=:all: torch` resolved `torch-2.5.1-cp312-cp312-manylinux2014_aarch64.whl` (91.8 MB on disk) rather than 2.13.0, so the trap is live and not a one-off. Use `manylinux_2_28_aarch64`.

**No Pi number in this document is measured — there is no companion-computer hardware yet.** Everything below is a **designed-for budget**, explicitly labelled as such, and stays that way until someone runs it on real silicon:

- **Budget, not measurement:** a YOLO nano `.pt` at `CV_IMGSZ=320` rather than 416 is the realistic onboard detector, and continuous detection on a Pi is not viable at any size. The duty cycle is what makes onboard perception plausible at all: a verify pass every ~2 s against a sub-millisecond tracker, rather than a detector pass every frame. `CV_TRACK_VERIFY_MS` is already the operator knob that trades re-anchor freshness for CPU with no code change.
- **Budget, not measurement:** ~1.5 GB of installed footprint (torch dominates) and thermal throttling in a sealed airframe bay are both real risks that only real hardware can size.
- **Documented escape hatch, not built:** a torch-free onboard profile (`onnxruntime`/NCNN for the detector plus an in-repo ~200-line IoU+Kalman associator registered under the same `engine_id`) — build it if and when a *measured* Pi says 407 MB of torch is the blocker, not before. `TrackerRegistry` is what keeps that a one-file change.
- **Deferred deliverable with a frozen interface:** the onboard frame-feeder — a small Python process capturing from the Pi/UVC camera and speaking the existing `Inference.DetectStream` contract to `localhost:50051`, forwarding only `DetectionResponse`s up the radio link (KB/s of tracks instead of MB/s of video). It needs no change to anything in this module; it is not built because the hardware does not exist.

## Gotchas
- **A ROI rescue is held to a stricter overlap gate than an ordinary match, and that difference is load-bearing** (`CV_TRACK_ROI_MIN_IOU`, default 0.2). Reusing the primary match's gate looks like the tidy choice — and it is what the wave shipped first — but C3 left that gate deliberately permissive (`CV_TRACK_COST_GATE_MIN_IOU=0.0`) precisely because a full-frame match has the whole scene competing to explain each box. A rescue has no such competition: the crop is taken *because* the object was predicted there, so it contains a handful of boxes and whichever one is cheapest wins. In a dense scene a neighbour the deliberately-wide crop happened to admit won the slot, and enabling ROI cost `clutter` **six id switches**. With the rescue's own gate: zero, and `small_target` keeps its full win (fragmentation 19 → 2, coverage 32 → 78 of 80 frames). If you tighten this, check `small_target` too — a gate tightened until nothing is ever rescued also shows zero id switches.
- **Several detector doubles in `tests/grpc/` are indexed by CALL COUNT, not by frame.** A ROI rescue is an extra `detect()` call, so an enabled rescue desynchronises those scripts and the test silently stops measuring what it is named for. The affected tests (memory recovery, the `InferenceGate` accounting one) now disable ROI explicitly, with the reason stated at each site. **If you add a scripted detector double, index it by something stable.**
- **A reconnect must NOT bump the track-key epoch on the `cost` path, and that distinction is the whole of what makes session pooling worth anything** (C5b review). The epoch exists so an engine's *restarted* key numbering cannot land on tracks that are still live — `bytetrack` mints its own keys from a counter it resets whenever it is rebuilt. On the `cost` path the **book** mints the keys (an `object()` sentinel for a new candidate, the track's existing key for a matched one), so there is nothing to protect against, and bumping orphans every track in the scene: the resumed stream re-books each object under a fresh key and hands it a new id. Measured before the guard: **ids 1,2 became 3,4 across a reconnect** — the pooling working perfectly and buying the operator nothing. `StreamTrackingSession._book_owns_keys()` is the predicate; `_release_engine(bump_epoch=…)` the seam. Note also that the test guarding this originally asserted `[3, 4]` and described it as the fix for finding B5 — "the counter did not restart at 1" is a weaker property that the defect satisfies, so the test now asserts that the same objects come back under the *same* ids, plus that a genuinely new object still gets a fresh one.
- **FOLLOW holding no lock re-acquires on a cadence, not on every frame** (`CV_TRACK_REACQUIRE_MILLIS`, default 250 ms). Scheduler trigger (c) used to fire unconditionally, so a FOLLOW stream whose target was simply *gone* spent a detector pass on **every frame for the rest of the stream** — more than ASSOCIATE would, and the end of the duty cycle FOLLOW exists for. It was nearly unreachable before the wall-clock LOST rule (a lock survived ~60 s of failed verifies), which is why it went unnoticed; it is reachable now. Measured on `long_occlusion | FOLLOW`: **3.60 → 1.47 detector passes/s with identical accuracy** (recovery 100%, IDSW 0 both ways), and in a stream where the target never returns the saving is unbounded rather than 2.4×. The limit is `min(reacquire, verify_every_millis)` — see `TrackingParams.effective_reacquire_millis` — because a limit that could exceed the verify cadence would be backwards: an operator asking for a short cadence wants the detector to look *often*, and a target they have LOST is more urgent than one they still hold.
- **A track's velocity is estimated from EVIDENCE ONLY, and smoothed — feeding a prediction back as an observation makes the motion model self-confirming** (C2 review, measured not supposed). Two defects compounded here and both are worth understanding before touching `TrackBook._observe`. First, velocity was taken as the instantaneous single-frame difference of box centres, so at a realistic detector jitter a perfectly **static** target measures a non-zero velocity — ±0.004 of frame height per frame at 10 fps is a few hundredths of a frame per second of pure noise. Second, a coasting track emitted its predicted box and then booked that same box as an `Observation`, so `_observe` re-derived from it exactly the velocity that had just produced it: the estimate could never decay and never be corrected, however wrong it was. Together they walked a static target's box off the object across an occlusion and failed the IoU re-anchor that would otherwise have recovered it. The fix is `Observation.predicted` (set by the session, never by an engine — see its docstring) plus `_VELOCITY_SMOOTHING`. **If you add a new observation path, decide deliberately whether its box is evidence or extrapolation; getting it wrong is silent.**
- **`tools/trackeval` resolves `Settings.from_env()`, not a bare `Settings()`** (C2 review). It did the latter briefly, which meant the harness ignored every `CV_TRACK_*` variable: an A/B run with the feature on and off returned two identical rows and read as "this changes nothing" when in fact **neither arm ever applied the setting**. A harness that silently discards configuration is worse than no harness, because it produces confident negative results. If you add a knob, check the harness can actually see it.
- **`ultralytics`' `BYTETracker` resets a PROCESS-GLOBAL track-id counter in its constructor** (T1, verified against ultralytics 8.4.104, not assumed): `BYTETracker.__init__` calls `self.reset_id()` → `BaseTrack._count = 0`, a **class attribute shared by every tracker instance in the process**. Measured consequence with two concurrent streams: stream A allocates id 1, stream B is constructed and allocates id **1** as well, and A's next new object then re-uses an id it has already issued. `ByteTrackEngine._new_tracker` restores `BaseTrack._count` after construction (guarded with `getattr`, so a future ultralytics that drops the attribute degrades silently rather than raising), which makes engine keys unique process-wide — measured A/B/A′ = 1/2/3 with the guard, 1/1/2 without. Belt and braces: `TrackBook` never publishes an engine's key anyway, it maps keys to its own per-stream ids, so even a regression here cannot leak an id across streams. Note the subtlety that bit during this work: assigning `STrack._count` does **not** work — it shadows the attribute on the subclass while `next_id()` keeps incrementing `BaseTrack._count`.
- **In `FOLLOW`, scheduler triggers (b) `TRACKER_FAILED` and (d) `BOX_INVALID` are raised only on tracker-only frames** (T1). They exist to *bring forward* the next verify pass; re-raising them on the verify frame that had already been brought forward asks for a pass that has just happened, and the detector then runs on **every single frame** for as long as the tracker stays unhappy — the duty cycle collapsing back into continuous detection, silently, exactly when the target is hardest. Found while building this (75 detector passes in 149 frames), fixed, and pinned by `test_an_unhappy_tracker_never_collapses_the_duty_cycle_into_every_frame`. The follow-on case — a tracker that reports lost *and* a verify pass that then cannot re-anchor — sets `_tracker_stalled`, after which the session stops calling the engine at all and coasts on the miss schedule until `LOST`. **Wave C1 (review finding C2) adds a THIRD way to raise trigger (b)**: a tracker-only frame whose `TrackerUpdate.confidence` falls below `params.min_tracker_confidence`, even though the update itself was a valid, successful box — the same "only on a tracker-only frame" restriction applies, checked by `test_a_persistently_weak_confidence_never_forces_two_verify_passes_in_a_row`, and it can still legitimately settle into an every-OTHER-frame pattern (tracker-only, verify, tracker-only, verify…) if the tracker's confidence never recovers — that is a 2× duty-cycle cost, not the ~30× collapse the T1 regression above was about, and it is not itself a bug.
- **`tracker_millis` on the response is the whole tracker path, including the frame decode, not just the engine call** (T1). That is the honest number for a duty-cycle claim (it is what a tracker-only frame actually costs the server) but it is 3–5× the engine's own cost on a JPEG feed — do not read it as "`lk` costs 2.4 ms". It is also an `int64` of milliseconds on the frozen wire, so a sub-half-millisecond frame reports **0**; on small frames that is the common case, not a bug.
- **A coasting box now PREDICTS forward instead of freezing (TRACKING-V2-PLAN wave C1, review finding C1)**: every place `session.py` used to set `box = held.box` (a tracker report of lost, an invalid box, the stalled-tracker branch) now calls `cv_service.tracking.predict.predict(held, now).box` instead, and the FOLLOW verify pass's own re-anchor test (`_select_target`) matches this frame's detections against the PREDICTED held box, not the stale last-committed one. The freeze used to be self-defeating: a verify pass measuring IoU against a position the object had long since left would fail to re-anchor for exactly the reason the freeze existed to work around. `predict()` clamps how far a single gap may extrapolate (`_MAX_EXTRAPOLATION_SECONDS`, 2s) so a pathologically long gap cannot fling the box across the frame.
- **`track_max_age_frames` and `track_max_age_millis` mean two DIFFERENT things and both are real (TRACKING-V2-PLAN wave C1, review finding B7)** — read `track.py`'s `_settle` before touching either. `max_age_frames` counts CONSECUTIVE FAILED VERIFY PASSES: in ASSOCIATE every received frame is a verify pass, so at the documented 10 fps sample rate the default (`30`) is ~3s of real time; in FOLLOW a verify pass only happens on cadence, so the SAME count could take up to a full minute of real time for a target the detector never re-confirms even once — this was the actual bug. `track_max_age_millis` (default `3000`, chosen so it is behaviour-preserving for ASSOCIATE at 10 fps) is a wall-clock backstop measured from `Track.last_confirmed`, independent of how many verify passes have run: whichever rule trips first declares `STATE_LOST`. `max_age_frames` is KEPT, not replaced — it still feeds `ByteTrackEngine`'s own `track_buffer` (a separate consumer, `_create_engine`), which is its own contract and unrelated to `_settle`.
- **An engine reset now bumps the book's key epoch instead of wiping it (TRACKING-V2-PLAN wave C1, review findings D1/D2)** — `_reset_engine` (an OpenCV exception mid-frame) and `_release_engine` (an operator changing mode/engine_id/max_age_frames while tracking stays active) both call `TrackBook.bump_epoch()`, which namespaces every FUTURE engine key into a fresh epoch without touching a single existing `Track`. The old `forget_keys()` call these replaced was strictly more destructive than the bug it was guarding against needed: a transient error or a config change on ONE target used to instantly retire EVERY track in the stream, including ones the engine reset could never have collided with. `forget_keys()` still exists and is still called from exactly one place, `_reset_state` (tracking going OFF) — a genuine stop, where there is no "still active, still coasting" state for anything to survive as. **Not yet built**: re-associating a coasting survivor onto the restarted engine's new numbering when the same object reappears is wave C3/C4's `assign.py`/`memory.py` — wave C1 only stops the instant amnesia of the whole-book wipe, it does not restore continuity across the reset.
- **`Transform`'s direction is a real gotcha, not a documentation nicety (TRACKING-V2-PLAN wave C2)**: it maps a point in the PREVIOUS frame to where that point appears in the CURRENT one — the direction `TrackBook.warp()` needs, since it is carrying a track's stored state from the frame it was last touched in forward into THIS one, one frame at a time. Both `flow_gmc.py` and `pose_gmc.py` state this in their own docstrings and both have an explicit test proving it (`test_a_pixel_pan_warps_a_previous_frame_point_to_its_new_pixel_position`, `test_yawing_right_moves_the_scene_left`) precisely because a compensator that warps backwards is exactly as plausible as one that warps forwards when read in isolation — it would make association strictly WORSE than no compensation at all while every individual unit still looked green.
- **Warping the STORED state every frame, not the box a `predict()` call happens to READ — this is the difference between a real fix and a silent no-op (TRACKING-V2-PLAN wave C2)**. A first version of this wave threaded `transform` as an argument into `predict(track, now, transform)`, applied once at whatever frame a call site happened to read the track. Every per-call unit test passed, and it was still wrong: a track stalled for N frames only ever picked up the CURRENT frame's single-frame delta at read time, never the N frames of camera motion that had accumulated while nothing read it — up to an N-x undercorrection, worst exactly when the SOT has stalled and prediction is all that's left, which is the ONE case ego-motion compensation exists for. `TrackBook.warp(transform)`, called once per frame for every live track before anything reads one, fixes this by making the STORED `box`/`velocity_*` always current, so `predict()` needs no transform at all. The accumulation invariant this fixes has its own direct tests now (`test_n_consecutive_identical_warps_move_a_track_by_n_steps_not_one` at the book level, `test_n_stalled_frames_accumulate_n_warps_not_one` at the session level) — if you ever see a compensation-adjacent change reintroduce a `transform` parameter on `predict()` or any `_coast`/`_select_target`-family method, that is very likely this same bug coming back.
- **`flow`'s `goodFeaturesToTrack` runs over the WHOLE frame, unmasked — including the tracked target's own box (TRACKING-V2-PLAN wave C2)**. This is deliberate, not an oversight: ego-motion is a property of the BACKGROUND, and excluding the target's own box would need to know where it is, coupling the motion compensator to the thing it is supposed to be agnostic about. The `_MIN_INLIER_RATIO` guard is what actually protects against the target dominating the point cloud (the "moving subject fills the frame" case the plan calls out): a foreground object contributing a comparable share of tracked points splits RANSAC's vote and the fit is refused (`IDENTITY`) rather than trusted, bracketed by two tests from both sides (`test_a_dominant_moving_subject_is_refused_rather_than_trusted`/`test_a_minority_moving_subject_does_not_block_compensation`).
- **Ego-motion compensation is FOLLOW-only in wave C2, and this is a design decision, not a partial implementation (TRACKING-V2-PLAN wave C2)** — see "Ego-motion compensation" above for the full reasoning (ultralytics' `BYTETracker` has no `STrack.multi_gmc`, verified, and wave C3 replaces the whole association core anyway). The practical consequence: `session.py`'s `process()` gates `_estimate_motion` on `self._params.mode == MODE_FOLLOW`, so an ASSOCIATE stream never resolves a compensator and never decodes a frame for motion purposes — `DetectionResponse.motion_millis`/`motion_engine_id` are `0`/`""` on every ASSOCIATE response, and this is the expected, tested outcome (`test_associate_never_asks_for_a_motion_compensator`, `test_associate_never_populates_motion_fields_on_the_wire`), not a bug to fix in a later wave without also giving ASSOCIATE its own frame decode (see the "cost discipline" note above for that future cost, explicitly not paid here).
- **CORRECTED within wave C2's own commit, recorded here because a prior draft of this Gotcha said the opposite and that version briefly shipped**: `tools/trackeval`'s CLI DOES read `CV_TRACK_*` environment variables, including `CV_TRACK_MOTION_ENGINE`/`CV_TRACK_APPEARANCE_ENGINE`. `tools/__main__.py`'s `_run_one` still calls `replay.run_replay(...)` without an explicit `settings=` argument, but `run_replay`'s own default is `settings = settings or Settings.from_env()` (not a bare `Settings()`), so the omission resolves to the real environment. `CV_TRACK_APPEARANCE_ENGINE=off PYTHONPATH="$PWD" .venv/bin/python -m tools.trackeval --scenario crossing --mode ASSOCIATE --engine cost` genuinely toggles appearance off — this is exactly how wave C3's own harness table (below) was produced. Passing an explicit `settings=dataclasses.replace(Settings(), ...)` directly to `run_replay` (bypassing the CLI) is still available and still useful for isolating a single knob without touching the process environment, but it is no longer NECESSARY to make an env var take effect.
- **A pre-existing (wave C1), NOT wave-C2, velocity-noise-extrapolation defect can mask ego-motion compensation's effect in a harness scenario, and was found while proving wave C2's fix against `pan_step`.** `Track.velocity_x`/`velocity_y`, once measured from a single position delta (including pure ground-truth JITTER noise measured while the camera was perfectly still and the target genuinely stationary), is extrapolated at that SAME constant rate for as long as the track keeps coasting — `_observe`'s velocity recompute on a coasting frame is mathematically a no-op (predicting `box + v·dt` and then re-deriving `v` from that exact delta returns the same `v`), so nothing ever re-measures or decays it once the SOT stalls. Combined with `last_seen` refreshing on every touch (so `predict.py`'s `_MAX_EXTRAPOLATION_SECONDS` clamp bounds one gap, never the CUMULATIVE drift across many short re-predictions), a long stall can extrapolate a noise-sized velocity into a drift LARGER than the box itself — measured directly on `pan_step`: ~0.15 of vertical drift over a 60-frame occlusion, from a target that never actually moved vertically at all. This is what hides `TrackBook.warp()`'s correct horizontal compensation in `pan_step`'s own metrics table (see "Ego-motion compensation" above for the full diagnostic) — it reproduces identically with `transform` forced to `IDENTITY`, i.e. it has nothing to do with ego-motion. Not fixed in wave C2 (out of scope: a `predict()`/`_observe()` velocity-model change, not a "warp the ego-motion transform through" one) — flagged here because whoever tackles it next should know a corrupted velocity this large would just as easily defeat C4's `ObjectMemory` motion-plausibility gate as it defeats a plain IoU re-anchor.
- **LK no longer only ever culls its corner set (TRACKING-V2-PLAN wave C1, review finding C3)**. Before this fix, `update()` could only shrink the surviving corner count frame over frame (via the flow status flag), so it decayed monotonically toward `_MIN_TRACKED_CORNERS` and the engine died there for good, however trackable the target still was — its only recovery was the next verify pass re-`init`-ing it from scratch. Two additions: a forward-backward round trip (`_forward_backward_inliers`, `_FB_ERROR_MAX_PIXELS = 2.0` px, Kalal et al.'s Forward-Backward Error) rejects a corner that jumped onto an occluder or the background BEFORE it can drag the box off the real target — the flow status flag alone only means "a match was found nearby", not "the match was correct"; and once survivors fall below `_RESEED_FRACTION` (half) of what `init()` started with, `_reseeded()` runs one more `goodFeaturesToTrack` masked to the CURRENT box and tops the set back up. Both are this engine's own algorithm constants (like the pre-existing `_MAX_CORNERS`/`_MIN_SCALE_STEP`), not operator config. `confidence` is unaffected by a reseed — it stays the fraction of `init()`'s ORIGINAL corner set still connected by an unbroken chain of forward-backward-verified correspondences, not whatever the count was topped back up to, so it is still an honest "how much of what this LOCK started with is still trackable" signal for trigger (b) above.
- **`OFF` emits no tracking fields at all, including `detector_ran`** (T1). The frozen contract calls `detector_ran` "the honesty field", but a stream that never asked for tracking has no duty cycle to be honest about, and the acceptance criterion for proto3 additivity here is that an absent `tracking` on the request produces a **byte-identical** response to the pre-T1 service. So `OFF` takes the untouched pre-tracking response path and sets none of fields 8–12; `detector_ran`/`detector_reason` are populated on every `ASSOCIATE`/`FOLLOW` response. Tested by serializing both and comparing bytes.
- **A `FOLLOW` verify pass costs one extra frame decode** (T1): the session decodes the frame to re-anchor the tracker, and `YoloDetector.detect()` decodes it again internally for the model. `_frame_loader` memoizes the session's side so it happens at most once per frame there, but the detector's own decode is that module's contract and is not shared. It affects ~1 frame in 30 in `FOLLOW`, so it was not worth widening `detect()`'s signature to plumb a decoded frame through.
- **`sys.path` gotcha** (unchanged): `protoc`'s Python codegen emits imports rooted at the proto package path (`from vision.v1 import cv_pb2`) — the generated tree's *root* (`cv_service/gen`) must be on `sys.path`. `gen_proto.sh` touches `__init__.py` everywhere so the tree imports as regular packages. Resolved in `cv_service/grpc/servicers.py` (`Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent / "gen"` — two `.parent`s now that this file lives at `cv_service/grpc/`, not `cv_service/`).
- **protobuf version-scheme note** (unchanged): `protobuf>=5.26.1,<6` vs Java's `protobuf-java` 3.25.5 is not a real mismatch — don't "fix" it.
- **Dockerfile build context** (unchanged): must build from the **repository root** — `docker build -f cv-service/Dockerfile -t cv-service .`. The Dockerfile's `CMD` runs `python -m cv_service.grpc.server` (moved from `cv_service.server`).
- **`opencv-python` vs `opencv-python-headless`**: do NOT add `opencv-python-headless` to the `cv` extra even though it would be preferable for Docker. `ultralytics` hard-depends on plain `opencv-python` (the GUI-enabled build), and pip has no notion that the two packages both provide `cv2` and conflict — installing both leaves whichever installs last silently overwriting the other's files on disk (confirmed locally: uninstalling one after both were present broke `cv2` entirely, `ImportError`). Instead the Dockerfile installs `libgl1`/`libglib2.0-0` at the OS level so plain `opencv-python` imports cleanly in the `python:3.12-slim` image.
- **`CV_MODEL` / weights location**: `YOLO("yolo11n.pt")` (a relative/known-alias name) downloads into the **current working directory** if not already present there — not a global cache dir. Locally this drops `yolo11n.pt` under `cv-service/` when running the server/tests from there; it's gitignored (`*.pt`/`*.onnx`/`*.engine`) — never commit it. The Dockerfile pre-downloads the `.pt` weights the same way at build time from `WORKDIR /app/cv-service`, then (CP-a) exports them to an OpenVINO IR directory (`yolo11n_openvino_model/`, also gitignored via `*_openvino_model/`) and points `CV_MODEL` at that directory instead — both the `.pt` source and the exported IR end up baked into the image so the container needs no network at runtime. Note the Dockerfile's baked default (`yolo11n.pt`) is intentionally independent of `cv_service/config.py`'s `DEFAULT_MODEL` (`yolo26n.pt`, unrelated to what the image bakes) — a docker-only choice, not doc drift. If the export step ever proves unbuildable (network/size), drop the `yolo export` line and leave `CV_MODEL=yolo11n.pt` — the server falls back to plain PyTorch inference, just slower (documented inline in the Dockerfile).
- **`confidence_threshold` ambiguity**: proto3 `float` means an unset `FrameRequest.confidence_threshold` reads as `0.0`, indistinguishable from "explicitly zero". Both `cv_service/grpc/servicers.py` and `YoloDetector.detect()` treat falsy/`None` as "use `DEFAULT_CONFIDENCE` (0.25)" — a caller wanting threshold `0.0` (accept everything) isn't representable on the wire today; not expected to matter for the demo.
- **First-request latency**: model construction + first `predict()` call include Ultralytics warmup (~1s observed locally even with cached weights); `inference_millis` only measures the `predict()` call itself, not model construction. `YoloDetector.__init__` pays this cost itself (a dedicated warmup `predict()` on a black frame, at `self.imgsz`) so the *first real* `detect()` call no longer does — this is what protects adapter-cv-grpc's 2s response timeout from tripping into outage backoff right at stream start. The warmup call runs for injected `model=` doubles too, not just the real model.
- **`imgsz`/OpenVINO export coupling (CP-a)**: `CV_IMGSZ` (default `416`, must be a multiple of 32 by Ultralytics convention — not enforced in code) is passed as `imgsz=` to every `predict()` call, including warmup. The Dockerfile exports the model to OpenVINO IR at build time with a **fixed** input size baked in (`yolo export model=yolo11n.pt format=openvino imgsz=416`) — that export `imgsz` MUST equal the runtime `CV_IMGSZ`, or inference silently runs against the wrong size. If you change one, change both (Dockerfile `ENV CV_IMGSZ=416` and `cv_service/config.py`'s `DEFAULT_IMGSZ` are the two places). Local/dev (non-Docker) runs plain `.pt` weights via `ultralytics.YOLO`, which has no such fixed-size constraint.
- **Warmup failure semantics**: a warmup `predict()` exception (bad model state, OpenVINO backend mismatch, etc.) raises `ModelUnavailableError` from `YoloDetector.__init__`, exactly like a load failure — the registry's `build_default_registry()` catches it and degrades to the echo fallback rather than crash-looping.
- **`LatestOnlyMailbox` drops a frame with no response at all, ever, for that frame's `sequence`** (V-d): a dropped frame never reaches `detect()`, so no `DetectionResponse` is ever produced for it. On the Java side this means that specific pending future simply times out (`adapter-cv-grpc`'s own `RESPONSE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS=2s`, see its MODULE.md) rather than being answered quickly — expected and harmless given how rare drops are meant to be in practice (see "V-d" above), but worth knowing if a dropped-frame's timeout ever shows up in logs: it is not a bug, it's the latest-wins design working as intended.
- **`CV_MAX_CONCURRENT_INFERENCES`/`CV_DEVICE` are no longer import-time-cached** (post-layering-refactor change): before `cv_service/config.py` existed, both were resolved once at *import* time (`concurrency.py`'s `DEFAULT_MAX_CONCURRENT_INFERENCES`, `inference.py`'s `DEFAULT_DEVICE`), so changing the env var after the process started never affected anything already imported. Now `Settings.from_env()` is called fresh each time a value is actually needed (`YoloDetector.__init__`, `ultralytics_train`, `process_gate()`'s first-call lazy build) — still effectively "resolved once per process" for the production path (nothing calls `Settings.from_env()` mid-request), but no longer accidentally frozen at the moment a *module* first happened to be imported, which was never a deliberate design goal, just an artifact of where the old constants lived. Tests that used to monkeypatch `inference_module.DEFAULT_DEVICE`/`concurrency_module.DEFAULT_MAX_CONCURRENT_INFERENCES` directly now `monkeypatch.setenv(...)` instead (see `tests/inference/test_detector.py`/`tests/test_config.py`).
- **`CV_DEVICE`**: `YoloDetector.__init__` reads it fresh per-instance via `Settings.from_env().device` (`device or settings.device`), so a *new* `YoloDetector`/`ModelRegistry` entry constructed later in the same process picks up whatever `CV_DEVICE` currently resolves to. A bad/unavailable device (e.g. `"cuda:0"` with no GPU present) is not special-cased: it surfaces as a normal warmup-`predict()` exception, which `YoloDetector._warmup` already converts to `ModelUnavailableError` like any other load/warmup failure — the servicer degrades to echo mode exactly as it would for a bad `CV_MODEL`.
- **Test-only nuance if you add more `DetectStream` tests with multiple frames**: any test iterator delivering 2+ frames with no artificial per-item delay races `_StreamReader`'s background thread against however fast the test happens to get scheduled — the reader can (and, empirically, sometimes does) put and overwrite several items before the consumer's first `get()` call, which is `LatestOnlyMailbox` correctly doing its job against a producer that (in the test) is unrealistically instantaneous, not a bug. Tests asserting an exact multi-frame response count/order use a small artificial per-item delay (`_PacedIterator`/`_DelayedIterator` in the test files) to avoid this — a real network is never actually instantaneous, so this is purely a test-engineering concern, not a production one.
- **Registry base dir is resolved from `Settings.model_dir`, not `os.getcwd()`** (CP-b, unchanged by the layering refactor): `Settings.model_dir` defaults to `Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent` computed **from `cv_service/config.py`** (i.e. always `cv-service/` regardless of the directory the process happens to be launched from — `cv_service/config.py` sits at the same depth under `cv-service/` that `cv_service/server.py` used to, so the computed default is unchanged) — deliberately more robust than the cwd-relative behavior `ultralytics.YOLO("x.pt")` itself uses for *downloading* a not-yet-cached alias (see "`CV_MODEL` / weights location" above), which is still cwd-dependent and unchanged; discovery just needs to reliably *find* whatever's already sitting in the checkout, not decide where a fresh download lands.
- **Docker image bakes an OpenVINO export only for the default model (`yolo11n`)** (unchanged): the Dockerfile's `yolo export ... format=openvino` step (CP-a) only runs for `yolo11n.pt`; `orion12l.pt` is present in the image (the `COPY cv-service/ ./cv-service/` step copies it, same as any other checked-in file) but the registry would load it via plain PyTorch inside the container too, same as locally — functional, just without OpenVINO's ~2-3x CPU speedup.
- **`detect_composite` logs each member's `inference_millis` at DEBUG, not INFO** (CP-b): this runs once per resolved model per *frame* (so up to 10+/sec per stream at the sampling rate `PipelineConfig.inferenceFps` targets) — INFO would spam logs at anything above a trivial frame rate. DEBUG keeps it available (`logging.getLogger("cv_service.inference.registry").setLevel(logging.DEBUG)`) without being on by default; contrast with the once-per-distinct-id unknown-model/load-failure warnings elsewhere in this module, which stay at INFO/WARNING because they're bounded (once per id, not once per frame).
- **`test_real_model.py`'s per-local-model and composite smoke tests skip (not fail) if a weights file is absent** (CP-b): `orion12l.pt` is a large (53MB), user-provided, gitignored file (see docs/plans/done/CV-MODELS-PLAN.md's provenance note — **never fetched from the network**, only ever the checked-out local file) — a clone without it present still gets a green (skipped, not failed) test suite, same contract `tests/inference/test_real_model.py`'s original `real_detector` fixture already established for "ultralytics not installed at all".
- **grpc Python servers GOAWAY ("too_many_pings") a client that pings with no active calls, by default** (docs/plans/done/REMOTE-CV-PLAN.md "Transport decisions" P1): `adapter-cv-grpc`'s `GrpcDetectionPort` pings every `KEEPALIVE_TIME_SECONDS` (20s) *including while idle* (`keepAliveWithoutCalls`, needed because a stream mid-`StreamPipeline`-outage-backoff has no in-flight RPC to piggyback a ping on) — without `grpc.keepalive_permit_without_calls=1` in `_KEEPALIVE_SERVER_OPTIONS`, this server would tear down that exact connection as abusive, defeating the client-side tuning entirely rather than helping it. `grpc.http2.min_ping_interval_without_data_ms=10000` is deliberately half the client's 20s interval (jitter headroom), not equal to it.
- **`StartTraining` on this host trains on CPU — it is genuinely SLOW** (CV-TRAINING Phase 2): the production deploy box (GB4005) is Intel-only / no CUDA (MEMORY: gb4005-inference-box), an inference appliance. A real multi-epoch fine-tune there can take a very long time; the trainer logs a one-time `"training on CPU — this is slow"` warning (`trainer._warn_cpu_once`). Device is **never hard-pinned to CPU** — `CV_DEVICE` (auto if unset) lets a GPU training-host build run on CUDA with zero code change. The honest operational path stays *train on a GPU box → rsync the artifact in → `PromoteModel`* (the `StartTraining` CPU path is buildable/correct, just slow).
- **Datasets must be rsync'd or uploaded onto the training host first** (CV-TRAINING Phase 2 / CV-TRAINING-V2): `StartTraining` does **not** receive dataset bytes over gRPC itself — `TrainingJobSpec.dataset_id` resolves to an already-present exported-YOLO dir at `<CV_DATASET_DIR>/<dataset_id>/` (`data.yaml`+`images/`+`labels/`), landed either by manual rsync or by `Training.UploadDataset`. A dataset that hasn't been copied in yet is a **normal reported outcome** — one `TrainingProgress{state:FAILED, message:"dataset <id> isn't on the training host — export it and copy it in first"}` then end-of-stream, **not** a gRPC abort. `datasets/` + `runs/` are gitignored (runtime data).
- **Produced training artifact naming + non-promotion** (CV-TRAINING Phase 2): a successful `StartTraining` copies `best.pt` into the model dir as **`<dataset_id>-<epochs>e.pt`** (deterministic; re-training the same dataset+epochs overwrites) and `ModelRegistry.register()`s it so `ListModels` shows it (`stage="available"`) immediately, no restart. It is **NOT** auto-promoted — the operator calls `PromoteModel` deliberately. If `registry is None` (echo mode), the artifact is still written and gets picked up by `discover_roster` on the next restart.
- **`StartTraining`'s `.train()` runs off the gRPC stream thread** (CV-TRAINING Phase 2): the blocking multi-minute training runs on a **background worker thread** managed by `cv_service.training.orchestrator.run_training_job`; the RPC generator only polls a `queue.Queue` for progress + yields, so a long train never wedges the server's `ThreadPoolExecutor` worker into an unresponsive state and client/`context` cancellation is honored promptly.
- **Ultralytics fires `on_fit_epoch_end` once extra** (CV-TRAINING Phase 2): after the last training epoch it runs a final validation and fires `on_fit_epoch_end` again with `trainer.epoch == trainer.epochs` (0-indexed), re-reporting the last epoch's metrics. `ultralytics_train` **suppresses** that trailing call so the stream carries exactly one `RUNNING` per epoch (never `epoch > total_epochs`). Verified against ultralytics 8.4.x via a throwaway 1-/2-epoch real train (not in the suite — CI never runs a real train).
- **`test_serve_passes_keepalive_options_to_grpc_server` fakes every collaborator `serve()` touches except `grpc.server` itself** (monkeypatches `InferenceServicer`/`TrainingServicer`/`add_*Servicer_to_server`/`_build_default_registry` to no-ops): calling the real `InferenceServicer()` default constructor would eagerly load whatever model the registry finds on disk, which is slow and environment-dependent — irrelevant to what this test actually checks (the `options=` kwarg reaching `grpc.server`).
- **`cost`'s default `min_iou=0.0` gate is deliberately permissive, and it is why `occlusion`'s recovery rate moved this wave even though nothing appearance- or memory-related was built for it (TRACKING-V2-PLAN wave C3)** — see "Association inversion" above for the full account. The short version: `cost`'s candidates include `STATE_LOST` tracks `TrackBook` has not yet expired (retained to `max_age_frames × 2`, T1's own unmodified rule, longer than `bytetrack`'s internal `track_buffer`), and with no hard IoU floor the solver alone is enough to re-match one across a gap `bytetrack` cannot. Confirmed genuine (not a bug) by ablation, but genuinely UNTESTED against a cluttered many-object scene where the same permissiveness could re-identify the WRONG lost track — this, not any single accuracy number, is the reason `bytetrack` stays the default and `cost` ships opt-in. If you tighten `CV_TRACK_COST_GATE_MIN_IOU` later, re-run the harness table above — `occlusion`'s `recov%` is the row most likely to move.
- **`pan`'s own harness scenario has no explicit background texture, unlike `pan_step` — so ASSOCIATE ego-motion compensation genuinely runs but too intermittently to move `pan`'s `mostly_tracked` metric (TRACKING-V2-PLAN wave C3, the SAME class of finding wave C2 already recorded for `pan_step | FOLLOW`)**. Directly probed, not assumed: `engines/flow_gmc.py.estimate()` against `pan`'s real frames returns a correct, non-identity transform whenever a landmark object happens to be on screen (the only source of corners `sequences.pan()` ever renders — no `_draw_world_texture` underlay, unlike `pan_step`), and `IDENTITY` otherwise. `pan_step | ASSOCIATE`'s own clean, ablation-confirmed win (`IDSW 2→0`, `recov% 0→100`, ego-motion compensation reverted by `CV_TRACK_MOTION_ENGINE=off`) is the proof the mechanism itself works; `pan`'s own `MT=0` staying `0` is a harness-scenario construction limit, not a code defect — do not read it as evidence ASSOCIATE compensation is broken.
- **`tools/trackeval`'s CLI genuinely reads `CV_TRACK_*` env vars today (`run_replay`'s own default is `Settings.from_env()`), CORRECTING a claim this file previously made** (found and fixed while proving wave C3's own harness table, not a wave-C3-introduced defect — the fix itself landed inside wave C2's commit, `f4dbe16`, but that same commit's own MODULE.md prose was never reconciled with it). See the "Ego-motion compensation" section's own point 1 above for the corrected account. If you are reading an OLDER version of this file (or a cached copy) that says the CLI ignores `CV_TRACK_*` entirely, that claim is stale — verify against the actual `tools/trackeval/replay.py` source, which this note does not modify (out of scope) but does correctly describe.
- **`TrackBook._retire` is the ONLY place a track may leave `self._tracks` for "it might come back" — never add a second one (TRACKING-V2-PLAN wave C4)**. `_expire()` is its one caller, and `_retire` is where the hand-off to `ObjectMemory.remember()` lives, BEFORE the `del`. This is deliberately structural, not a convention documented and hoped for: if a future change adds another place that pops a `Track` out of `self._tracks` (a new lifecycle rule, a manual eviction API, anything) without routing it through `_retire`, that track is deleted WITHOUT being remembered — exactly the defect this wave exists to close, silently reintroduced. `forget_keys()` (tracking OFF, a genuine wipe) is the one deliberate exception and stays that way — see its own docstring for why a full stop has no "still coasting" state for anything to survive as.
- **`ObjectMemory.claim()` returning `None` inside `_attempt_recovery` is a same-frame RACE, not a failure to log or investigate (TRACKING-V2-PLAN wave C4)**. Two unmatched targets in ONE frame can each score above `MemoryParams.min_confidence` against the SAME dormant identity — a crowd is exactly where this happens, which is why `clutter` is the scenario the plan calls out by name. `match()` is read-only and can return the same `Recovery` to two callers in the same loop; `claim()` is what actually pops the entry, exactly once. Whichever unmatched target's turn comes first in `assignment.unmatched_targets` wins the id; every other target that would have matched the SAME identity falls back to a fresh one, silently and correctly — this is the intended "refuse rather than guess" behaviour (a false recovery is worse than a missed one), not a bug to fix by, say, scoring all candidates against all dormant entries up front and doing a global assignment. If you ever see this path change to raise, log a warning, or retry, that is very likely a misunderstanding of what the `None` means.
- **`clutter`'s harness numbers cannot prove ANY crowd-recovery claim about `ObjectMemory`, and this is a scenario-construction fact, not a wiring gap (TRACKING-V2-PLAN wave C4)** — its own 60 frames cap any object at 59 consecutive misses at most, one short of `TrackBook`'s own default retention limit (`max_age_frames × 2` = 60), so no object in it can EVER reach `_expire()`, the gallery is never populated, and `.match()` is a `size()==0` no-op for the entire scenario regardless of how the memory-matching code behaves. `clutter`'s unchanged `IDSW=0`/`MT=10 of 10` (re-verified across five seeds for this task) is evidence the WIRING introduced no regression, not evidence the crowd-safety gates work correctly under load — that claim rests on `test_memory.py`'s own gate-rejection tests and `_attempt_recovery`'s same-frame-race handling (the Gotcha above), by code inspection, not by this harness scenario's own numbers. A future wave wanting an empirical crowd-recovery measurement needs a scenario whose gap genuinely empties `TrackBook` for at least one of several closely-spaced objects — `clutter` as it exists today cannot supply that.
- **A reconnect does NOT hand the reconnecting object back its OWN id, and that is correct, not a half-finished fix (TRACKING-V2-PLAN wave C5b)** — see "Session registry — reconnect" above for the full account. `reset_for_reconnect()` reuses `_release_engine()`'s epoch bump (review findings D1/D2), so the very next re-anchor after a reconnect books under a NEW id, exactly like an ordinary `engine_id` config change already does today (`test_an_engine_change_rebuilds_and_retires_the_ids`, unchanged). What survives is the BOOK (id counter, every other track) and the GALLERY (so `cost`'s own existing recovery path can still reclaim the SAME id once the object reappears, same as it would after any other engine reset) — not an automatic "this exact object keeps `#7`" guarantee. If you are asked to make a reconnect preserve the exact id of whatever was locked at disconnect time, that is a NEW capability (giving FOLLOW's own re-anchor a memory-consulting path it does not have today), not a bug in this wave.
- **`TrackBook.apply()` must be called exactly ONCE per `process()` call, even when a frame touches more than one target (TRACKING-V2-PLAN wave C5b)** — it ages EVERY live track in the book once per call, so N calls in one frame silently N-multiplies `age_frames` for every track, not just the ones an engine actually touched this frame. Multi-target FOLLOW's own `_follow_verify`/`_follow_predict` batch the locked target's observation with every extra's into one list before calling `apply()` — see `_build_coast_observation`/`_settle_followed`'s split for why `_coast` itself couldn't be reused as-is for the batched path. If a future change to `session.py` calls `self._book.apply(...)` from inside a per-target loop, that is this exact trap.
- **`tools/trackeval/metrics.py`'s FOLLOW scoring does not know which emitted track is the LOCKED one, and multi-target FOLLOW (`CV_TRACK_FOLLOW_TOP_K>1`, opt-in, not the default) exposes that (TRACKING-V2-PLAN wave C5b)** — the greedy IoU matcher treats every tracked box on a frame as an equally valid candidate for the scored ground-truth object, which was harmless when FOLLOW only ever emitted one box but can now let an "extra" box win a match slot the locked box would otherwise have gotten, registering as a spurious `IDSW`/`FM` on a scenario with closely-clustered objects (`clutter`, confirmed) even though the locked target's own id and box are BYTE-IDENTICAL between `K=1` and `K=3` runs (verified directly, not inferred — see "Multi-target FOLLOW" above). Out of scope to fix here (`tools/` was not modified, per instruction); a future `metrics.py` change would need to prefer or restrict FOLLOW matching to `outcome.locked_track_id` specifically.
- **`_roi_rescue`'s permissive gate can misidentify a NEIGHBOUR in a dense, similarly-coloured scene, and this is inherited, deliberate behaviour, not a wiring bug (TRACKING-V2-PLAN wave C5c)** — see "ROI re-detection" above for the full account, including the exact numbers. `CV_TRACK_COST_GATE_MIN_IOU=0.0` is a considered default for the PRIMARY `cost` match (wave C3's own reasoning: a strict floor would forbid the wide-displacement recovery ego-motion compensation exists for), and this wave's own instruction was to merge a rescue through the "existing association machinery", not a second, ROI-specific gate — so the SAME permissiveness applies to a crop several times a candidate's own size, where it can admit a neighbouring object instead of refusing an empty match. Measured on `clutter`: `IDSW` 0→6 with `CV_TRACK_ROI_ENABLED=1`. The shipped default (`CV_TRACK_ROI_ENABLED=False`) is unaffected — this only matters to a deployment that opts in, and one doing so in a dense/crowded scene should tighten `CV_TRACK_COST_GATE_MIN_IOU` first or evaluate the risk for its own scene density. If you are asked to make the rescue safer in a crowd without touching the primary match's own gate, a ROI-specific (stricter) `AssignGates` instance is the natural next knob — not built in this wave, since the task's own instruction was to reuse the existing gates, not add a new configuration surface.
- **ROI re-detection pays its cost even when it cannot possibly help, and there is no cheap way to know that in advance (TRACKING-V2-PLAN wave C5c)** — `occlusion`/`long_occlusion`/`crowd_recall`/`pan`/`pan_step` all trigger real rescue attempts (10–61 per scenario) that NEVER find a match: the underlying failure in each is genuine occlusion (invisible to a crop exactly as it is to the full frame) or prediction drift (the crop is centered on the wrong place), neither of which apparent-size recall can fix. `_roi_rescue`'s eligibility check (confirmed + unmatched) cannot distinguish "probably just small" from "probably occluded" or "probably drifted" — it has no signal for either. This is measured, reported cost with zero accuracy return on five of ten scenarios when the feature is opted into; a future wave wanting to reduce it would need a cheaper pre-check (e.g. only rescue a candidate whose predicted confidence, `predict.py`'s own decay, is still above some floor) that this wave does not add.

## Status
Real inference (Ultralytics YOLO, CPU by default) implements `Inference.DetectStream` per `docs/plans/done/MVP1-PLAN.md` §C7 bullet 1; falls back to the Phase 0 echo stub when the `cv` extra is absent or the model can't load, so the service never crash-loops for lack of a model. `Training.ListModels`/`Training.PromoteModel`/`Training.StartTraining`/`Training.UploadDataset` are all implemented (CV-TRAINING Phase 2 + CV-TRAINING-V2 Wave W2) — see the API surface above and MODULE.md history for the full per-item design writeups (`ListModels`/`PromoteModel` against the shared `ModelRegistry`; `StartTraining` a real Ultralytics fine-tune with cancellation, off-thread execution, and never-auto-promote; `UploadDataset` a streamed dataset archive landed atomically). **`docs/plans/done/MVP2-PLAN.md` §V-d done**: `DetectStream` decouples per-stream frame receipt from inference and bounds cross-stream concurrent inference — see "V-d" above. **`docs/plans/done/CV-MODELS-PLAN.md` items 1-2 done (CP-b)**: model registry replaces the single hard-loaded detector with a lazy `{model_id -> YoloDetector}`, plus comma-separated composite mode — see "Model registry & composite mode" above. **CV-MODELS-PLAN follow-up done**: `yoloe-26s-seg-pf.pt` is a routable, opt-in open-vocabulary `model_id` covering people/vehicles/buildings — see "Routable model roster" above. **`docs/plans/done/REMOTE-CV-PLAN.md` P0/P1 done**: explicit `CV_DEVICE` knob + HTTP/2 keepalive server options — see `cv-service/DEPLOY-GPU.md`.

**`docs/plans/done/TRACKING-PLAN.md` wave T1 done (this task)**: `cv_service/tracking/` implements the detect-then-track duty cycle behind the frozen wire contract of TRACKING-PLAN §4.A — eight modules, one charter each (`params`/`scheduler`/`track`/`lock`/`registry`/`engines.base`/`engines.{bytetrack,lk,ncc}`/`session`), everything but the three engines pure stdlib, no module importing `cv_pb2`. `InferenceServicer` creates one `StreamTrackingSession` per `DetectStream` call and threads it into `_handle_request`; `serve()` builds and probes the `TrackerRegistry` at startup. `OFF` (the default, and what an old Java client produces) is **byte-identical** to the pre-T1 service, verified by comparing serialized responses. Three real defects found and fixed while building it, all documented in Gotchas: ultralytics' process-global track-id counter, the `BOX_INVALID`/`TRACKER_FAILED` latch that collapsed the duty cycle back into per-frame detection, and a locked target serving `min_hits` verify passes (six seconds) before it stopped rendering as tentative. `pyproject.toml`'s `cv` extra gains explicit `opencv-python>=5.0,<6` and `lap>=0.5.12` pins (R2/R3 — `lap` is a hidden runtime dependency ultralytics AutoUpdates from the network *mid-stream*, which is a guaranteed in-flight failure offline) plus invariant P1 stated in a comment; the Dockerfile gains a verified "no new install step needed" note and a commented, ready-to-uncomment fetch of the deferred ONNX tracker assets, and stays deliberately single-arch. Measured numbers (engine cost, end-to-end per-frame cost, `FOLLOW` duty ratio) are in "Tracking engine" above; ARM64 wheel verification and the honest "no Pi number is measured" statement are in "Running on ARM64 / a companion computer". **127 new tests, 320 total, all green.** Not in this wave, by design: the domain/application/adapter/API/UI halves (T2–T7) and the default flip to `ASSOCIATE` (T8).

**`docs/plans/done/TRACKING-PLAN.md` wave T8 done — the plan is fully implemented.** No cv-service code changed: the flip is one line in `vision-domain` (`PipelineConfig.defaults()` → `TrackingConfig.defaults()`) plus its deployment counterpart `vision.tracking.default-mode=ASSOCIATE` in vision-app, which is what a stream now states on every `FrameRequest.tracking` — cv-service simply takes the `ASSOCIATE` branch it has had since T1. What landed *here* is documentation of two measurements: the **aarch64 resolution check** (re-run verbatim, exit 0, real output recorded in "Running on ARM64" above — and it is a resolution check, **not** a promise of a green ARM build) and the **§10 touchable-outcome numbers** ("Wave T8 — the measured outcome" above): `ASSOCIATE` costs less than run-to-run noise over `OFF`, `FOLLOW` drops the detector 30× and CPU 22.9×, and a track id survived a synthetic occluder over real photographic content. The two outcomes that need a camera — stable ids on live video and the cockpit follow-lock demo — are **not** done and are recorded as awaiting H1 hardware, not as passed.

**`docs/plans/active/LAYERING-REFACTOR-PLAN.md` wave G done**: the former 1031-line `cv_service/server.py` monolith is split into `config.py` (the one `Settings`/`Settings.from_env()`, replacing 5 ad-hoc env reads — 3 of them previously frozen at import time), `grpc/servicers.py` (wire<->domain translation only, the sole `cv_pb2` touchpoint), `grpc/server.py` (composition root — `serve()`/`main()`), `training/dataset.py` (dataset layout + zip landing + the one `sanitize_dataset_id` definition, previously duplicated), `training/trainer.py` (the ultralytics fine-tune core, moved with its dataset-layout re-exports), `training/marker.py` (renamed from the misleading `training.py`), and `training/orchestrator.py` (the `StartTraining` job-lifecycle state machine, lifted out of the servicer method, yielding plain `JobEvent`s instead of touching `cv_pb2` directly). `cv_service/inference.py`/`registry.py`/`concurrency.py` moved to `cv_service/inference/{detector,registry,concurrency}.py` unchanged in behavior. The four previously-unannotated `TrainingServicer` gRPC handler methods (`StartTraining`/`UploadDataset`/`ListModels`/`PromoteModel`) are now type-annotated. `_build_default_detector()` (dead code — confirmed via repo-wide grep, no call site, only mentioned in comments/docstrings) was deleted. The `yolo11n.pt` vs `yolo26n.pt` doc-drift between `README.md`/`DEPLOY-GPU.md` and the actual code default (`cv_service/config.py`'s `DEFAULT_MODEL = "yolo26n.pt"`) is reconciled — both docs now say `yolo26n.pt`; the Dockerfile's independently-chosen baked OpenVINO export (`yolo11n.pt`) is unrelated and left as-is (see Gotchas). This is a **structural refactor only**: every `CV_*` env var default is byte-identical to the literal it replaced (verified in `tests/test_config.py`), the gRPC wire format/HTTP-visible behavior is unchanged, and all 193 tests pass (`scripts/test.sh`) — up from the prior suite's total test count, the difference being modest new direct-unit coverage for functions that gained their own dedicated module (`Settings.from_env()`, `cv_service.training.dataset`'s path-safety helpers) rather than any removed coverage; every pre-existing test was moved (not rewritten) to mirror the new package layout (`tests/{inference,training,grpc}/` + `tests/test_config.py`), with two deliberate, narrow adaptations where the code they exercise structurally changed: (1) `TrainingServicer`'s upload-size-cap test now passes `max_upload_bytes=` as a constructor kwarg instead of monkeypatching a module-level constant (the constant became a `Settings`-sourced constructor default); (2) `YoloDetector`'s `CV_DEVICE`-resolution tests now `monkeypatch.setenv(...)` instead of patching an import-time-frozen module attribute (which no longer exists — `CV_DEVICE` is resolved fresh via `Settings.from_env()` at each construction instead of once at import time, per the plan's explicit "kill the import-time reads" goal).
