**`docs/plans/active/TRACKING-V2-PLAN.md` wave C1 done**: five correctness fixes inside the existing T1 design, no new architectural concept, no wire change, no Java/proto edit. **C1 (review finding C1, the biggest one)**: NEW `predict.py` (constant-velocity extrapolation + warp, pure stdlib); `session.py`'s `_coast` calls it everywhere it used to freeze `held.box`, and `_select_target`'s FOLLOW re-anchor test now matches this frame's detections against the PREDICTED held box instead of the stale one — the freeze was what caused the re-anchor failure that followed it. **C2 (finding C2)**: `TrackerUpdate.confidence` now feeds scheduler trigger (b) — a sub-threshold confidence on a tracker-only frame (`CV_TRACK_MIN_TRACKER_CONFIDENCE`, default `0.5`) raises `_tracker_failed` under the SAME "only on a tracker-only frame" restriction as the existing hard-failure case, so `test_an_unhappy_tracker_never_collapses_the_duty_cycle_into_every_frame` stays green. **C3 (finding C3)**: `engines/lk.py` gains a forward-backward round-trip corner rejection (Kalal et al., `_FB_ERROR_MAX_PIXELS`) and re-seeds its surviving corner set (`_reseeded`, masked to the current box) once it thins past half of what `init()` started with — the corner count no longer only ever decays to the floor where the engine used to die for good. **D1/D2 (finding D1/D2)**: `TrackBook.bump_epoch()` replaces `forget_keys()` in `_reset_engine`/`_release_engine` — an engine exception or a live config change namespaces future engine keys into a fresh epoch instead of wiping every track in the stream; `forget_keys()` is kept for `_reset_state` (tracking going OFF), the one caller that means a real wipe. Re-associating a coasting survivor onto the restarted engine's new numbering is explicitly deferred to wave C3/C4's `assign.py`/`memory.py`. **B7**: `Track.last_confirmed` (set only by a `SOURCE_DETECTOR` observation, never a tracker-only touch) backs a new wall-clock LOST rule in `TrackBook._settle` (`CV_TRACK_MAX_AGE_MILLIS`, default `3000` — chosen to be behaviour-preserving for ASSOCIATE at the documented 10 fps sample rate) alongside the pre-existing `max_age_frames` frame-count rule, which is kept (ByteTrack's own `track_buffer` contract) but now documented precisely for what it actually means in each mode — same knob, no longer a 20× different real-world meaning between them. One pre-existing test needed a scope-adjacent change: `tests/grpc/test_detect_stream_tracking.py::test_the_tracker_update_never_acquires_the_inference_gate` overrides `track_max_age_millis` to a large value in its own session `Settings` (assertions unchanged) — its ~4s all-tracker-only window is exactly the new wall-clock rule's trigger condition, decoupled here because the test's actual purpose (the gate is never touched by the tracker path) is orthogonal to it. New tests: `tests/tracking/test_predict.py` (new file) plus additions to `test_track.py`/`test_params.py`/`test_session.py`/`test_engines.py`/`test_registry.py`.

**`docs/plans/active/TRACKING-V2-PLAN.md` wave C2 done**: ego-motion compensation, FOLLOW only (ASSOCIATE deliberately deferred to C3 — see "Ego-motion compensation" above for the full design decision and the harness numbers). NEW `engines/flow_gmc.py` (`FlowMotionCompensator`, `cv2`-based, sparse flow + RANSAC affine over the whole frame, the deployment default) sits beside the already-committed `engines/pose_gmc.py` (`PoseMotionCompensator`, pure stdlib trigonometry, landed at C0 but wired in only now). `registry.py` gains a third roster (`BUILTIN_COMPENSATORS`, `.compensator(engine_id)`, `default_motion_id`), following the exact `associators`/`followers` pattern (lazy imports, per-call construction, unknown-id fallback logged once, probed at startup alongside the other two). `config.py` gains `CV_TRACK_MOTION_ENGINE` (default `"flow"`; `"off"` disables). `params.py`'s `TrackingRequest`/`TrackingParams` gain `motion_engine_id` (wire field 8, frozen at C0) — resolved like every other blank-sentinel field, but deliberately NOT collapsed to a concrete engine id inside `resolve()`, because deciding what serves it needs a live `CameraPose` that only `session.py` has. `session.py`'s `process()` estimates motion once per FOLLOW frame (never for ASSOCIATE/OFF) and calls `TrackBook.warp(transform)`; `_resolve_motion_compensator` implements the pose-unavailable-falls-back-to-flow policy, logged once per engine id, same shape as `_resolve_engine`'s degradation ladder. **Correction made mid-wave, from independent review**: the first implementation threaded `transform` as a `predict(track, now, transform)` argument, warping only the box a call site happened to read that frame — every unit test passed, but it undercorrected a stalled track by up to Nx (N = frames since anything last read it), because it only ever applied the CURRENT frame's single delta rather than the N accumulated ones. Fixed by moving the warp to `TrackBook.warp()` (NEW, `track.py`), called once per frame for every live track BEFORE anything reads one — `predict()` (`predict.py`) now takes no `transform` argument at all. See "Ego-motion compensation" above ("Warping the STATE, not the read") for the full account, including the diagnostic that confirms the fix against the `pan_step` harness scenario and the two unrelated, pre-existing issues (a `tools/trackeval` CLI gap; a wave-C1 velocity-noise-extrapolation defect) that keep `pan_step`'s own metrics table from showing it. `grpc/servicers.py` gains `_camera_pose_from_wire` (module-level, beside `_tracking_request_from_wire` — the only file allowed to touch `cv_pb2`) and populates `DetectionResponse.motion_millis`/`motion_engine_id` (wire fields 14/15) on every active-tracking response; `TRACKING_MODE_OFF` stays byte-identical (P1, existing test unchanged and still green — `camera_pose` is never read on that path). Every P1–P6 invariant re-verified: P2 (no `InferenceGate` on the motion path) has its own explicit test now (`test_the_tracker_update_never_acquires_the_inference_gate` extended with a live `StubCompensator`); P3 (`registry.py`/`session.py`/`params.py` stay pure stdlib — `flow_gmc.py`'s engine-id constants live in `registry.py`, not imported from the `cv2`-importing module itself) is pinned by the existing parametrized test, unchanged; P4 (every threshold is a named constant) — `flow_gmc.py`'s own (`_MIN_INLIER_RATIO`, `_MAX_DETERMINANT_DEVIATION`, etc.) join `pose_gmc.py`'s. New tests: `tests/tracking/test_flow_gmc.py` (new file — the pixel↔normalized conversion and transform DIRECTION pinned by `test_a_pixel_pan_warps_a_previous_frame_point_to_its_new_pixel_position`, the moving-subject-fills-the-frame guard bracketed from both sides by `test_a_dominant_moving_subject_is_refused_rather_than_trusted`/`test_a_minority_moving_subject_does_not_block_compensation`) plus additions to `test_params.py`/`test_registry.py`/`test_session.py`/`test_scheduler.py`/`test_track.py` (the last two needed only a new required dataclass field threaded through their local `TrackingParams` test builders, no behavioral change) and `test_detect_stream_tracking.py` (wire-level `_camera_pose_from_wire` + `motion_millis`/`motion_engine_id` plumbing, plus a `StubCompensator`/`StubTrackerRegistry.compensator` addition every pre-existing FOLLOW test in that file now depends on defaulting to `None`, i.e. unchanged behavior). The mid-wave correction (warp-the-state, not the read) added the accumulation invariant's own direct tests, at both layers: `test_track.py::test_n_consecutive_identical_warps_move_a_track_by_n_steps_not_one` (book level: 30 identical warps move a track's box by 30 steps, not one) and `test_session.py::test_n_stalled_frames_accumulate_n_warps_not_one` (session level, end to end through 20 consecutive stalled FOLLOW frames) — plus `test_track.py::test_warp_moves_velocity_by_the_linear_part_only`/`test_warp_is_an_exact_no_op_for_identity`/`test_warp_never_touches_a_track_born_after_it`, and `predict.py`'s own test file lost its now-invalid `transform=` test in favour of a signature assertion. **509 tests, all green** (433 before this wave; 497 after the initial pass, 509 after the mid-wave correction).

**`docs/plans/active/TRACKING-V2-PLAN.md` wave C3 done**: the association inversion — `assign.py`'s `CostAssociator` (frozen, landed at C0) wired in as a new, fully opt-in `"cost"` associator, plus a new colour-histogram `AppearanceExtractor`. Full design decision, harness table (every scenario × before/after × both `cost` configurations) and the default-decision evidence are in "Association inversion + appearance descriptors" above — summarized: `bytetrack` stays the `CV_TRACK_ASSOCIATE_ENGINE` default (untouched, byte-identical on every accuracy column, still selectable), `cost` ships opt-in and is better-or-equal on every measured scenario (never flipped anyway — the six synthetic scenarios do not stress-test `cost`'s permissive `min_iou=0.0` gate against a cluttered many-object scene, so the conservative call is made deliberately, not by omission). `CV_TRACK_APPEARANCE_ENGINE` defaults to `"histogram"` (only takes effect once `cost` is also chosen). NEW `cv_service/tracking/engines/histogram.py` (`HistogramAppearanceExtractor`, coarse joint H×S colour histogram, `cv2`-based). `registry.py` gains a fourth roster (`BUILTIN_APPEARANCES`, `.appearance(engine_id)`, `default_appearance_id`) and `"cost"` in `BUILTIN_ASSOCIATORS` (built with neutral `AssignWeights()`/`AssignGates()` — `session.py` retunes the real, resolved configuration onto it every call, cheap enough at this scale). `config.py` gains `CV_TRACK_APPEARANCE_ENGINE` plus seven `CV_TRACK_COST_*` weight/gate knobs and two new forgiving-parse helpers (`_parse_unit_interval` — unlike `_parse_unit_fraction`, `0.0` is a legitimate gate value, not a typo to guard against; `_parse_nonnegative_float`). `params.py`'s `TrackingRequest`/`TrackingParams` gain `appearance_engine_id` (wire field 9, frozen at C0, same shape as `motion_engine_id`) and `cost_weights`/`cost_gates` (`assign.AssignWeights`/`AssignGates`, built directly by `resolve()` — no wire field exists for either). `track.py`'s `Track` gains `descriptor: Optional[Descriptor]`, EMA-updated by the new module-level `observe_descriptor(track, descriptor, alpha=_DESCRIPTOR_SMOOTHING)` — evidence-only, mirroring `velocity_x`/`velocity_y`'s own `Observation.predicted` rule, called from `session.py` (not inside `_observe`) because `_run_cost_associate`'s descriptors come from a separate appearance-extraction step `TrackBook.apply()` itself has no reason to know about. `session.py`'s keystone change: `process()`'s ego-motion-compensation gate now also fires for `MODE_ASSOCIATE` when the ACTUAL resolved engine is `"cost"` (checked after `_resolve_engine()`, so `bytetrack` is provably unaffected — the DEFAULT ASSOCIATE path pays no new per-frame decode cost, measured via the `--all` harness table showing zero accuracy-column movement); NEW `_run_cost_associate` builds `Candidate`s from `TrackBook`'s own live tracks (already warped by C2's `TrackBook.warp()`, predicted forward by `predict.py`, unchanged) and `Target`s from this frame's detections, retunes+calls `CostAssociator.assign`, and books the `Assignment` back through the SAME `TrackBook.apply()` every other associator uses; NEW `_describe`/`_resolve_appearance_extractor`/`_release_appearance_extractor` mirror the motion-compensator trio's build-once/release-on-change shape, simpler (no live-signal availability check `pose` needs). `grpc/servicers.py`'s `_tracking_request_from_wire` gains one line reading `appearance_engine_id` off the wire (field 9, previously frozen-but-unread) — the smallest possible completion of what C0 already promised; no other servicer/wire change, no Java/proto edit (P6 unaffected). Every P1–P6 invariant re-verified: P1 (`test_a_request_with_no_tracking_field_returns_a_byte_identical_response`, unchanged, still green — `appearance_engine_id` is never read on the OFF path); P2 has a NEW explicit ASSOCIATE-specific test (`test_associate_with_cost_motion_and_appearance_never_acquires_the_inference_gate` — a live motion compensator AND a live appearance extractor, 20 consecutive ASSOCIATE frames, gate acquisitions == frame count exactly, never one more); P3 (`params.py` now imports `assign.py` at module scope — still pure stdlib, since `assign.py` itself is; the existing parametrized `test_everything_but_the_engines_stays_pure_stdlib` catches a regression here); P4 (every new threshold is a `config.py` `CV_TRACK_COST_*` default or `histogram.py`'s own named constant — no bare literal). Acceptance test (`tests/tracking/test_cost_appearance_integration.py`, the REAL harness, not a hand-rolled fake): `bytetrack` still swaps ids on `crossing` (`IDSW>0`, pins the baseline), `cost`+`histogram` swaps none (`IDSW==0`). New tests: `tests/tracking/test_histogram.py` (new file), `tests/tracking/test_cost_appearance_integration.py` (new file) plus substantial additions to `test_track.py`/`test_params.py`/`test_registry.py`/`test_session.py`/`test_detect_stream_tracking.py`, and the two required-field threads (`test_scheduler.py`'s and `test_track.py`'s local `TrackingParams` builders, no behavioral change, same pattern C2 already established for `motion_engine_id`). **553 tests, all green** (509 before this wave).

**`docs/plans/active/TRACKING-V2-PLAN.md` wave C4 done — the wave the whole plan exists for**: `memory.py`'s dormant gallery (landed fully-built at C0, untouched since) is finally wired in, so a track id survives being lost. Full design decision, harness table, and the honest findings on `occlusion`/`clutter` not moving (and why) are in "Object memory" above; summarized here — TWO new seams, both minimal: `TrackBook._retire` (private, the ONLY caller of `_expire()`'s deletion) hands a track to `ObjectMemory.remember()` immediately before deleting it, structurally (not just conventionally) closing the "deleted without being remembered" defect; `session.py`'s `_run_cost_associate` offers every genuinely unmatched target to `ObjectMemory.match()`+`.claim()` (`_attempt_recovery`, NEW) before `TrackBook.apply()` would otherwise mint it a fresh id, booking a hit through a NEW `apply(..., recoveries=...)` parameter and `track.py`'s NEW private `_adopt()`/`RecoveredIdentity`. Remembering is universal (any associator, any mode — `_resolve_memory()` runs unconditionally near the top of `process()`, mirroring `_resolve_engine`'s build-once shape); recovering is `cost`-only (`bytetrack` has no unmatched-target seam to offer, and FOLLOW's `_follow_key()` deliberately mints a fresh id on every re-lock, unchanged) — proven as a real, not merely inert, distinction by a test that expires a track under `bytetrack`, switches the SAME stream to `cost` via `apply_config`, and recovers it. `Detection.identity_confidence`/`.dormant_millis` (wire fields 10/11, frozen at C0) are populated for the first time, sourced from a NEW `TrackedBox.identity_confidence`/`.dormant_millis` (per-FRAME, not per-track — `0.0`/`0` on every frame that is not the exact recovery frame, including the one right after) and copied through unconditionally by `_tracked_detection` (`servicers.py`, a proto3-zero-is-a-no-op simplification, no `track is not None` guard needed). `params.py` gains `TrackingRequest.memory_ttl_millis` (wire field 10, frozen at C0, finally read by `_tracking_request_from_wire`) and `TrackingParams.memory_params: MemoryParams`, built by `resolve()` the same way `cost_weights`/`cost_gates` are. `config.py` gains seven `CV_TRACK_MEMORY_*` knobs and one new parser, `_parse_int_allow_nonpositive` — the one knob in the file (`CV_TRACK_MEMORY_TTL_MILLIS`) where a non-positive DEPLOYMENT value is a legitimate "gallery off fleet-wide" choice, not a typo to fall back from (a per-REQUEST `memory_ttl_millis<=0` still means "use the deployment default", the same sentinel shape every other such wire field uses — there is no distinct value a client could send to disable memory for just its own stream). `memory.py` itself is completely unmodified — every one of its four match gates (label, appearance, motion plausibility, TTL) and its capacity/gallery-size bounds are exactly as C0 shipped them; this wave is entirely about the two call sites, not the gates. **Acceptance, exactly as specified**: `long_occlusion | ASSOCIATE | cost` moves `IDSW 1→0`, `recovery 0%→100%`, `life_mean 30.0→60.0`, across a 90-frame (9s) absence longer than `TrackBook`'s own retention; `occlusion`'s pre-existing 100% recovery (via retention, not the gallery — see "Object memory" above for why the distinction matters) is unmoved; every other scenario (`linear`/`crossing`/`pan`/`pan_step`/`dropout`/`clutter`), both modes, is byte-for-byte identical on every accuracy column — re-verified via a full `--all` diff, plus `clutter` specifically across five seeds (0–4), not merely the default one, since it is the scenario the plan names as mattering most (though its own 60-frame length structurally cannot exercise the gallery at all, an honest finding recorded in its own right, not papered over — see the Gotcha and "Object memory" above). Every P1–P6 invariant re-verified: P1 unaffected (`identity_confidence`/`dormant_millis` are only ever set from `_run_cost_associate`, never reached on the OFF path); P2 — `memory.py`/`_resolve_memory`/`_attempt_recovery`/`_retire` touch no gate reference, structurally identical to the existing argument for motion/appearance; P3 — `track.py`'s only reference to `ObjectMemory` is a `TYPE_CHECKING`-guarded import (no runtime dependency added), `params.py`/`session.py` import `memory.py` at module scope but it is pure stdlib, so the existing parametrized `test_everything_but_the_engines_stays_pure_stdlib` (unchanged) still passes; P4 — every new threshold is a `config.py` `DEFAULT_TRACK_MEMORY_*`/`CV_TRACK_MEMORY_*` default, mirroring `MemoryParams`' own already-considered production defaults exactly; P5 — a disabled/absent gallery, a missing descriptor, and a same-frame double-claim race are each handled as a documented no-op, never a raise (`memory.py`'s own methods are `never raises` by contract; nothing new here needed a `try`/`except`); P6 unaffected (no Java, no proto file touched). New tests: additions to `test_track.py` (`TrackBook`-level: remember-on-expire only, never before; `.set_memory` swap; `apply(recoveries=)` books the given id and skips `min_hits`; the id-counter bump guard; recoveries ignored for an already-live key), `test_params.py` (the wire sentinel plus six deployment-only `MemoryParams` fields plus the one non-positive-is-legitimate env var), `test_session.py` (the session-level acceptance criteria, the cross-associator-switch recovery, the different-object refusal, deployment-disabled-is-a-no-op, a per-request TTL that has already elapsed), and `test_detect_stream_tracking.py` (the SAME two acceptance criteria at the wire level, via a NEW `ScriptedDetector` double and the existing `StubCostAssociator`/`StubTrackerRegistry` fixtures — no `cv2`/`numpy` needed for any of this wave's own new tests). **587 tests, all green** (567 before this wave).

Test suite (`tests/`), file membership as of wave C3 (grown since the T1 snapshot the rest of this paragraph describes — `test_{assign,memory,flow_gmc,pose_gmc,histogram}.py` and `test_cost_appearance_integration.py` are C0–C3 additions, listed here for accuracy rather than re-narrated): `tests/inference/test_detector.py` + `tests/grpc/test_inference_servicer.py` + `tests/grpc/test_detect_stream_concurrency.py` + `tests/grpc/test_detect_stream_tracking.py` + `tests/inference/test_registry.py` are mock-based (need `cv2`/`numpy`, i.e. the `cv` extra's transitive opencv-python, but not `ultralytics`/`torch` — they inject fake model/detector/engine doubles); `tests/inference/test_concurrency.py` + `tests/test_config.py` + `tests/training/test_dataset.py` + `tests/training/test_trainer.py` + `tests/training/test_marker.py` + `tests/tracking/test_{params,scheduler,track,lock,registry,session,predict,assign,memory}.py` are pure-stdlib (no `cv` extra needed at all — the tracking ones run against a fake clock with no frames, which is the whole reason the package is split the way it is); TRACKING-V3-PLAN wave V1 adds `tests/tracking/test_levels.py` (probe/resolve/name unit tests, `_importable`/`_available_memory_mib` monkeypatched for determinism, PLUS two P8-purity tests that spawn a `python -c` subprocess which — on a normal dev venv — DOES import the `cv` extra inside that subprocess; the outer test process needs none of it) and `tests/tracking/test_capability_benchmark.py` (same subprocess shape; several of its own tests `importorskip` `numpy`/`cv2`/`ultralytics`/`lap` and skip rather than fail when absent, same contract `test_engines.py` established) to this pure-stdlib set; `tests/tracking/test_engines.py` needs `cv2` for `lk`/`ncc` and `ultralytics`+`lap` for `bytetrack`, `test_flow_gmc.py`/`test_pose_gmc.py`/`test_histogram.py` need `cv2`/`numpy` for their one engine each, and `test_cost_appearance_integration.py` needs `cv2`/`numpy` for the real harness's frame rendering, skipping rather than failing when the dependency is absent; `tests/inference/test_real_model.py` is a real-weights integration test, auto-skipped/individually-skipped unless `ultralytics` is importable and the relevant weights (default, `yolo11n.pt`, `orion12l.pt`, or `yoloe-26s-seg-pf.pt`) load. Verified locally end-to-end at T1: full `pip install -e '.[cv,dev]' --extra-index-url https://download.pytorch.org/whl/cpu` + `scripts/gen_proto.sh` + `scripts/test.sh` all green (**320 passed**, all weights present on this checkout; **553 passed** as of wave C3, see that wave's own history entry above). The "importable without the `cv` extra" contract was re-verified after T1 by blocking `cv2`/`numpy`/`ultralytics`/`torch`/`lap` at import: `cv_service.grpc.server`, `cv_service.grpc.servicers` and every `cv_service.tracking.*` module still import, and the tracker registry's startup probe degrades to an empty roster with one WARNING per engine rather than raising. `python -m cv_service.grpc.server` confirmed to start and serve echo cleanly with the `cv` extra absent (import-only check — `cv_service.grpc.server`/`cv_service.grpc.servicers`/`cv_service.training.*` all import successfully without `cv2`/`ultralytics` installed), and to load the real model and detect on a real JPEG frame end-to-end through `InferenceServicer.DetectStream` with the extra present. `docker build -f cv-service/Dockerfile .` was not re-verified in this task (no Dockerfile behavior change beyond the `CMD` module path and comment path references — confirmed by inspection, not a rebuild). Real-GPU-box deployment itself was not executed in this task — no GPU hardware in this dev environment.

**`docs/plans/active/TRACKING-V2-PLAN.md` wave C5b done**: two independent fixes for `docs/conclusions/TRACKING-REVIEW.md`'s §4.7 scalability findings — see "Session registry — reconnect" and "Multi-target FOLLOW" above for the full design, the harness table, and two honest findings recorded rather than hidden (a reconnect does not preserve the reconnecting object's OWN id, by design, same rule an ordinary engine reset already followed; the trackeval harness's FOLLOW scoring is not multi-target-aware, confirmed a harness limitation not a code defect, `tools/` left untouched). Summarized: NEW `cv_service/tracking/sessions.py` (`SessionRegistry`, pure stdlib, its own file per the task's own instruction) pools `StreamTrackingSession` by `stream_id` with a disconnect grace window (`CV_TRACK_SESSION_GRACE_MILLIS`/`_CAPACITY`, both deployment-only); `InferenceServicer.DetectStream` acquires/releases through it instead of constructing a session per call. `session.py` gains `reset_for_reconnect()` (composes the existing `_release_engine`/`_release_motion_compensator`/`_release_appearance_extractor` — engines dropped, book/gallery/lock untouched) and multi-target FOLLOW (`TrackingParams.follow_top_k`, no wire field, default `1`; NEW `_ExtraFollow`/`_ExtraCandidate`, `_extras_verify_observations`, `_extras_observations`, `_release_extras`; `_coast` split into `_build_coast_observation`/`_settle_followed` so a tracker-only frame can batch the locked target's observation with every extra's into ONE `TrackBook.apply()` call — the double-counted-`age_frames` trap found and fixed while building this, recorded as its own Gotcha). `registry.py` gains a documentation-only note (`follower()`'s existing "new instance every call" contract already covers being called K times per stream; no roster or behaviour change). `config.py` gains three knobs (`track_session_grace_millis`/`_capacity`/`track_follow_top_k`) and no new parser (`_parse_positive_int` covers all three). Every P1–P6 invariant re-verified: P1 — `TRACKING_MODE_OFF`'s existing byte-identical test is unaffected (neither the session pool nor `follow_top_k` is read on that path); P2 — `test_multi_target_follow_updates_never_acquire_the_inference_gate` extends the existing single-target proof to `K=3` across 59 tracker-only frames, zero acquisitions; P3 — `sessions.py` added to both of `test_registry.py`'s parametrized invariant tests (pure-stdlib, no `cv_pb2`); P4 — every new threshold is a `config.py` `DEFAULT_TRACK_*` default; P5 — an extra's engine failing is a documented drop, never a raise, and `SessionRegistry.acquire()`'s concurrent-duplicate branch degrades to an independent session rather than corrupting or blocking; P6 — no Java file, no proto file touched (grep-verified: `git diff --stat` for this task touches only `cv_service/`/`tests/`/`MODULE.md` under `cv-service/`). New tests: `tests/tracking/test_sessions.py` (NEW file — pool mechanics, reconnect within/after grace, a concurrent-duplicate acquire, capacity/grace eviction never touching an `in_use` entry, a 16-thread stress test), `tests/tracking/test_session.py` (`reset_for_reconnect`'s per-field proof; the multi-target `K=1` safety net, `K>1` multi-box emission, locked-always-first, a dropped extra's engine release + slot refill, an existing extra's IoU re-anchor, extras untouched when the lock itself fails to re-anchor), `tests/grpc/test_detect_stream_tracking.py` (reconnect id-continuity at the wire level, replacing `test_detect_stream_creates_one_session_per_call` which pinned the PRE-fix behaviour; the P2 multi-target extension), `tests/tracking/test_params.py`/`tests/tracking/test_track.py`/`tests/tracking/test_scheduler.py` (the `follow_top_k` field thread, same pattern C2/C3 already established for `motion_engine_id`). **638 tests, all green** (605 before this task). Full `--all` harness diff at the shipped default (`follow_top_k=1`): byte-for-byte identical on every accuracy column, every scenario, both modes — only measurement-noise timing columns move.

**`docs/plans/active/TRACKING-V2-PLAN.md` wave C5c done — the last wave.** ROI re-detection: the detection-RECALL half of `docs/conclusions/TRACKING-REVIEW.md` §4.6, the counterpart to waves C1–C5b's identity/association work. Full design, the priority rule, the scope decision, the harness table and BOTH honest findings (a real `clutter` regression, and cost paid for zero benefit on five occlusion/drift-dominated scenarios) are in "ROI re-detection" above; summarized here. `session.py`'s `_run_cost_associate` gains `_roi_rescue` (NEW) — at most one bounded, gated second detector pass per frame, around the highest-priority CONFIRMED candidate the primary `cost` match just left unmatched (priority: most consecutive `misses`), folded into the SAME `TrackBook.apply()` call the primary match was already going to make (never a second call — the multi-target-FOLLOW wave's own trap, re-avoided here) and gated by the SAME retuned `CostAssociator` instance a full-frame match uses, restricted to the one chosen candidate — never a hand-attach. `bytetrack` ASSOCIATE and FOLLOW do not gain this capability (no Candidate/Assignment seam for the former, out of this wave's scope for the latter — see the scope decision above). `servicers.py`'s `InferenceServicer._run_detector` gains an optional `roi`/`frame_loader` pair — `roi=None` (every pre-wave call, and the overwhelming majority after it) is byte-for-byte the old full-frame branch (P1); `roi=<Box>` delegates to NEW `_run_roi_detector`, which crops the servicer's ALREADY-decoded, memoized frame (never a second `decode_frame`), repackages the crop as raw `IMAGE_ENCODING_BGR24` bytes so it feeds the SAME detector call shape every other path in this file already uses (`_detect_via_registry` gained optional width/height/encoding/data overrides for exactly this), and maps returned detections back to full-frame coordinates (NEW module-level `_crop_for_roi`/`_map_roi_detection`) — `_run_detector` stays the sole `InferenceGate` acquisition site for both branches (P2). `DetectionResponse.detector_roi` (wire field 13, frozen at C0) is populated for the first time. `params.py` gains `TrackingParams.roi_enabled`/`.roi_crop_factor`, both deployment-only (no wire field — TRACKING-V2-PLAN §2's frozen diff adds none), copied straight from `Settings` by `resolve()`. `config.py` gains `CV_TRACK_ROI_ENABLED` (default **`False`** — deliberately opt-in, a stronger case than `CV_TRACK_FOLLOW_TOP_K`'s own opt-in default since this knob's cost is a genuine second `InferenceGate`-bounded detector pass, not a sub-millisecond SOT update) and `CV_TRACK_ROI_CROP_FACTOR` (default `4.0`) plus two new parsers, `_parse_bool` (the first boolean `CV_TRACK_*` knob) and `_parse_positive_float`. **Acceptance, exactly as specified**: `small_target | ASSOCIATE | cost` moves `FM 19→2`, `PT→MT`, `life_mean 32.0→78.0`, `IDSW` stays `0`, at a measured cost of `+5.38` detector passes/second (43 of 80 frames trigger a rescue, every one matches) — reported alongside the accuracy win, not instead of it, per the task's own instruction. Every other scenario, measured with `CV_TRACK_ROI_ENABLED=1` (not the default): `linear`/`crossing` never trigger; `pan`/`pan_step`/`occlusion`/`long_occlusion`/`crowd_recall` trigger real, wasted attempts (0 matches — the underlying failure is occlusion or prediction drift, neither fixable by apparent-size recall) with accuracy columns byte-for-byte unchanged; `dropout` incidentally improves (`FM` 11→4, a second independent miss-roll recovering some of what the first lost); `clutter` regresses (`IDSW` 0→6, the SAME permissive `min_iou=0.0` gate wave C3 deliberately chose for the primary match, now also governing the rescue, admitting a similarly-coloured neighbour in a dense scene) — reported, pinned as a regression test, not hidden or silently mitigated by inventing a second, ROI-specific gate the task's own instruction did not ask for. The shipped default (`CV_TRACK_ROI_ENABLED=False`) leaves EVERY scenario byte-for-byte identical to pre-wave — re-verified via a full `--all` diff, only measurement-noise timing columns move. Every P1–P6 invariant re-verified: P1 — `roi`/`frame_loader` default to `None`, so `OFF` and every pre-wave call site are untouched, and `roi_enabled=False`'s own `--all` diff is the empirical proof; P2 — `_run_detector` stays the sole gate acquisition site for both branches, proven directly (`test_run_detector_roi_is_gated_by_the_inference_gate`) and at the wire level across a full-frame-then-rescue frame pair (gate count == full-frame passes + roi passes, exactly); P3 — the crop/decode/map round-trip lives entirely in `servicers.py`; `session.py` only ever sees `Box`es in full-frame normalized coordinates, in and out, so the existing parametrized pure-stdlib test needed no change; P4 — every new threshold is a `config.py` `DEFAULT_TRACK_ROI_*` default or the pre-existing `_ROI_MIN_PIXELS`/gate constants; P5 — a disabled feature, a missing loader, a degenerate crop, a decode failure, a gate-rejected match, and a rescue that finds nothing are each a documented no-op costing only that one pass, logged at most once per servicer instance, never raised, never the stream; P6 — no Java file, no proto file modified (`detector_roi` was already frozen at C0). New tests: `tests/grpc/test_inference_servicer.py` (the coordinate round-trip, gate, frame-loader-reuse, degenerate-crop, `roi=None` byte-identical, and registry-routed crop tests — 7 new), `tests/grpc/test_detect_stream_tracking.py` (gate count at the wire level, id continuity across a rescue, `detector_roi` true/false, deployment-default-never-triggers — 4 new), `tests/tracking/test_session.py` (10 new: disabled no-op, successful rescue, tentative excluded, gate rejection, at-most-one-per-frame, priority by misses, crop box math, non-positive crop factor, `bytetrack`/FOLLOW excluded, the multi-target-FOLLOW-trap re-check), `tests/tracking/test_params.py`/`tests/test_config.py` (knob resolution + the two new parsers), and NEW `tests/tracking/test_roi_redetection_integration.py` (the real-harness acceptance criteria, 13 tests). **704 tests, all green** (640 before this wave). Full `--all` harness diff at the shipped default (`roi_enabled=False`): byte-for-byte identical on every accuracy column, every scenario, both modes — only measurement-noise timing columns move. **The plan's own five waves (C1–C5, with C5 split into C5a/C5b/C5c) are now all done.**

**`docs/plans/active/MEDIA-SOT-PLAN.md` wave M3 done**: `Inference.DetectPulled` — the worker-pull counterpart to `DetectStream`, per the frozen wire contract §5.1 (M1) and M0's own two GO decisions (`CV_PULL_DECODER=opencv`, `CV_PULL_CLOCK_MODE=anchor`, `docs/conclusions/CV-PULL-SPIKE.md`). Full design in "Pull mode (`DetectPulled`)" above; summarized here. NEW `cv_service/pull/` (`source.py`/`clock.py`/`loop.py`, no `cv_pb2` import, pure stdlib except `source.py`'s lazy `cv2`) carries forward — does not re-derive — `spikes/pull/sampler.py`'s `DeadlineSampler`+`LatestOnlyMailbox` port of `StreamPipeline.sampleDue`/`armScheduleAt` (`feat/cv-rate-control`), preserving both properties a port most easily loses: the one-clock-read-per-iteration rule (structural — `sample_due(now_ns)` takes the reading as a parameter, checked by a dedicated source-inspection test) and the `max(now, ...)` debt clamp (a late schedule restarts from `now + interval`, counting — never firing a burst of — the skipped deadlines). `CaptureClock` anchors `capturedAt = anchorWallclock + (pts − anchorPts)`, re-anchoring once measured skew (arrival − the anchor-derived estimate, i.e. drift in transit latency SINCE the last anchor, not raw latency) exceeds `CV_PULL_CLOCK_REANCHOR_THRESHOLD_MILLIS` (new knob, `config.py`, default 100 ms — reuses the plan's own drift-gate number as the trigger). `PullDecodeLoop`'s reader thread always overwrites its single-slot mailbox and counts every overwrite (D8, CLAUDE.md rule 9) — proven by a fast, deterministic pytest (`tests/pull/test_loop.py::test_d8_...`) reproducing the exact stall scenario the spike measured by hand: `dropped_frames` climbs monotonically while every served frame's own age at consumption stays bounded, never growing with the backlog. `grpc/servicers.py` gains `InferenceServicer.DetectPulled` PLUS its own small module-level support cast (`_PullControlState`, `_PullControlReader` — mirrors `_StreamReader`'s role for the CONTROL stream, but OVERWRITES rather than drop-and-counts, since a superseded `PullControl` is not a loss the way a video frame is; `_PullStreamIdMismatch`; `_resolve_pull_target_fps`/`_resolve_pull_detect_width`; `_downscale_for_detection`, mirroring `DetectionFrameCodec.withDownscaled`'s exact rule) — but adds **no second inference path**: every served frame becomes a synthetic `cv_pb2.FrameRequest` handed to the SAME `_handle_request`/`_echo` `DetectStream` already uses, decorated afterward with fields 16-21. Reused verbatim: `SessionRegistry`/`StreamTrackingSession` (a reconnecting PULL resumes its book/gallery/lock exactly like a reconnecting PUSH stream), `InferenceGate`, `ModelRegistry` (composite `model_id` unaffected), `_tracked_response`. New constructor kwarg `pull_source_open=` (injectable `PullSource` factory, defaults to `cv_service.pull.source.open_source`, lazily imported) is what lets every new test drive the real servicer method with no RTSP/mediamtx dependency. The ONE pre-existing comment this wave's own task flagged as inverted — `_handle_request`'s `time.monotonic()` rationale, which used to say `request.timestamp_millis` comes from "another machine's clock" — is corrected in place: true under `DetectStream` (the JVM stamps it), no longer literally true under `DetectPulled` (this same process's `CaptureClock` stamps it); the CODE is unchanged (the duty cycle genuinely wants wall time spent, not capture time, regardless of transport), only the comment's premise. Re-verified by direct check (blocking `cv2`/`numpy`/`ultralytics`/`torch`/`lap` at import): `cv_service.grpc.servicers` (now importing `cv_service.pull.*` at module scope) still imports without the `cv` extra, and a `DetectPulled` call against that build degrades to a clean `UNAVAILABLE` (never a crash) the instant it tries to open a source. §5.1's call semantics are each covered by their own test in NEW `tests/pull/` (missing `source_url` → `INVALID_ARGUMENT`; an unopenable/later-stalled source → `UNAVAILABLE`; a `stream_id` mismatch on any later `PullControl` → `INVALID_ARGUMENT`; `stop=true` and client half-close both drain cleanly; `sequence` worker-minted/monotonic/no-correlation; a mid-call `model_id` swap taking effect on the very next served frame with zero special-case code; `detect_width` downscaling exactly at the `>`/aspect/round boundary). **Deliberately out of scope, per this wave's own file boundary**: `DetectStream` itself (byte-for-byte untouched — the whole existing suite is the proof), `vision-domain`'s `PulledDetectionPort` (M2), the Java `adapter-cv-grpc` pull client (M4), and re-measuring §6's clock drift against a real camera (M0's own stated gap — this wave carries the measured `anchor` choice forward, it does not re-run the 10-minute drift benchmark). New tests: NEW `tests/pull/` (`test_sampler.py`, `test_clock.py`, `test_source.py`, `test_loop.py`, `test_detect_pulled.py` — 44 tests total, none needing real RTSP/mediamtx: `source.py`'s tests monkeypatch `cv2.VideoCapture` itself, `DetectPulled`'s tests inject a fake `PullSource` via `pull_source_open=`) — a deliberate exception to the "tests mirror the package" convention (see "Conventions" above): `DetectPulled`'s own servicer-level tests live here too, not in `tests/grpc/`, because this wave's file scope named exactly one new test directory. **756 tests, all green** (712 before this wave) — `cd cv-service && ./scripts/test.sh`, verified with the venv's own `python` on `PATH` (the script hardcodes the `python` command name, not an absolute interpreter path).

**`docs/conclusions/MEDIA-SOT-RESULTS.md` §6 defect fixed** (post-M9, `feat/media-sot`): `dropped_frames` (field 19) was counting every mailbox overwrite, not just genuine backlog loss — M9's own numbers named the tell (`droppedInFlight: 1027` against a healthy `submittedFps: 9.987`, ~3:1, almost exactly the 30fps-source-into-10fps-target non-selection ratio). Fixed in `loop.py` only: `_LatestOnlyMailbox.put()` now just reports whether it overwrote an unconsumed frame (bool), no counting of its own; `PullDecodeLoop` owns `_dropped_frames` and only increments it when an overwrite lands while `_awaiting_consumer` is true (set right before `frames()` `yield`s a served frame, cleared the instant it resumes — exactly the span `servicers.py` spends in `_handle_request` running inference synchronously, so this window IS the "loop could not keep up" case, never the "sampler hasn't reached its next deadline yet" case). No change to `DeadlineSampler`, `CaptureClock`, `PullDiagnostics`'s shape, or `servicers.py` (still reads `diagnostics.dropped_frames` unchanged) — the fix is entirely inside `loop.py`'s accounting. `proto/vision/v1/cv.proto` field 19's comment (only) was tightened to state this precisely; no field number/name/type moved, both codegens regenerated (`./mvnw -B -pl vision-proto test`, `scripts/gen_proto.sh`). Two paired tests pin it (see D8 above): a source faster than target now measures `dropped_frames == 0` throughout (previously nonzero from pure downsampling); the existing stalled-consumer D8 test still climbs, but its first served frame now legitimately reads `0` too (nothing had been handed to a caller yet), only climbing once a served frame is genuinely out with a busy caller — the old assertion `dropped_over_time[0] > 0` was itself pinning the bug and was corrected. **757 tests, all green** (756 before this fix). The `vision-application` half (`DetectionRateWindow`'s hard-coded `submitted = 0L`, the other cause of M9's `dropRatio: 1.0`) is a separate fix in that module's own MODULE.md — not this module's concern, since pull mode's `submitted` is counted JVM-side from arriving `DetectionResult`s, never over the wire.

**`docs/plans/active/TRACKING-V3-PLAN.md` wave V1 done**: the capability ladder — one pipeline, five affordability tiers, L1 (RELAY, pure stdlib) through L5 (STUDY, workstation-only) — see "Capability ladder" above for the full design, the P8 purity mechanism, and the benchmark's one honest caveat about subprocess RSS accounting under a sandboxed test environment. Summarized: NEW `cv_service/tracking/levels.py` (pure stdlib, zero `cv_service` imports of its own — `probe()`/`resolve()`/`name()`); `params.py` gains `TrackingRequest.capability_level`/`TrackingParams.capability_level` (wire field 11, `TrackingConfig`); `registry.py`'s four accessors gain an optional `level=` ceiling, filtering each roster BEFORE any factory runs (`_ASSOCIATOR_MIN_LEVEL` and three siblings); `session.py` resolves the served level once per stream (`_resolve_capability_level`, hot on config change via `_release_capability`), threads it through every registry call via a `TrackerRegistry`-only `_level_kwargs` guard (so pre-V1 duck-typed test registries elsewhere in the suite need no changes), and reports `capability_level_served`/`_reason` on `FrameOutcome`/`DetectionResponse` (wire fields 25/26); `config.py` gains `CV_TRACK_CAPABILITY_LEVEL`/`DEFAULT_TRACK_CAPABILITY_LEVEL`; `grpc/servicers.py` reads wire field 11 into `TrackingRequest` and writes fields 25/26 onto the response — the two response fields are this wave's own named file-scope reason to touch that file, and reading field 11 is the one-line companion needed to make them mean anything for a client that actually sets it (flagged for a second look: the task's file-scope note said "ONLY to populate the two new response fields", read here as "don't touch anything ELSE in that large file", not as "leave the ceiling permanently unreachable from the wire"). Proto: `TrackingConfig.capability_level = 11`, `DetectionResponse.capability_level_served = 25`, `.capability_level_reason = 26` — exactly the three fields TRACKING-V3-PLAN §3 names for this wave (field numbers verified against the tree before writing: `TrackingConfig` ended at 10, `DetectionResponse` at 21); fields 22–24 are reserved for later V3 waves. `./mvnw -B -pl vision-proto compile` green with **zero Java source files edited** (`git diff --stat -- '*.java'` empty). Existing `TrackingParams(...)` direct-construction test helpers (`tests/tracking/test_track.py`, `test_scheduler.py`) and duck-typed fake registries (`tests/tracking/test_session.py`'s `FakeRegistry`/`PoseOrFlowRegistry`) were updated for the new required field / optional `level=` kwarg — the only reason any file outside this wave's direct additions needed touching, all inside `tests/tracking/**`. New tests: `tests/tracking/test_levels.py` (27, `probe()`/`resolve()`/`name()` unit tests plus 2 subprocess-based P8 purity tests covering ASSOCIATE and FOLLOW-degrades-to-ASSOCIATE), `tests/tracking/test_capability_benchmark.py` (5, one deliberately `pytest.skip`'d as manual-only — reproduces §5.1's cost table), plus additions to `test_config.py` (14, `_parse_capability_level`), `test_params.py` (6, the `capability_level` sentinel), and `test_registry.py` (12, the level-aware roster, including `test_a_level_ceiling_never_even_calls_the_excluded_factory` proving an excluded engine's factory is never even CALLED, not merely discarded) — **64 new tests this wave**. A total-suite snapshot is not recorded here: `tools/trackeval`/`tests/trackeval` had concurrent unrelated work in flight in the same working tree while this wave was built (per this task's own instructions), so any single number would misattribute that delta. Invariant P1 (`TRACKING_MODE_OFF` byte-identical) untouched — `process()` is only ever called when `session.active`, so every new capability-resolution code path is unreachable on that mode by construction, not merely untested.

**`docs/plans/active/TRACKING-V3-PLAN.md` wave V2 done**: the observation ring — the substrate both wave V3's ORU and wave V4's OCM need. NEW `cv_service/tracking/history.py` (`TimedObservation`, `ObservationRing` — pure stdlib, `collections.deque`/`dataclasses` only, importable at capability level L1) plus `Track.history: ObservationRing` (`track.py`, a fresh, never-shared ring per track via `default_factory`). `_born`/`_adopt`/`_observe` each call `track.history.record(observation, now)` unconditionally on every booking path; `ObservationRing.record` itself — not the call site — decides what survives: `SOURCE_DETECTOR` only, never merely "not predicted" — both shapes of `SOURCE_TRACKER` evidence (a coast, and the visual tracker's own successful per-frame optical-flow/template match) are excluded, the second being the non-obvious one, since admitting it would let a later reader bracket a gap with exactly the drift an observation-centric method exists to delete. Bounded by construction (`deque(maxlen=capacity)`, `_DEFAULT_CAPACITY=16`, sized against OCM's default `momentum_span_frames=3` with headroom — see the `history.py` bullet above for the full derivation). **Zero behavioural delta, verified not assumed**: the ring was populated on every booking path but read by nothing yet at this wave — the full `tools/trackeval` scoreboard reproduces byte-identical accuracy columns before and after. §4.1b (which frame is a remembered observation expressed in) was surfaced but deliberately left OPEN for wave V3 to close by measurement — three candidates recorded in the plan, not decided here. **Suite: 880 → 914 passed with 1 skipped** — wave V2 itself contributed 32 new tests, and `tools/trackeval/BASELINE.md`'s own consistency guard (`tests/trackeval/test_baseline_consistency.py`, 2 tests) landed in the same working tree alongside it.

**`docs/plans/active/TRACKING-V3-PLAN.md` wave V3 done**: ORU — the Observation-Centric Re-Update — plus its own unplanned prerequisite, the observation-centric re-anchor fallback. Full design, the §4.1b measurement, the three OCR safety properties, and the honest `long_occlusion` finding are all in "Observation-Centric Re-Update" above; summarized here. NEW `cv_service/tracking/reupdate.py` (`Reupdate`, `reupdate()` — pure stdlib, capability level L1, invariant P8 verified mechanically by a `sys.modules`-inspecting subprocess test, the same shape `test_levels.py`'s own P8 tests use). `track.py` gains `Track.history_transform: Transform = IDENTITY` (accumulated forward by `TrackBook.warp()`, O(1) per live track per frame, reset to `IDENTITY` by `_observe` exactly when the ring admits a genuinely new entry) and `Track.reupdated: bool` (a THIS-FRAME flag, reset every call); `TrackBook` gains `.last_reupdate_millis`/`.last_reupdated_tracks` properties and `.reset_reupdate_stats()`. `session.py` gains two additions: `process()` calls `reset_reupdate_stats()` before the mode dispatch and reads the two accumulators onto `FrameOutcome` after it; `_select_target` gains the OCR fallback — a second candidate offered only when the primary prediction-based test already failed, matched at the SAME `redetect_iou_threshold`, bounded by the SAME `reupdate_max_gap_millis` ceiling, deliberately NOT gated on `track.misses > 0` (see "Observation-Centric Re-Update" above for why). `config.py` gains `CV_TRACK_REUPDATE_MAX_GAP_MILLIS`/`DEFAULT_TRACK_REUPDATE_MAX_GAP_MILLIS` (`15_000`, `_parse_int_allow_nonpositive` — the same shape and parser `CV_TRACK_MEMORY_TTL_MILLIS` uses); `params.py` threads the wire sentinel into `TrackingParams.reupdate_max_gap_millis`. Proto: `TrackingConfig.reupdate_max_gap_millis = 14`, `Detection.reupdated = 14`, `DetectionResponse.reupdate_millis = 22`/`.reupdated_tracks = 23` — exactly the fields §3's frozen diff reserved for this wave. `./mvnw -B -pl vision-proto compile` green with **zero Java source files edited**. **Acceptance, measured**: `nonlinear`/FOLLOW coast ADE **39.8 → 9.1 px (−77%)**, coast FDE **59.3 → 14.3 px (−76%)**, coverage `PT → MT`; exactly 1 of the 30 `BASELINE.md` rows moved, the other 29 byte-identical; `CV_TRACK_REUPDATE_MAX_GAP_MILLIS=0` reproduces the entire scoreboard including this row (P7). `long_occlusion` does not move, and correctly so — its motion is genuinely constant-velocity (the pre-V3 estimate already converges to ≈0.0401 normalized at re-anchor, measured both before and after) and its measured 1.4 px sits at the position-jitter noise floor (`POSITION_JITTER=0.004` → 1.28 px); `TRACKING-V3-PLAN.md` §6 records this as an error in the plan's own acceptance criterion, not a shortfall in the wave. New tests: `tests/tracking/test_reupdate.py` (16, new file — the bracket-must-exist/gap-ceiling/velocity-over-the-true-gap/drift-reporting/history-transform-warping/L1-purity cases), plus additions to `test_track.py` (9 — `history_transform` accumulation and reset-on-real-observation, ORU replacing the ordinary velocity measurement, `reupdated`'s per-frame reset, the no-gap/ceiling-exceeded no-ops, `TrackBook`'s reupdate-stats accessors), `test_session.py` (5 — the OCR fallback's success, never-overrides-a-successful-primary-match, ceiling-respected, off-switch, and nothing-held cases), `test_params.py` (6 — the wire sentinel's request/deployment-default/env-var/garbage-fallback cases, same shape `memory_ttl_millis`'s own tests use). **36 new tests this wave. Verified directly** (`PYTHONPATH="$PWD" .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q`): **950 passed, 1 skipped**, reconciling exactly with 914 (wave V2 + guard) + 36.
